California Masonic Memorial Temple – San Francisco

i

DAVID R. DOAN
Grand Master
October 13, 2004 – October 12, 2005

ii

DAVID R. DOAN
Grand Master – 2004-2005
Most Worshipful David R. Doan, of Moorpark, California, was born
and raised in Venice, California. He attended Santa Monica College where he
received an Associate of Arts Degree in Criminal Justice. He attended the Los
Angeles campus of National University where he received a Bachelor of
Science degree in Public Administration with honors. He is also a graduate of
the FBI National Academy for law enforcement executives at Quantico,
Virginia. He has been married to his wife Teri since 1977 and they have two
adult children.
He became a Police Officer for the Los Angeles Police Department in
1974. He has worked in all parts of the City in patrol, detectives, narcotics, and
administrative assignments. As a Captain he has commanded Devonshire Patrol
Division, North Hollywood Patrol Division, Detective Headquarters Division,
Planning and Research Division, and Pacific Area. As a Commander he has
worked as the Department’s Employee Relations Administrator and as the
Department’s Risk Manager. In January of 2002 he was promoted to his current
rank of Deputy Chief of Police.
He is active in many professional, community and charitable
organizations, including the International Association of Chiefs of Police, the
Los Angeles County Police Officer Association, and the FBI National Academy
Associates. He is a member of the Rotary and has been active in the Lions and
Optimists. He has served as a Director of the San Fernando Valley Charitable
Foundation that raises funds for Children at risk including Children with AIDS.
He is currently on the Board of Directors for the Midnight Mission in the Skid
Row Section of Los Angeles.
He was raised to the Sublime Degree of Master Mason in November of
1975 in Triangle Lodge No. 548, where his grandfathers, father, uncles and
brother belonged. Triangle Lodge is now Sunset Lodge No. 369. In addition to
serving as Master of this Lodge in 1993, he was the President of the Temple
Board for five years and served as the Officer’s Coach. He is also a member of
the lodge closest to his home, Conejo Valley Lodge No. 807 in Thousand Oaks.
He is a Senior DeMolay and has received its Legion of Honor. He is a
member of numerous Masonic Organizations. He is a Past Venerable Master of
the Lodge of Perfection at the Los Angeles Scottish Rite and has been honored
with rank and decoration of Honorary Inspector General of the 33rd Degree. He
is an Ambassador for the Al Malaikah Shrine Temple. He is a member of Santa
Monica Bay York Rite Bodies; Santa Ana Chapter, National Sojourners; San
Juan de Los Angeles Council, Allied Masonic Degrees and the Saint Gabriel
Conclave, Red Cross of Constantine.

iii

He served Grand Lodge as the Grand Chaplain in 1997 under Most
Worshipful Allen B. Gresham. He was a member of the Grand Lodge Trial
Review Committee for nine years and was its Chairman for several years. He
was a chartered member of the Grand Lodge Research Committee and was its
Chairman for 3 years. He has served as a Trustee for the California Masonic
Foundation, California Masonic Memorial Temple and the Nob Hill Masonic
Management Corporation. In 1997 he became a Trustee of the Masonic Homes
of California and served the Board as Vice-President before being elected to the
office of Junior Grand Warden in 2001. He was elected the Grand Master of
Masons in California in October of 2004.

PUBLIC OPENING CEREMONY
Sunday, October 9, 2005
1:00 PM
The Public Opening of Grand Lodge was held in the Masonic
Auditorium of the California Masonic Memorial Temple, 1111 California
Street, San Francisco, California beginning at 1:00 p.m. The Grand Lodge was
raised by Worshipful Robert W. Doan and Brother Christopher R. Doan, father
and son of the Grand Master and members of Sunset Lodge No. 369 and Conejo
Valley Lodge No. 807, respectively.
Grand Master David R. Doan, accompanied by his Grand Lodge
Officers, entered the Auditorium to the sound of the organ, and assumed their
stations. The Grand Chaplain gave the invocation.
The Grand Master welcomed all in attendance to the Public Opening of
the 156th Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of California. He thanked
his father and son for raising the Grand Lodge.
The Grand Marshal was directed to introduce the visiting dignitaries
from other Masonic jurisdictions and the leaders of the concordant and
appendant bodies of the Family of Freemasonry in California. The following
were present:

At the conclusion of the introductions, the Grand Master remarked, “It
is a special pleasure and a privilege to welcome you to our Annual
Communication. To each of you I express the appreciation of all Masons in
California.”
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Marshal to retire and with
the assistance of the Knights of St. Andrew of the San Francisco Scottish Rite
Bodies, present the Flag of Our Country before the Altar of Freemasonry. This
was followed by the “Mending America Flag Quilt” which was selected by the
Smithsonian Institute as the official “September 11, 2001 Commemorative
Quilt.”

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

7

Mending America Flag Quilt
After the tragic events of September 11, 2001, Richard and Nancy
Gray decided to use their resources to create a quilt to commemorate this event
and to memorialize those who lost their lives as a result of it. In as much as
Nancy is the owner of Sewing Attic, the largest sewing school in the United
States, they were perfectly prepared for the task. Nancy designed the quilt on
her return flight from a business trip to New York. The quilt was assembled by
her students and several community volunteers. The Smithsonian Institute
recognized their tremendous effort by selecting it as the official September 11,
2001 Commemorative Quilt. Brother Gray is a member of Irvine Valley Lodge
No. 671.
The Grand Master thanked Brother Gray for making it possible to
include this very important symbol in our ceremony today.
The Grand Master then called on Donna Herring, Worthy Grand
Matron of the Grand Chapter of California, Order of the Eastern Star to bring
greetings on behalf of the Family of Freemasonry and our visitors from other
jurisdictions.
Grand Master Doan then commented on the several events which took
place prior to the Public Opening. These special events began on Friday
evening, October 7th with the Mason of the Year Dinner honoring Warren J.
Blomseth, Past Grand Master. On Saturday, October 8th the Grand Master and
his corps of officers dedicated the new Yurt structure at the Masonic Home at
Union City and on Sunday morning, October 9th there was a Ribbon Cutting
Ceremony for the new Henry Wilson Coil Masonic Library located on the 2nd
Floor of the Masonic Memorial Temple.
Grand Master Doan introduced Brother Mark Tabbert, a member of
Mystic Valley Lodge in Arlington, Massachusetts, and Director of Collections
at the George Washington Masonic National Memorial. Brother Tabbert is the
author of the new book American Freemasons: Three Centuries of Building
Communities. Brother Tabbert’s book will be on sale in the Lobby following
the close of Public Opening.
Leadership Development Program Recognition
With Leadership Development as one of the highest priorities, the
Grand Lodge sponsored three training programs this year including two
Inspectors Seminars, four Wardens Retreats, and five Lodge Management
Certification programs. In all, nearly 1,000 participants received leadership
training this year.
The Lodge Management Certification Program consists of five courses
that are conducted simultaneously at a particular location. The courses include:

8

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Hall Association Management, Lodge Finance, Membership Development,
Program Planning, and Lodge Administration. Each course is a full day in
length. At the end of each course, participants must pass an examination before
they are given a certification in that course. In order to complete more than one
course, a participant must attend more than one location or take the courses over
a number of years. This was the second year the program was offered. Three
members have already completed all five courses.
The Grand Master was joined at the podium by Worshipful Fred
Kleyn, Brother Norm Dant, and Brother Cris Alarcon. Worshipful Fred is from
Spring Valley and is the Inspector of the 493rd Masonic District. Brother Dant
is the Senior Deacon on Downey-United Lodge No. 220. Brother Alarcon is the
Senior Steward of Sacramento Lodge No. 40.
The Grand Master congratulated the members for completing the
Lodge Management Certification Program and presented each one with a
plaque.
Remarks from the Youth Orders
The Grand Master called on the four youth leaders of the three
Masonic youth orders in California throughout the day. Miss Danielle Jordan,
Grand Bethel Honored Queen, represented the International Order of Job's
Daughters. Following her remarks the Grand Master presented her with a
donation on behalf of the California Masonic Foundation to benefit the Job’s
Daughters leadership training program.
Masonic Student Assistance Program Presentation
The Grand Master remarked that community awareness is an important
effort of Masonry in California. While certain elements of Masonry are for
members only, the majority of the work is done for the public good. One of the
most impressive community projects is the Masonic Student Assistance
Program. Last year was the 10th anniversary of this program in California.
History was made this year with the assistance and dedicated work and generous
support of Naval Lodge No. 87 in Vallejo. Naval Lodge sponsored the largest
MSAP training class in the county. In August, over 120 faculty members of
Solano County schools participated in the three day training class held at the
Vallejo Masonic Center. The cost of the program, nearly $15,000, was donated
by a contribution from the Lodge.
The Grand Master invited Worshipful Anil Comelo, Master, and
Brother Robert Wilson, Senior Warden, of Naval Lodge to join him at the
podium. The Grand Master called on Most Worshipful M. William Holsinger,
PGM, President of the California Masonic Foundation, and Most Worshipful

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

9

Don Baugher, Executive Director of the National Masonic Foundation for
Children in Alexandria, Virginia to assist him in presenting a certificate of
appreciation to Naval Lodge.
Remarks from the Youth Orders
The Grand Master then called on Brother Gregory Wilhoit, Master
Councilor, Northern California Jurisdiction, Order of DeMolay for his remarks,
after which he presented a donation on behalf of the California Masonic
Foundation to benefit the DeMolay leadership training program.
Daniel Carter Beard Masonic Scouter Award
The Grand Marshal was directed to conduct the Daniel Carter Beard
Masonic Souter Award recipients West of the Altar. The Grand Master, with
the assistance of the Deputy Grand Master, presented the following brethren
with this award recognizing their service to youth through Scouting: Steven L.
Yeffa, a member of Pleasanton Lodge No. 321 and Ronald O. Reynolds, a
member of Orinda Lodge No. 122.
Remarks from the Youth Orders
The Grand Master then called on Mr. Brian James, Master Councilor,
Southern California Jurisdiction, Order of DeMolay for his remarks, after which
he presented a donation on behalf of the California Masonic Foundation to
benefit the DeMolay leadership training program.
Grand Master's Youth Support Award
The Grand Master announced that four brothers had been selected to
receive the Grand Master's Youth Support Award to recognize their outstanding
contribution to the Masonic youth orders. The Grand Master then called upon
Worshipful V. Kent Armstrong, Chairman of the Grand Lodge Youth Orders
Committee to assist him in presenting the Grand Master’s Youth Support Award
to the following honorees: Richard N. “Hap” Arnold, a member of Glendale
Lodge No. 368; George Evans, a member of Fontana Lodge No. 653; Leonard
B. Froomin, a member of San Francisco Lodge No. 120; and Donald A.
Journey, a member of San Marino Lodge No. 408.
The Grand Master directed the Grand Marshal to escort the recipients
to a place West of the Altar. The Grand Master and the Deputy Grand Master
then presented the awards while Brother Armstrong read the biography of each
honoree.

10

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The distinguished Masons were saluted with the Public Grand Honors.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the recipients to
seats in the auditorium.
Remarks from the Youth Orders
The Grand Master then called on Miss Shannyn Allan, Grand Worthy
Advisor, International Order of Rainbow for Girls, for her remarks, after which
he presented a donation on behalf of the California Masonic Foundation to
benefit the Rainbow Girls leadership training program.
Presentation of the New Square and Compass
One of the greatest attributes of Masons is their willingness to reach
out and help those in need. Earlier this year when it was learned that the Grand
Lodge had suffered the loss of the implements that adourn the sacred altar, the
Square and Compass, a Masonic Family stepped forward and commissioned
their replacement. The Grand Master invited Worshipful Rosser “Doc”
Adamson and Brother Rick Adamson to the podium to make this presentation.
Accompanying them was Worshipful Vic Majarian who crafted the beautiful
instruments.
Both the square and the compass are hand-scrolled 24 carat gold with
an 8.5 carat sapphire at the apex of the compass. The square is engraved to read
Grand Lodge of California.
The Grand Master thanked these brothers for their remarkable gift and
for their dedication to the Craft and their commitment to the traditions and rich
heritage of the Fraternity.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

11

DEVOTIONAL SERVICE
The Grand Master then observed that as the program drew to a close,
we would turn our thoughts inward and to the attention of the Grand Chaplain
for an Inspirational Message.
The Grand Master then called upon the Grand Chaplain, the Very
Reverend Reuben B. Zari, for his message. The complete text of the Grand
Chaplain’s Message can be found in Appendix G.
The Grand Master thanked the Very Reverend Reuben B. Zari for his
truly inspirational message.
The Grand Master concluded by thanking all for attending the Public
Opening of the 156th Annual Communication of Grand Lodge and reminded
everyone that Mark Tabbert would be in the lobby signing copies of his book,
“American Freemasons, Three Centuries of Building Communities”. The
Grand Master led the Grand Lodge Officers out of the auditorium.

ONE HUNDRED FIFTY-SIXTH
ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
MONDAY MORNING, OCTOBER 10, 2005
The 156th Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of Free and
Accepted Masons of the State of California was called to order by Grand Master
David R. Doan, on Monday, October 10, 2005.
Worshipful Robert W. Doan and Brother Christopher Doan, the father
and son of the Grand Master, raised the opening session of Grand Lodge.
Reception of the Past Grand Masters
The Grand Master welcomed the brethren and directed the Grand
Marshal to present the Past Grand Masters of the Grand Lodge of California at
the Altar. The Grand Marshal then introduced each Past Grand Master,
following which the Grand Master, accompanied by the Deputy Grand Master,
personally greeted each one. The Grand Master asked the brethren to join him
in saluting our Past Grand Masters by the battery of three times three. The Past
Grand Masters were then escorted to seats reserved for them in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Secretary to send to each absent Past
Grand Master the usual letter of greeting from the Grand Lodge.
Roll Call
The Grand Secretary was asked to call the roll of Grand Lodge
Officers, Past Grand Masters, and Past Grand Officers, and to ascertain if a
constitutional number was present to convene the Grand Lodge of California.
The following were present as indicated by their response to the calling of the
roll:

The Grand Secretary announced that the Committee on Credentials had
reported that at the close of Registration on Sunday, there were 583 Master
Masons registered as of the end of the day, with at least twenty percent of the
Lodges chartered by this Grand Lodge represented, and that therefore a quorum
was present as required for the transaction of business.
The Grand Secretary reported that all Lodges have paid their annual
per capita assessment to Grand Lodge except for the following Lodges:

Grand Lodge Opening

The Grand Master then opened Grand Lodge in ample form. The
Grand Marshal and the Deacons retired to escort the Flag of the United States of
America into the Auditorium, which was followed by the Pledge of Allegiance.
Introductions
The following were asked to rise as groups for recognition: Hiram
Award Recipients, Diamond Veteran Award Recipients, Golden Veteran Award
Recipients, Worshipful Masters, Lodge Secretaries, Grand Lodge Committee
members, members of Constitutional Boards, District Inspectors, and Visiting
Brethren from other jurisdictions. Each group was greeted with a round of
applause.

Memorial Service
The Grand Master called on the Very Reverend Reuben B. Zari, Grand
Chaplain, to present a service of remembrance for our departed brethren.
Grand Master’s Message
The Grand Master then relinquished his chair to the Deputy Grand
Master for the purpose of delivering the Grand Master’s Message (Appendix
A). After his message, the Grand Master received a standing ovation, following
which he resumed his station.
Legislation
The Grand Master stated that the Grand Lodge would begin
consideration of the legislation before us by considering the Grand Master’s
Decisions and Recommendations. Then the Grand Master asked the Deputy
Grand Master to assume his station, while he delivered to Grand Lodge the
Grand Master’s Decisions and Recommendations for their consideration.

at the end of the discussion and voting on them. He further stated that no ballots
on this part of our legislative business will be accepted after the ballots are
collected today. This year, he explained, the ballots would be counted
separately, and the results of the voting on the Grand Master’s Decisions and
Recommendations announced Tuesday morning.

TUESDAY MORNING, OCTOBER 11, 2005

Grand Lodge was called on promptly at 9:00 a.m. by Grand Master
David R. Doan and the Grand Chaplain offered prayer. The Grand Master
announced that Grand Lodge had been raised this morning by Wor. Rosser
“Doc” Adamson and his son, Brother Rick Adamson, both members of Sunset
Lodge No. 369.
The Grand Master then invited Worshipful Loyal Stark, Past Master of
Acacia Lodge No. 243, Hayward, to the lectern. Brother Loyal, who brought
fraternal greetings from the world of Eastern Star, is serving the last year of his
term as Most Worthy Grand Patron of the General Grand Chapter of the Order
of the Eastern Star.
“Most Worshipful Grand Master:
This is indeed a distinct honor to be able to address my Brethren of this
esteemed California Grand Lodge.
I bring you greetings on behalf of some 700,000 members of the Order
of the Eastern Star, from Aruba to Alaska, from Manila to Milan, and from San
Juan to San Francisco.
We all share common values and principles whether the language is
English, Italian, Portuguese, Spanish or German whether so ever dispersed
around the globe.
I personally have been able to witness the Fraternal Love and
cooperation of Masonic Lodges and Eastern Star Chapters working together in
their communities across the world.
Whether it is the Lodge and Chapter in Bucaramba, Columbia working
together to assist the Children’s Hospital and Ronald McDonald type house. Or
the Lodge and Chapter in Milan that sponsors a golf tournament to raise
thousands of Euro for the local hospital, or the countless acts of fraternal love
during the recovery along the Gulf Coast, providing shelter for homeless Sisters
and Brothers. Or one needs only to look at the local community of Martinez
where the Lodge and Chapter work hand in hand to assist each other.
The good news is that when they do, the community benefits. The
really good news is that when there is this close fraternal bond, the Masonic
Fraternal Family prospers.
How proud I am to be able to state that I am a member of this
wonderful Grand Lodge that is pre-eminent and innovative in the service and
leadership to our Craft.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

23

I am even more proud to state that I am a part of the Greatest Fraternal
Organization there is in the World today, THE MASONIC FRATERNAL
FAMILY.
Most Worshipful, I sincerely thank you for this opportunity.”

and carried by a majority affirmative vote.
Election of Officers
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Secretary to explain the
balloting procedure for the election of officers. No ballot would be necessary if
the office were not contested. If more than one nomination were received for an
office, ballot cards would be distributed for the purpose of balloting for that
office.
Election of the Grand Master
Deputy Grand Master Frederick L. Sorsabal was nominated Grand
Master by Most Worshipful C. Ray Whitaker, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the
nominations be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a
unanimous ballot for Right Worshipful Frederick L. Sorsabal to serve as Grand
Master for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was
declared unanimously elected.

APPENDIX A

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

33

GRAND MASTER’S MESSAGE
Brethren of the Grand Lodge:
It is the duty of the Grand Master at the Annual Communication to
submit a written message setting forth: All of his official acts during the year;
The general condition of Masonry within the Jurisdiction; and Recommending
such legislation as he deems necessary or expedient for the welfare of the Craft.
My complete written message will be published in the Proceedings for this
Annual Communication. After the conclusion of my speech, I will separately
present my Decisions and Recommendations for your consideration. This
morning I will give you an overview of the important issues in my message that
warrant being highlighted.
Strategic Planning
In February of this year, after two years of planning and development,
the Executive Committee approved an updated strategic plan. The plan outlines
the mission, values, vision, and strategic initiatives for the Grand Lodge of
California over the next five to seven years. The plan represents the vision of
the members of the Executive Committee over the last two years. Input was
obtained from various Masonic Leaders around the state and from each of the
Grand Lodge Committees and Boards. Copies of the Plan were distributed in
June to each of the lodges.
The Plan has four parts, Our Mission, Core Values, the Vision
Statement and Strategic Initiatives. The last section, Strategic Initiatives is
updated each year by the Grand Master according to his plans of action for the
year. The document is a living breathing document that will change over time
and is meant as a guide towards the bright and prosperous future we all desire
for our beloved fraternity. Some of the significant parts of the plan are as
follows.
Freemasonry
Freemasonry is the world’s first and largest fraternal organization. It is
a body of knowledge and a system of ethics based on the belief that
each man has a responsibility to improve himself while being devoted
to family, faith, country, and fraternity.
Our Mission
The mission of the Grand Lodge of California is to understand and
convey the wisdom of Masonic teachings to its constituent lodges in a
way that engages and inspires members to conduct their lives according
to Masonic tenets and values. The Grand Lodge charters and guides

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

34

Masonic lodges within California and provides important resources and
unity to lodges and members.
Our Values
Our Mission is guided by the enduring and relevant tenets of our
fraternity - Brotherly Love, Relief, and Truth - and our core values,
which include:
Tolerance
The fraternity values religious, ethnic, cultural, social, and
educational differences. We respect the opinions of others and
strive to improve and develop as human beings.
Freedom
Masons value the liberties outlined in the U.S. Constitution and
continually promote freedom of speech and expression, freedom to
worship a Supreme Being in an individual way, and other
important liberties. We believe it is our duty to vote in public
elections and to exercise all of our liberties within due and proper
bounds.
Philanthropy
We make a difference in our communities through charitable
giving, community service, and volunteerism.
Family
We strive to be better spouses, parents, and family members and
are committed to protecting the well being of members and their
families, especially when they are in need.
Personal growth
Our continuous pursuit of knowledge, ethics, spirituality, and
leadership brings more meaning to our lives.
Ethics
Our lives are based on honor and integrity, and we believe that
honesty, compassion, trust, and knowledge are important.
OUR VISION
Freemasonry in California is a relevant and respected fraternity
committed to attracting, developing, and retaining members of high
quality who seek self-improvement and opportunities to make a
positive difference in their communities. Through the study of our
heritage and dedication to new and ongoing Masonic education,

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

35

members understand and can apply the tenets of Freemasonry. The
organization is well led and governed.
Our vision is measured by member vitality, lodge prosperity, and the
general awareness of Freemasonry throughout California. The plan
describes:
Member Vitality
As Masons in California are informed and well connected to their
lodge and to the Grand Lodge. They understand and practice what
it means to be a Mason both inside and outside the lodge. They
actively develop their Masonic knowledge and share it with others.
Lodge Prosperity
As a lodge is a brotherhood of Masons who attract and develop
leaders, retain quality members and inspire pride in the
membership and the community.
Community Awareness
That California communities are aware of Masonry’s mission and
values through lodge activities and programs that improve public
education and the general well being of fellow citizens.
The complete listing of this year’s Strategic Initiatives and the results
of this years efforts will be in my written message. I will highlight a few of this
years Strategic Initiatives in my remarks.
Ritual
Our Ritual is a beautiful expression of our values and is the foundation
for our Masonic Education. This year we held a Ritual Competition as a means
of recognizing those who present our Ritual in an outstanding manner. Tonight
we will recognize the winners of the Divisional Competitions and the
Jurisdiction Winner before the start of the French First Degree. The use of the
competition format has increased interest in the proper performance of our ritual
and will continue next year. I think it is a wonderful way to both encourage and
reward outstanding Ritual Work.
Grand Lodge has also undertaken a study of the methods of
supplementing the way we deliver the lectures for the three degrees. Many
Grand Lodges authorize the use of visual aids and have developed multi media
presentations that are used during the presentation of the lectures. The Deputy
Grand Master has headed up the effort to look at ways to supplement the
lectures with visual aids. I see this as an opportunity to enhance our ritual and
perhaps to put back into the lectures some of the material that over the years has
been taken out and placed into the monitor without adding more memory work.

36

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

I have seen the material done by other Grand Lodges and it is most impressive.
The Deputy Grand Master will report on this issue tomorrow morning.
Masonic Education
Last year I made a commitment to you that Masonic Education would
be a priority. I have symbolized that priority by making a presentation on
Masonic Education at each and every Grand Lodge Opening. In addition, I sent
a brief paper on Masonic Education to every lodge once a month so that it could
be read at a stated meeting, or included in the lodge trestleboard. However, the
major contribution to Masonic Education was the project which I assigned to the
Deputy Grand Master – the creation of a new approach to Masonic Education in
California, and the launching of a “grass roots” program which will eventually
pull together all the various aspects of Masonic Education into one
comprehensive program.
Last year you approved funding for Masonic Education as a part of the
Grand Lodge Budget, and as a result we were able to offer four workshops to
introduce the new Candidate Education booklets which I approved for
distribution, and to recruit volunteers interested in revamping our system of
Masonic Education in California. Those volunteers met on Saturday, October 1,
taking the first step in developing a comprehensive program of Masonic
Education. Over the next three years you will see the new program unfold.
Funds committed this year will enable this task force to create the new structure
for Masonic Education, and create new materials for use by our lodges.
This new program of Masonic Education will be different than any
which have gone before it, not only because it will be created by a task force
based at the lodge level, but also because it will be closely tied to the heart of
Freemasonry – the Three Degrees of Ancient Craft Masonry. Not only will it
focus on helping our candidates to truly understand the meaning of becoming a
Mason, it will create an unparalleled opportunity for each new Mason to commit
himself to lifelong learning. It will emphasize that the process of becoming a
Mason only starts with the degrees he receives. In order to truly become all that
he can be, every Mason in every lodge in California will have a chance to grow
daily in his understanding of Freemasonry. And this understanding will be
firmly grounded in Applied Masonry – that Masonry which makes a difference
for each individual Mason, for his lodge, for his family, and for his community.
My thanks to our Deputy Grand Master for his leadership this year in launching
this new program, and for others who helped bring it about.
Yesterday, I had the honor and privilege to cut the ribbon symbolizing
the opening of the Henry Wilson Coil Library on the 2nd floor of this beautiful
building. The library is a tribute to a man who loved Masonic Education and a
resource for each of us to pursue our Masonic Education efforts. I encourage
each of you to visit it and enjoy our heritage.

Grand Lodge Operations
One of the important duties of the Grand Master is the stewardship of
the Grand Lodge Budget and oversight of the Grand Lodge operations. Grand
Lodge staff has worked very hard this year to keep the budget balanced and I
am pleased to report that we are projecting a small surplus at the end of this
year’s budget that will be added to the Grand Lodge Endowment Fund. I have
taken a hard look at the way Grand Lodge operates to see if we could find ways
to reduce costs. My recommendations number 2005-05 Optional Grand Lodge
Officers and 2005-06 Change the Role and Selection of the Grand Lecturer are
two of the results of that review.
The issue of moving Grand Lodge out of San Francisco is often
mentioned as a means to reduce Grand Lodge expenses. I had a study done to
see if we could reduce costs by moving. The idea of selling this beautiful
building and using it to fund Grand Lodge is not an option. If the building is
sold all of the proceeds must go to the Masonic Home of California. It can not
be used for any other purpose. This was the intent of the members who raised
the funds to build it. The building generates revenue and on an annual basis
part of the profit is paid to the California Masonic Foundation as the owner of
the building. The report looked at different ways to move the Grand Lodge out
of this building to save costs. It did not make a difference where we proposed
to put the Grand Lodge office it was more expensive. One option might reduce
the cost of labor, but rental costs and transportation expenses were higher.
There was not a combination of these cost centers that would result in less costs
that could over come the reduced rent that we enjoy in this building due to the
combining of the staff members from the Homes, Foundation, CMMT with the
small Grand Lodge staff. There may come a time when we will be able to save
money by moving; therefore, I recommend that Grand Lodge periodically
review the report and update its assumptions. The report is included in my
written message.
The theft of Grand Lodge Jewels is a significant loss to our fraternity.
A full report on the theft will be made by the President of the California
Masonic Memorial Temple and I will reserve my comments on the issue until
after the report is presented.
Leadership
The leadership of our lodges is critical to the future of our craft. I
recently read the message to the craft of Grand Master Jonathan Doan Hines,
Grand Master of Masons in California in 1883-84. He stated in his message that
“Masters and Secretaries were elected to their offices just because they were
good fellows.” That concern in some cases is still true today. These two
officers are important positions that have an impact on the success of a lodge
and we need to elect people who are well qualified to do the work. The Grand

38

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Lodge Leadership Committee is working hard to provide a series of leadership
training course beginning with Lodge Certification Program and ending with the
Wardens’ Retreat. The Lodge Certification Program provides valuable training
on a wide range of subjects such as Hall Associations. The Wardens’ leadership
training is an excellent training program that every Warden; regardless of
whether you have previously served as Master or not will learn something new.
Grand Lodge continues to work to reduce the costs and the Deputy Grand
Master will report on an effort he is undertaking to help lodges that may not be
able to afford to send their Wardens. At a time when more and more of our
leaders are new Masons it is critical that our Wardens attend this excellent and
essential training.
Lodge Issues
This year several lodges have had problems with Hall Associations that
created significant problems for the lodge. In each of the cases, the Hall
Association had adopted the attitude that they were independent from the lodge.
In two cases the Hall Association Directors committed Masonic Misconduct
and are being tried for violating our code. Hall Association’s are owned by
their Lodge and must conform to the CMC and be open in their business
practices.
The Executive Committee has work very hard this year with the
Assistant Grand Lecturers and Inspectors in helping lodges overcome their
difficulties. Two of the lodges this year lost their charter because they did not
have qualified officers, were not doing their required financial reports and in
one case were not meeting on a regular basis. Grand Lodge has worked with
these lodges, sent committeemen to help, provided training and ultimately both
of these lodges now have qualified officers and I am pleased to inform you that
both lodges now have their charters and are again a positive force for good in
their communities. The Executive Committee continues to work with lodges
that are experiencing difficulties to help them to address their issues and keep
them a vital part of the Grand Lodge of California.
Membership
Membership continues to be a significant issue for our fraternity.
While our membership numbers are declining, our fraternity is not in decline.
The decline in membership will stop in the next six to eight years. The interest
in the fraternity is increasing. This year, my brothers, we had more degrees
performed than any of the last 12 years. My brothers, we averaged 3 Master
Masons raised per lodge which is the best average in the last 21 years. The
Masters and Wardens of our constituent lodges are making a difference and I
applaud you.

Interest in the values and teachings of our fraternity are still as
meaningful today as they have been since the formation of the Grand Lodge of
California in 1850. Lodges that are active in their community and provide
activities and programs that are of interest to their current members are
attracting new members.
While it is the responsibility of lodges to make men masons, Grand
Lodge does offer help to the lodges with membership. The Lodge Support
Committee has developed interesting lodge programs that can be used by
lodges. The Membership Development Committee has created a great
membership program. The Pass It On Program is a wonderful program that has
generated great interest in our craft and has been very successful for lodges that
use it. I encourage lodges to make use of the Pass It On Program. It works.
The movie National Treasure and the cover story in the US News and

encourage our lodges to make use of this renewed interest in our fraternity.
While we don’t have the National Treasure as depicted in the film, we do have
many treasures that we are willing to share. Whether it is our commitment to
the principle of the Constitution, our support for the public schools or the values
we teach as Masons, we have many treasures with which to share with anyone
who is interested. Masonry is a meaningful fraternity that has much to offer
society and we are proud to Pass It On.
Lodge Development
The creation of new lodges is the job of the Grand Lodge and over the
last couple of years our efforts have resulted in new lodges being formed. I had
the pleasure this year to constitute two new lodges, Academia Lodge No. 847 in
Oakland and Lux Lodge No. 846 in San Diego. Both of these lodges were
formed with the intent that they be small lodges with members devoted to
significant Masonic Education.
You will be voting on the issuing of Charters for two more lodges that
have been working under dispensation for the last year, Panamericana Lodge
UD in Granada Hills and Ararat Lodge UD in Sierra Madre. Both of these
lodges will be foreign language lodges using the current ritual of the Grand
Lodge of California.
Lastly there are also two clubs that have formed recently in Los
Angeles and Santa Cruz that are working hard to able to receive a dispensation
to form a lodge. One of the clubs proposes to be a regular lodge and the other
plans to follow the Academia and Lux Lodge format. There is plenty of room in
California for lodges of many styles, languages and goals. All it takes is not less
than 25 or more than 50 Master Masons in good standing to form a lodge.

The Grand Lodge of California is one Grand Lodge among many in the

Grand Master’s Special Fund
This year Teri and I selected the Traditions Unit at our Home in Union
City as the Grand Master’s Charity. The Traditions Unit provides care to our
residents suffering from dementia and Alzheimer. It is a terrible disease that
takes someone’s memories from them at a time in their lives in which that is
what gives them a great deal of their pleasure. Our therapists in the Traditions
Unit indicate that the more they can stimulate the minds of our resident, the
better chance they will hold on to their memories. One of the areas that
stimulates a lot of memories for our residents is being out in nature.
As you know we have a beautiful garden at the Home in Union City.
Our residents are elderly and they don’t spend much time in the garden,
regardless of the temperature, because the winds make it uncomfortable for
them to be outside. In order to make it possible for our residents to spend time
in the garden, we decide to build a Yurt. A Yurt is a Mongolian Tent Structure,
which is the oldest known structure in the world. It has many interesting
architectural features, so it is also interesting to us as Masons. It has multiple
sides, often with clear panels, a hard floor and a dome like roof structure. The
Yurt we have had built has windows in each of the multiple sides. The
fountains in the garden have been rebuilt to make them safe for our residents

42

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

and are incorporated into the Yurt. Our residents can now spend more time in
the garden.
I hope that many of you were able to see it yesterday when you visited
the Home. I was honored to be able to dedicate the rebuilt garden and the Yurt
last Saturday in a short ceremony for the residents. We have also had the flower
beds raised in the gardens so that our residents can work in the soil. It is
difficult for many of our residents to get down on their hands and knees and this
gives them an opportunity to again work in our mother earth. This also helps to
keep many pleasant memories alive for residents. We also raised money to
build memory boards. A memory board is just what is sounds like -- a board
with memories attached to it. It can be a ball, a bat, a glove, or a doll or some
other toy or item that our residents would be familiar with that are attached to a
board so they can’t be lost. It is amazing to see the power of touch that God has
given us, to see our residents spending hours running their hands over these
familiar objects and having memories unlocked that have been hidden away by
that insidious disease.
You have been very generous in your charity and as of this morning we
have raised nearly $48,000 for this project. Tonight the ladies will be attending
a play, called Love Letters, and a part of the ticket price will go to this charity.
You should be very proud of the work that is done in your name by the Masonic
Homes of California, in our Home at Union City, as well as at the Home in
Covina where we also have children or in the many communities around the
state where we are helping over 100 brothers and widows with Masonic
Outreach Services. Your support of our Masonic Homes makes a difference in
the lives of hundreds of people and you should be proud of your contribution to
this worthwhile cause.
Theme for 2005
My Theme this year is “Freemasonry, Values that Brighten Our
Communities, Share the Light”. The symbol of my theme is the Lighthouse. As
a lighthouse provides guidance to the mariner, traveling on a dark and stormy
night upon a turbulent sea, so that he can find safe harbor. So too does the
values of Freemasonry provide guidance to those adrift in our turbulent and
often dark and stormy world, so that they may find a safe harbor to anchor their
lives.
The word Freemasonry means not only our beloved blue lodges, it
represents all of the organizations that make up the Family of Freemasonry in
California. Whether it is one of the adult organizations or one of our youth
organizations, all of them teach important lessons that add to the lessons taught
in our blue lodges. Together these lessons showcase the values that we as
Masons hold dear. Our values are as important today, as they were 300 years

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

43

ago. The intolerance that we see around the world has created a wave of
terrorism in an effort to force people to conform to their values and beliefs.
Image how much better the world would be if everyone followed our
example of uniting men of every country, sect and opinion and causing true
friendships to exist among those who might otherwise remain at a perpetual
distance. Image how great our world would be if everyone believed that to
relieve the distressed is a duty incumbent on all men and that their goal was to
soothe the unhappy, to sympathize with their misfortunes, to compassionate
their miseries, and to restore peace to their troubled minds.
Our values are meaningful and when we practice outside of the lodge
those great moral lessons taught in it, the light of freemasonry will drive out the
darkness of intolerance and prejudice and we will make our communities, our
state, our nation and our world a brighter and better place. We are men of
principles we make a difference in the world.
We have provided families identification information for more than
300,000 kids since we began the Child ID program, you have make a difference
in countless families lives.
We have trained 4252 teachers since we began the Model Student
Assistance Program and they have assisted 41,346 children, you have made a
difference in those children’s lives.
We awarded $850,000 in scholarships to 140 college students this year.
Making college possible for some and more affordable for others, you have
made a difference in their lives.
We raised nearly $240,000 dollars in California this year to provide
relief to the victims of Hurricanes, Tsunamis and floods around the world; you
made a difference in countless people lives.
We raised nearly a million dollars for our Masonic Charities in
California this year that makes possible all of the good work done by the
California Masonic Foundation and the Masonic Homes of California. You are
making a difference in lives of our brothers and widows in need. You are
making a difference in countless ways in every community in our state where
Freemasonry is present.
We are men of principles, we believe in God, we believe in tolerance,
we believe in the goodness of man, we believe in freedom of thought, freedom
of religion and the right to self determination.
We are Freemasons; we make a difference in our communities, our
state, our country and the world.
We are sharing the light of Freemasonry.

Grand Master’s Written Message
Brethren of the Grand Lodge, in addition to the oral message that I
delivered at the Annual Communication there are several issues that need to be
reported in compliance with Section 1060 of the California Masonic Code.
Reportable Acts
There are a number of actions that I had to take as Grand Master that
did not rise to the level of a Grand Master’s Decision or Recommendation, but
are Official Acts that are required to be reported.
Clandestine Masonic Organizations
During my year as Grand Master, it came to my attention that there
were organizations that were either recruiting members in California or were
operating in California in violation of Section 15130 of California Masonic
Code which prohibits membership in any Masonic Organization that has not
been recognized by Grand Lodge. A Masonic Organization is any group,
chapter, order, club, association or organization that requiring Masonic
affiliation as a perquisite to membership, except Masonic Lodges (CMC Section
19).
Solomon Club
I became aware that a group calling itself the Solomon Club was
seeking members in California using the internet. It was not clear that
membership required a Masonic Affiliation or that the group was
meeting in California. I advised the craft that this organization was not
a recognized Masonic Organization in California and caution was order
of the day in any dealings with the group.
Knights Preceptors
In January of 2005 I became that a group of California Masons had
obtained a charter from an organization in Texas to open a chapter of
Knights Preceptors in California. They allegedly confer degrees, and
have a ritual which is only available in a tiled meeting to members who
must have a Masonic Affiliation. I inform those brothers that they
could not open such an organization in California without first
obtaining the approval of Grand Lodge at its annual communication.
They were provided with the information on how to request
recognition. They ceased operations and no request for recognition
was submitted to Grand Lodge for consideration.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

45

Snakes
It came to my attention in the summer of 2005 that an organization
calling itself “The Grand and Glorious Knights of the Creeping
Serpent” commonly called the Snakes was operating in California. The
organization has a ritual for initiation which is only available in a tiled
meeting to members who must have a Masonic Affiliation. I was
informed that the group had two chapters in California, one in the
North and one in the South. Apparently, the Snakes are an invitation
only organization and its membership. An investigation was conducted
that established that the Snakes do exist in Arizona and in the
Philippines and that California Masons have attended meetings in
Arizona as members of a California Chapter of the Snakes. California
Masons were also going to Arizona to be initiated into the Snakes. It
was also alleged that the group practice dangerous hazing rituals on its
candidates.
There is no record of such an organization having received recognition
from the Grand Lodge of California, nor would anyone step forward as
a leader of the group in California. The Grand Lodge of Arizona,
while recognizing the group in Arizona had no information on the
leadership of the Snakes in California. The Grand Master of Arizona
issued a decree that no California Mason was to be initiated into the
Snakes in Arizona. I sent a letter to all of the lodges in California
informing them of the existence of the Snakes and reminding our
members that they can not belong to such a group unless and until it is
recognized by the Grand Lodge of California. No one step forward on
behalf of the organization. I spoke with the Grand Master of the
Philippines about the group and while he was aware of its existence in
his country he denied any knowledge of its leadership or members. He
assured me that no California Mason would be allowed to join the
organization in his country.
Near the end of my term several brothers began coming forward and
provided information about the group and its members. Specific
direction was given to all those brothers about the dangers of belonging
to a clandestine organization and letters where sent out to the purported
leaders.
No one stepped forward to seek recognition of the
organization at the annual communication of Grand Lodge. I have left
the matter to our new Grand Master to continue to pursue as it is clear
that the Snakes are operating in California in violation of our law and
members who participate are in violation of their Masonic obligations.

46

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The purpose of the law is to protect our members and the fraternity’s
good name. There are many organizations that desire to claim the name of
Freemasonry to give them instant credibility and in some cases the ability to
solicit money using the fraternity’s good name. There have been incidents
where masons have formed unauthorized clubs that have hurt the fraternity. A
couple of years ago in New York, a lodge had a clandestine club within the
lodge that used hazing as a part of the induction into the club. The hazing
included the use of a blank pistol. Unfortunately, a member confused a real
pistol for a blank pistol and killed another member. That is why it is critical that
all organizations that require a Masonic affiliation must submit their
constitution, bylaws and rituals to Grand Lodge for review and approval at our
annual communication.
Violation of the California Masonic Code by the Scottish Rite in California
In March of 2005 I had to take a significant action as Grand Master in
addressing violations of the California Masonic Code by the Scottish Rite in
California.
As you may know, some years ago, the Valley of Los Angeles had to
move out of their Wilshire Blvd Building due to financial problems. They have
tried a number of things to make the building financial viable however they are
restricted by zoning laws as to what they can do at that location. Additionally,
there has been significant opposition from the homeowners association around
the building to any changes to the zoning.
Recently, the City of Los Angeles created neighborhood councils to
advise the local City Councilmember, Mayor and to give more people a voice in
the government of the city. These councils are advisory only and only have the
power to adopt their budget, based on the money allocated to them by the city.
One of these neighborhood councils was formed for area that includes the
Wilshire Blvd building, called the Greater Wilshire Neighborhood Council.
These councils do not require residency in the area to participate; they only
require that you have an interest in property within the area of the Council.
Therefore, a business owner, people who work in the area and people who own
property but don’t live there have as much right to vote in the election as
someone who actually lives there. These rules allow every member of the
Valley of Los Angeles to vote in the election.
It came to my attention that the Scottish Rite was lobbying its members
to run candidates that would be sympathic to their desire to have the zoning
changed to allow more profitable use of the building on Wilshire Blvd. My
investigation into the matter determined that the Sovereign Grand Inspector
General in California, (SGIG) was telling the Personal Representatives for the
valleys in Los Angeles County to lobby its members to vote for candidates
selected by the Scottish Rite.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

47

This is a violation of Section 380 of the California Masonic Code and I
issued the following edict dated March 22nd, 2005 to prevent any further
violations of the code.
Edict
It has come to my attention that the California Scottish Rite Leadership is
lobbying its members to vote in a specific way in the upcoming Elections for the
Greater Wilshire Neighborhood Council in Los Angeles. This has created
conflict among its members.
Section 380 of the California Masonic Code reads as follows:
§380.

POLITICS AND RELIGION.

Grand Lodge and constituent Lodges shall not interfere or voice an
opinion with regard to political or religious matters. Members, when
they are assembled in constituent Lodges or Grand Lodge, may not
discuss political or religious matters. Members must have a belief in a
Supreme Being and a future existence; however, no invocation,
benediction, or prayer shall be delivered which would have a sectarian
significance.
The purpose of Section 380 is to maintain peace and harmony within our
fraternity by preserving the right of all Masons to vote their individual
consciences and to follow their chosen religion without any interference from
Masonry.
Section 23190 of the California Masonic Code reads as follows:
§23190. MEMBERS OF MASONIC ORGANIZATIONS.
All members of Masonic Organizations within this Jurisdiction shall be
subject to the same standards of conduct and propriety as are or may
be imposed upon Master Masons in this Jurisdiction.
The purpose of Section 23190 is to make all Masonic Organizations conform to
the standards of conduct and propriety required by the California Masonic
Code. In my opinion Section 380 of the California Masonic Code is section
dealing with conduct and propriety and therefore applies to the California
Scottish Rite Bodies.
Therefore, the leadership of the California Scottish Rite shall cease lobbying its
members on how to vote in the Elections for the Greater Wilshire Neighborhood
Council in Los Angeles. Nor can its members discuss the election during any
tiled meetings of the Scottish Rite. Clearly all masons have a right to their
opinion and may express their opinions to other Masons; they just cannot do it
during a tiled meeting of any Masonic Organization or in the name of any
Masonic Organization(s).

48

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The organizational structure of the Scottish Rite makes it difficult for its leaders
to express their individual opinions about this election without it being the
official position of the Scottish Rite in violation of Section 380 of the California
Masonic Code. In order to avoid this conflict the leadership of Scottish Rite
may publish to its members information about the election process such as how
the vote is conducted, who can vote and how that is done and so forth. They
may also provide information about each of the candidates including any
official candidate’s statements, however, if they provide the information about
candidates, it must be for all candidates and they cannot take a position on any
candidate or infer their support in any way. The standard is that they maintain
the peace and harmony of their members by not voicing an opinion with regard
to religious matters.
A copy of the edict and my letter to the SGIG is on file at the Grand
Lodge office. The edict clearly states what the code requires and how it applies
to Scottish Rite. I issued instructions on what the Scottish Rite Leadership
could and could not do in informing its members of the election. I sent a copy
of the letter to the SGIG as well as to each of the Personal Representatives in
Los Angeles County since their lobbying of their members was also a violation
of our code independent of the SGIG’s violations. Copies of these letters are on
file at the Grand Lodge Office
I heard nothing from the SGIG, no phone call, no letter of
acknowledgement.
I did get an acknowledgement from the Personal
Representative for the Valley of Los Angeles and others that they would obey
the edict. I let the silence from the SGIG pass as acceptance of the edict. This
was not the first time that the SGIG had not responded to letters from the Grand
Master and since I was satisfied that no further violations would take place I felt
the matter was settled.
On April 15th the SGIG removed his Personal Representative for the
Valley of Los Angeles. He then berated him about my edict and stated that he
believed that he was behind the edict. These actions left the clear impression
that this was the cause of his removal. The SGIG has the absolute right to
replace his personal representative at any time for any reason; however, his
actions indicated to me that he was going to ignore my edict and continue to
violate the code. There was still no letter or phone call from the SGIG.
I then called the Sovereign Grand Commander in Washington D.C.,
whom I have met on other occasions and requested his assistance to resolve the
problem with his SGIG in California. I made it clear that I would do whatever it
took to see the California Masonic Code obeyed, including suspending the
SGIG, closing the Valley of Los Angeles and all of Scottish Rite in California if
necessary to prevent further violations of the California Masonic Code. After
several phone calls I was assured that the SGIG would obey the edict and that

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

49

the removal of the Personal Representative was an internal Scottish Rite matter
and had nothing to do with my edict. I required written acknowledgement of
the edict and assurances that it would be followed. I was told such a letter
would be written and signed by the SGIG.
While these calls were taking place, the SGIG appointed a new
Personal Representative for the Valley of Los Angeles. At an informal meeting
of the Valley of Los Angeles to discuss the removal of the Personal
Representative on April 22nd the new Personal Representative was introduced
and proceeded to tell the members of his plans for the valley. I have been
informed by several of the people in attendance that he stated that he wanted the
Valley to move back into the Wilshire Blvd Building and that the best way for
that to happen was to elect people to the Greater Neighborhood Council that
would support their efforts. His statement was a violation of the March 22nd
edict. This further violation of our code by the newest leader of Scottish Rite in
Los Angeles indicated to me that the SGIG had no intention of following the
California Masonic Code and was again telling each of the Personal
Representatives for the valleys in Los Angeles County to lobby its members to
vote for candidates selected by the Scottish Rite. I prepared to take action
against SGIG.
The Sovereign Grand Commander then assured me that the new
Personal Representative’s actions were his alone and not those of the SGIG. I
was assured that he would be removed as the Personal Representative and that
the SGIG would acknowledge the edict. I received a letter from the SGIG dated
April 26th, which is on file at the Grand Lodge Office that acknowledges my
letter of March 22nd. The letter also acknowledged that the Grand Lodge and
the Grand Master are the ultimate and final authority over all matters relating to
the craft including Masonic organizations in California. I have accepted this
letter and agreed not to take further action to discipline the SGIG. I made it
clear to the Sovereign Grand Commander and the SGIG that any further
violations of my edict or repudiation by the SGIG of his letter to me would
result in disciplinary action.
The new Personal Representative was allowed by the SGIG to resign
his position. The SGIG also has written a letter to former Personal
Representative to clear up any misconceptions about his removal. A copy of
that letter is on file at the Grand Lodge Office. As part of my agreement with
the Sovereign Grand Commander, I sent a letter to each of the Scottish Rite
valleys in California with a copy of my edict, a copy of the SGIG’s letter to me
and a copy of the letter to former Personal Representative in an effort to dispel
the rumors and to make the clear the supremacy of Grand Lodge and the
California Masonic Code. Copies of these letters are on file in the Grand Lodge
Office.
I gave the new Personal Representative several opportunities to explain
his actions on April 22nd. Ultimately, he finally denied saying anything that

50

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

violated the code or my edict. Since he was still the Chairman of the Scottish
Rite Museum Committee which operates its museum in the Wilshire Building
and therefore still in a leadership position and based on reports from several
different people present at the April 22nd meeting that he did in fact violate my
edict, I suspended his rights as a Master Mason to prevent any potential
violations of my edict pending an investigation into the events of April 22nd. I
appointed Most Worshipful M. William Holsinger to conduct an investigation
into his actions of April 22nd and to determine if Masonic charges should be
filed.
I consulted with Most Worshipful Jack Levitt, who is a member of the
Jurisprudence Committee. He reviewed the code, my edict and provided advice
to me that confirmed my powers to deal with violations of the California
Masonic Code including the suspension of a member. His advice and counsel
was helpful to me in resolving this matter.
At the conclusion of the investigation by Most Worshipful M. William
Holsinger, we discussed the results and whether Masonic Charges should be
filed. After considering all of the facts, including the fact that I let the SGIG
apologize and acknowledge the supremacy of the California Masonic Code
without Masonic Charges, I decided to give the new Personal Representative the
same opportunity. In July of 2005, Most Worshipful M. William Holsinger
contacted him and I was assured that he would write a letter similar to one I
received from the SGIG. Upon receipt of the letter, I agreed to lift the
suspension. If he did not write the letter, then I would report his suspension as a
Grand Master’s Decision, which if approved by Grand Lodge would make the
suspension the same as a penalty from a Masonic Trial. Several months passed
and no letter arrived. In October as I was preparing for the Annual
Communication, I contacted him and reminded him that if he did not write the
letter that I would present his suspension as a Grand Master’s Decision. The
letter finally arrived a few days before the Annual Communication.
Consequently, I lifted the suspension effective at the close of the Annual
Communication and did not report the suspension as a Grand Master’s decision.
This was a significant incident involving a Masonic organization in
California. While I was prepared to issue the first reprimand to a Past Grand
Master in over a century, I believe the solution that I crafted protects the
authority of Grand Lodge while allowing the SGIG the opportunity to retire
with dignity. I am satisfied that I have acted within the code and that the matter
is resolved.
All Masons must conform to the California Masonic Code, whether
they are the Grand Master, a Past Grand Master or the newest Entered
Apprentice, we are all Masons who took our obligation upon the Holy Bible in
open lodge to conform and uphold the California Masonic Code.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

Authorization of the Grand Lodge of Iran in Exile to
Perform a First Degree in California
I requested the Grand Lodge of Iran in Exile to perform a First Degree
using their ritual at Santa Monica Lodge. Most Worshipful Ali Razi, Grand
Master of the Grand Lodge of Iran in Exile agreed to my request. All California
Master Masons were invited to attend the degree. The purpose of my request
was to provide Masonic Education about how the rituals of the various Grand
Lodges around the world may be different, yet they all deliver the same
important moral lessons of our beloved fraternity.
The degree was presented on April 14th at the Santa Monica Masonic
Center. There were many Grand Lodge Officers present from the Grand Lodge
of California, the Most Worshipful Prince Hall Grand Lodge of California and
the Grand Lodge of Iran in Exile as well as brethren from several lodges. It was
a great evening with Masonic Fellowship before the degree and at a table lodge
after the degree. All present were greatly impressed by the degree and it was a
wonderful example of the universal nature of our fraternity.
Lodge Supervision
One of the most important tasks of the Grand Master is to insure that
Masters and Lodges conform to the California Masonic Code. When a problem
arises with a Master or a lodge, generally wise counsel from an Inspector, an
Assistant Grand Lecturer or the Grand Master is all it takes to correct the
problem. It is the rare occasion when the Grand Master has to take a significant
action. Over the last couple of years a number of lodges have had their charters
suspended by the Grand Master for serious misconduct. When I was installed as
Grand Master there were three lodges that had their charters suspended by Most
Worshipful Howard Kirkpatrick, but had been making good progress in
resolving their problems. As a result Grand Master Kirkpatrick did not
recommend that their charters be revoked because I agreed to continue the
suspension so the lodge could have more time to resolve their problems and not
lose their charters.
On October 14, 2004, as one of my first official acts I renewed the
suspension of the following lodges:

52

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Culver City – Foshay Lodge No. 467
The Charter had been suspended by Grand Master Kirkpatrick and he
had given them specific instructions on what they needed to do get
their charter back. Since the lodge had been making good progress in
resolving the conflicts among its members that resulted in abuse of the
ballot boxes, I agreed to continue the suspension. The lodge was given
a list of tasks to complete. Once they had completed all of their tasks
and they were able to qualify its principal officers I restored their
charter on April 6, 2005.
La Jolla Lodge No. 518
The Charter had been suspended by Grand Master Kirkpatrick and I
agreed to continue the suspensions. The lodge was making good
progress in rebuilding the lodge from time when its charter was
suspended because the lodge did not have qualified officers and had
not been meeting on a regular basis. The officers placed in charge of
the lodge after the suspension did a great job of rebuilding the lodge.
The lodge had to rebuild the finances of the lodge, rebuild the lodge
activities and get its members to return to the lodge. Once the lodge
completed all of these tasks and was able to qualify its principal
officers for the coming Masonic year, I was pleased to restore their
Charter on September 12, 2005.
During the course of my tenure as Grand Master I found it necessary to
suspend the charters of the following lodges:
Marin Lodge No. 191
The Master of Marin Lodge and its Treasurer accused the Hall
Association of financial irregularities and demanded that an audit be
done. The lodge refused to conduct the audit. Grand Master
Kirkpatrick looked into the issue and determined that an audit was
justified and in fact was required to clear up the allegations of Masonic
Misconduct. Marin Lodge was directed to undertake the audit. The
lodge at a stated meeting voted to delay the audit until after the Master
and Treasurer were no longer in office. This delay of several months
was on top of several months of delaying and would people time to
cover of misconduct. Therefore, on October 16, 2005, I suspended the
lodges charter and ordered that the audit forthwith.
The audit uncovered violations of the Masonic Code by the Hall
Association and charges were filed against the brother responsible.
The suspension of the charter and the closer oversight uncovered

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

53

serious conflicts among the members of the lodge. Accusations of
Masonic misconduct were made by members of the different factions
and disharmony prevailed in the Lodge. Unfortunately, the problems
could not be resolved before the annual communication. However,
progress was being made and it appeared to me that given some more
time the lodge could be saved. Therefore, I recommended to the new
Grand Master that he continue the suspension and when he agreed I did
not ask the Grand Lodge to revoke their charter.
Visalia-Mineral King Lodge No. 128
In October of 2004 it came to my attention that the lodge could qualify
its proposed officers for 2005 and its current officers would not
continue to serve. The lodge was also facing a financial crisis as it was
spending down its capital and would be bankrupt within the next 24 to
36 months. The lodge did not want to surrender its charter nor would it
take the necessary actions to bring itself into compliance with the
California Masonic Code. Therefore, on October 31, 2004 I suspended
the lodge’s charter.
I replaced the officers of the lodge and set them on a course to rebuild
the financial stability of the lodge, qualify its officers and to get enough
of its members to participate in the activities of the lodge. The new
officers worked very hard, the lodge membership came back together
and did the things necessary to rebuild the finances of the lodge and its
hall association. Once the lodge was no longer threaten with financial
ruin, the lodge set about putting in a place a qualified team of officers.
The lodge demonstrated to the satisfaction of the Grand Lecturer that
they could perform the degrees of Masonry in conformance with our
law. I then allowed the lodge to again perform degrees and to select
their officers. Once the newly elected and appointed officers qualified
for their respective offices, I returned their charter on September 7,
2005. I am very proud of the hard work done by the lodge and the
Inspector to rebuild this lodge and to keep the light of Freemasonry
shining in Visalia.
Southwest Lodge No. 283
Members of Southwest Lodge complained that a member of their lodge
had stolen significant amounts of money from the Hall Association and
that members of the Hall Association had covered up the theft and the
lodge was not informed of the theft nor were Masonic Charges filed. I
directed that inquiries be made to see if there was any truth to the
allegations and if true that the Master take the appropriate actions to
file charges. Some time passed and no progress was being made to

54

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
determine if the allegations were true. The Directors of the Hall
Association would not provide the necessary information to the Master
and claimed the records were lost or missing. I then directed the
Inspector to conduct an investigation into the matter. As a result of the
Investigation, it became clear to me that most likely the theft did occur,
that the Hall Association had not been providing the lodge with
required financial information, that the Hall Association members
could not or would not produce their records for Inspection by the
Master or the Grand Master’s representative and that the Master could
not or would not compel them to do so.
Therefore, on March 3, 2005 I suspend the charter of the lodge and
closed its operations until an independent audit of the lodges records
were conducted. I removed all of the members of the Hall Association
and demanded that the records of the hall association be turned over to
the Inspector. Failure to turn over the records would result in Masonic
Charges and if necessary in the filing of a civil suit against each of the
hall association members. I also called a meeting of the lodge where I
ordered all members living within a specified distance of the Lodge
Hall to attend the meeting. Failure to attend without an acceptable
excused would result in Masonic charges. Consequently, there was
standing room only at the meeting, where my representative informed
the lodge of the reasons for the suspension of the charter and what
would be required of the lodge to get it back. The membership was
shocked to learn what had been going on in the lodge. Committees
were formed with my approval to find and take possession of the Hall
Association records. Another committee was formed to investigation
the allegations of theft and cover up by members of the Hall
Association. Another committee was formed to assist in the audit of
the lodge records.
The audit of the lodge’s records revealed no theft of lodge funds, but
did uncover violations of the Masonic Code. The lodge proposed
changes to their procedures to bring them into conformity with the
code and once the investigation into the Hall Association determined
that the Master, Wardens and lodge secretary were not involved in
Masonic Misconduct, I allow the lodge to operate on a limited basis
while they work to solve the problems with the Hall Association. They
were able to find some of the Hall Association records and were able to
confirm that a theft did take place and that there was a cover-up. The
lodge was in the process of getting its financial house in order and was
about to file Masonic Charges against the brother who committed the
theft and those that covered up the theft at the time of the Annual
Communication. I believed that the lodge was well on its way to
getting its charter back, they just needed more time. Therefore, I
recommended to the new Grand Master that he continue the suspension

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

55

and when he agreed I did not ask the Grand Lodge to revoke their
charter.
Abell Lodge No. 146
Based on information from the annual reports of the lodge and from an
examination of the lodge records by a representative of the Grand
Master several significant problems were found. There was no
evidence in the minutes of the required candidates’ proficiencies and
completion of the Basic Candidate Education Course before
advancement. There was no evidence in the minutes of the lodge that
the required Annual and Semi-Annual Financial Reports of the
Secretary and Treasurer have been made to the lodge. There was no
evidence in the minutes of the lodge that the required Annual and
Semi-Annual Financial Reports of the Hall Association have been
delivered to the lodge. There was no evidence that officers are
proficient in the ritual for the positions they hold, and no evidence of
any plan to remedy this situation. The lodge did not want to surrender
its charter nor would it take the necessary actions to bring itself into
compliance with the California Masonic Code. Therefore, on March 4,
2005, I suspended the lodge’s charter.
A plan was put into place to help the lodge overcome its problems
using Grand Lodge committeemen, the Inspector and the Assistant
Grand Lecturer. Unfortunately, the problems could not all be resolved
before the annual communication. However, progress was being made
and it appeared to me that given some more time the lodge could be
saved. Therefore, I recommended to the new Grand Master that he
continue the suspension and when he agreed I did not ask the Grand
Lodge to revoke their charter.
Theft of Grand Lodge Jewels
The report of the Theft of the Jewels by the President of the California
Masonic Memorial Temple made at the annual communication, including my
remarks are included in the proceedings. The investigation of the theft by the
San Francisco Police Department and our private investigator is following
several leads. The new Grand Master was briefed on the case and has indicated
that he will provide an update to the craft on the Investigation.
Report on the Study of Moving Grand Lodge out of San Francisco
