GRAND MASTER’S DECISIONS AND RECOMMENDATIONS
The Board of Trustees of the Masonic Homes have been charged with
the responsibility of providing high quality services to our residents and
protecting the resources entrusted to their care. In its ongoing efforts to meet
these obligations, the Board of Trustees (Board) for the Masonic Homes of
California (Homes) has identified a significant funding problem that could be
facing the Homes in a few years. Currently the cost to provide health care is
increasing two and one half times the rate of inflation. A significant part of the
Homes budget is affected by health care costs. The Board has projected the
Homes’ budget for next five years, and there is a potential funding shortfall due
to rising costs and a static funding stream. Over the past few years, the Board
has worked hard to achieve operational efficiencies and reduce expenses but it
predicts that these efforts will not be enough to offset rising costs. It is not
prudent to anticipate an upturn in the stock market that would increase the
revenue from investments above their current performance level. Therefore, the
Board is looking at additional ways to reduce costs and to increase revenue. It
has been many years since the Board has asked Grand Lodge to levy a per capita
assessment of the membership for the support of the Homes under Section
13020.A.3 of California Masonic Code. The Board does not want to use this
source of funding, but it may be forced to ask if it cannot otherwise maintain a
balanced budget.
With these concerns, the Board has asked the Grand Master to clarify
the circumstances under which the Homes may provide the required services for
its residents. The Board has also asked what it can do with the underperforming
assets of the Homes. I have addressed their questions in separate decisions.
Grand Master Doan’s Decision No. 1
Masonic Homes Duty to Provide Care
The California Masonic Code in Section 8010 provides in part that,
“The Board of Trustees of the Masonic Homes of California shall control,
conduct and manage homes, hospitals and other facilities for the care,
maintenance and support of the aged, indigent or infirm Master Masons, their
wives, widows and children, and schools for the education of such children….”
There is no question that the Homes must provide for the care,
maintenance and support of its residents. It is also clear that this support
includes medical care (such as hospital care and skilled nursing care) and
education of our residents who are children. Section 8010 can be read to require
the Homes to own and operate homes, hospitals, schools and other facilities in
order to provide the required services. It has been more that 80 years since the
Homes has operated a hospital and the Homes has never operated a school for

156

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

children. The Board found that it could provide quality hospital care more
effectively by contracting with a community hospital than the Board could
provide itself. The Board has always partnered with the local school district for
the education of the child residents of the Covina campus. The Board also
provides skilled nursing care in Covina by contracting with service providers in
the area. I also noted that CMC Section 8010 allows the Board to grant
non-resident assistance instead of admission to the Homes. Over the years, the
Board has changed the way it provides quality service to its residents when a
more economical means of providing a service was available. To do otherwise
is not logical nor in keeping with our traditions.
Therefore, it is my decision that CMC Section 8010 should be read to
allow the Board to continue the tradition of providing the highest quality care
available to its residents in the most appropriate and effective setting. This
includes contracting out of services to others who can provide those services
more economically than the Homes can provide.
If you approve this decision, the following narrative will be added to
the appendix of the California Masonic Code in which the Grand Master’s
interpretative decisions are recorded:
Interpretation of Section 8010 of the California Masonic Code.
Masonic Homes Duty to Provide Care
(2005 G.M. Decision No. 1)
The Board may provide the required services to its residents in the
most economical manner while maintaining an acceptable quality of care. This
includes contracting out of services to others who can provide those services
more economically than the Homes can provide. The Board is not required to
provide these services in homes, hospitals, schools and other facilities owned
and operated directly by the Homes.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Under California Masonic Code section 8010, the Board of Trustees of
the Masonic Homes of California is required to control, conduct and manage
homes, hospitals and other facilities for the care, maintenance and support of the
aged, indigent or infirm Master Masons, their wives, widows and children, and
schools for the education of such children. The Board has expressed concern
about its financial ability to continue properly providing high quality services to
residents of the Homes as mandated by this code section. Among other things,
rising health care costs, coupled with possible reduced investment income, have
caused the Board to look for additional funding sources, as well as additional
cost saving measures, should the need arise.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

157

One method of reducing costs, which has been in practice for decades,
is for the Board to enter into outside contracts for the provision of services,
rather than providing such services directly. For example, the Board has
contracted with local community hospitals to provide certain health care, rather
than operating its own hospital. Another example is sending child residents to
local schools in Covina, rather than operating its own school on the grounds of
the Covina Home. This is both sound economic policy and consistent with the
mandate of section 8010.
Grand Master Doan has been asked to clarify the circumstances under
which the Board may properly contract out the provision of services it is duty
bound to provide. In his Decision 2005-1, the Grand Master has made it clear
that the Board of Trustees, in fulfilling its duties under California Masonic Code
section 8010, may contract for services to be provided to residents of the
Homes, when it finds it is both economically advantageous to do so, and the
contracting provider will render quality service.
This Decision is consistent with the policy and general purposes of this
Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee finds that there is no financial impact to the
Grand Lodge budget or per capita.
Decision AFFIRMED.
Grand Master Doan’s Decision No. 2
Masonic Homes Right to Use Assets
The California Masonic Code in Section 8040 provides: “The nonprofit corporation formed under the laws of California, known as ‘Masonic
Homes of California’ shall be vested the title to all property, real and personal,
including the Masonic Homes Endowment Fund, and all rights of action
belonging to the charity, or to which it may become entitled.” The Board
clearly controls all of the assets of the Homes. The Board has a fiduciary duty
to see that the assets are used to the best benefit of the Homes and to protect
those assets for future generations.
The Board is considering several plans to make use of its vacant land.
Before the Board undertakes any of these plans, it asked the Grand Master to
determine whether it can use this under utilized asset to increase the income to
the Homes. I believe that CMC Section 8040 clearly gives the Board the right
to use Homes assets as it is considering. In fact, the California Masonic
Memorial Temple board did the same thing when it rented the Temple to raise

158

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

funds for the maintenance of the building. However, my opinion in this matter
is only binding while I am Grand Master. Even if you approve my
interpretation, it does not bind my successor and any plan to increase the income
on the Homes’ vacant land will take more than one Grand Master’s term to
complete. However, your vote will be some assurance to the Homes Board that
it may proceed.
Therefore, it is my decision that CMC Section 8040 allows the Board
to make use of the Homes assets, such as vacant land, as it deems appropriate
for the benefit of the Homes. The Board can form a wholly owned corporation
if necessary to make use of its assets. The Board may not use any of it assets in
such a way that would violate other provisions of the CMC or force a change in
the admission policies of the Homes.
If you approve this decision, the following narrative will be added to
the appendix of the California Masonic Code in which the Grand Master’s
interpretative decisions are recorded:
Interpretation of Section 8040 of the California Masonic Code.
Masonic Homes Right to Use Assets
(2005 G.M. Decision No. 2)
Section 8040 allows the Board to may make use of the Homes assets,
such as vacant land, as it deems appropriate for the benefit of the Homes. The
Board can form a wholly owned corporation if necessary to make use of its
assets. The Board may not use any of its assets in such a way that would violate
other provisions of the Code or force a change in the admission policies of the
Homes.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
As required by California Masonic Code section 8040, all assets of the
Masonic Homes of California are held by a non-profit corporation. The Board
of Trustees of the Homes is vested with the duty to properly manage those
assets. The Board is currently considering several plans to make use of certain
vacant land held by the corporation in order to generate additional income to be
used for operation of the Homes.
Before undertaking steps to implement any of its plans for the vacant
land, the Board wanted assurance that it had the authority to do so. Thus, the
Board requested the Grand Master to determine whether it had the authority to
proceed.
In his Decision 2005-1, the Grand Master noted that the Board has
expressed concern about its financial ability to continue properly providing high
quality services to residents of the Homes. Putting vacant land to its highest and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

159

best use is one way in which the Board can raise the funds necessary to
accomplish this purpose. Accordingly, in Decision 2005-2, Grand Master Doan
has determined that, under the provisions of section 8040, the Board of Trustees
has the necessary authority to use the assets of the Homes, such as vacant land,
as it deems appropriate for the benefit of the Homes. Grand Master Doan notes
that while this decision does not bind future Grand Masters, it should give some
comfort to the Board that it may properly proceed to implement its plans.
This Decision is consistent with the policy and general purposes of this
Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee finds that there is no financial impact to the
Grand Lodge budget or per capita.
Decision AFFIRMED.
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 1
Lodge Investment Policy
Section 21030 of the CMC establishes the policy for Lodge
Investments. It is a prudent policy that is designed to protect lodge assets so
they are available to meet the lodge’s obligations for taking care of its members
and the lodge’s needs. This year two issues came to my attention that convinced
me that the policy needs to be modified.
First, I have learned that a number of our California lodges are in
violation of our California Masonic Code with respect to investments. A
prudent investment strategy, in today’s economic environment, includes
holdings in a broad-based portfolio of stocks. Most individual investors do not
have sufficient investment capital to purchase a sufficiently diverse equity
portfolio of individual stocks. These investors often choose mutual funds that
invest in a wide range of companies and industries. A popular type of mutual
fund for this purpose is one that tracks the S&P (Standard and Poors) 500 index.
Indeed, one of the investment options offered to California lodges through our
Grand Lodge Committee on Investments is the Vanguard Group Fund which
tracks the S&P 500. Unfortunately, while this is certainly considered by many
to be a prudent investment, our California Masonic Code does not allow it. This
has happened because the restrictions in our Code have not kept pace with the
changing standards in the industry.
The second issue involves the prohibition of lodges investing in real
estate. One of our lodges approached me with the opportunity to finance a real
estate deal in their community that would have benefited a community project

160

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

while at the same time providing a better potential return on the investment for
the lodge than could be earned in any authorized investment.
The lodge has a significant amount of money and the proposed
investment would have only locked up a portion of their funds for a short time.
The investment would have been collateralized with the property that was
professionally appraised for more than the amount of the loan. It appeared to be
an attractive and prudent investment for the lodge and a good deal for their
community. I had to deny their request because the CMC does not allow any
investment in real estate that is not going to be used for a lodge building.
I do not propose that all real estate deals be authorized, as many would
require that a lodge’s money be unavailable for too long a period of time.
Therefore, I propose to give the Grand Master the ability to authorize
investments on a case-by-case basis after consulting with the Grand Treasurer
and the Grand Lodge Investment Committee. This will allow the Grand Master
to approve the current mutual fund that is in use by many lodges, so they don’t
have to divest of the valuable asset just because our rules have not kept up with
the industry standards. It would also allow future Grand Masters to approve
some short-term real estate investments that appear to be in the best interest of
the lodge.
It is therefore my recommendation that we authorize the Grand Master
to add to the list of permissible investments for California lodges such other
prudent and appropriate investments as may be approved from time to time by
the Grand Master after consultation with the Grand Treasurer and the Grand
Lodge Committee on Investments.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Section 21030 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:
§21030. INVESTMENT.
Exclusive of a Lodge investment in an incorporated Temple Association, a
Lodge may invest its funds only as provided in this section. All Lodge
investments shall be held in the name of the Lodge. The Lodge’s investments
allowed under this section shall be administered either by an investment
committee appointed by the Master or by a Board of Trustees elected by the
Lodge. If a Lodge uses a Board of Trustees to administer the Lodge’s
investments, the Trustees shall be governed by a standing resolution of the
Lodge, the form of which shall be promulgated by the Grand Master.
Through the Lodge investment committee or Board of Trustees, the Lodge shall
confer and counsel as to any proposed or contemplated investments under this
section with a Registered Investment Advisor of its choice. A Lodge may freely
choose to accept or reject any and all recommendations made to it by the
Advisor. Alternatively, a Lodge may retain a Registered Investment Advisor

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

161

operating as an investment manager who offers standardized portfolios through
a managed investment account.
A Lodge may invest its assets, exclusive of Lodge Temple property and Lodge
Temple securities, as follows:
A.

B.

At least 20% of the assets available for investment, as valued at the
time of deposit or purchase, must be in:
1.

Cash or cash equivalents;

2.

Accounts of depository institutions insured by the Federal
Deposit Insurance Corporation, including but not limited to
checking accounts, savings accounts and certificates of
deposit;

3.

Obligations of the United States Government or obligations
whose payment is pledged by the full faith and credit of the
United States Government;

4.

Obligations of the State of California and its political
subdivisions including but not limited to general obligation
bonds, revenue bonds, warrants, revenue anticipation notes,
certificates of participation and comparable securities;

5.

Short-term evidences of indebtedness with a maturity of not
greater than one year and whose issuer holds a credit quality
of Prime A1, A2 or A3, as determined by a nationallyrecognized credit rating organization, including but not
limited to commercial paper, bankers acceptances, repurchase
agreements, certificates of deposit and comparable securities;
or

6.

Investment companies or investment trusts that invest in the
above referenced financial instruments.

Up to 80% of the assets available for investment, as valued at the time
of deposit or purchase, may be invested in:
1.

Evidences of indebtedness, preferred stock and common
stocks which must be fully registered with the Securities and
Exchange Commission and whose issuers have a market
capitalization of at least $500 million; or

2.

Investment companies or investment trusts that invest in the
above referenced financial institutions.; or

3.

Such other prudent and appropriate investments as may be
approved from time to time by the Grand Master after
consultation with the Grand Treasurer and the Grand Lodge
Committee on Investments.

162

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The Grand Master, in his Recommendation 2005-01, suggests the
enlargement of the opportunities for lodge investments, but in a prudent way so
that the lodge assets may be protected and so that they are available “to meet the
lodge’s obligations for taking care of its members and members in need.” He
recognizes that the policy of the Grand Lodge is conservative in its investment
approach for the benefit of the lodges. Of course, it should be conservative so
that the obligations of the lodge, as to its members and its own needs, are met.
The Grand Master proposes that Section 21030 of the California
Masonic Code be amended so as to add the opportunities of other investments
than previously listed, but only with the approval of the Grand Master, after
consultation with the Grand Treasurer and Grand Lodge Committee on
Investments. This Recommendation is a reasonable and desirable approach in
giving the lodges opportunities to invest their funds in a prudent way, while
safeguarding them by having them reviewed and approved by the Grand Master
and those in Grand Lodge who are charged to monitor these activities.
This Recommendation is consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee finds that there is no financial impact to the
Grand Lodge budget or per capita.
Report of the Committee on Investment:
Grand Master’s Recommendation No. 2005-01 seeks to amend Section
21030 B 3 of the California Masonic Code entitled Lodge Investment. If
approved, this change will allow lodges to invest in:
“Such other prudent and appropriate investments as may be
approved from time to time by the Grand Master ‘after
consultation with the Grand Treasurer and the Grand Lodge
Committee on Investments.’”
This is a new addition to the section of the Code which currently
allows lodge investment in stocks and mutual funds of companies where the
market capitalization is at least $500 million.
This change to the CMC is necessary to allow lodges to invest in the
stock of large, well established companies whose capitalization may not be at
the half billion dollar level.
It also allows lodges to invest in other prudent and worthwhile
investments that are not otherwise authorized by the Code.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

163

The Grand Lodge Investment Committee believes this change is
necessary to allow ldoges to invest in other, prudent investment vehicles that
would otherwise be prohibited by the CMC.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 2
Use of Raffles by Concordant and Appendant Masonic Organizations
It has long been the policy of the Grand Lodge of California to prohibit
gambling. It is not appropriate for our lodges to use the proceeds of gambling to
fund their activities. A lodge is expected to fund its activities from the funds it
obtains from its members and the name of Masonry can not be used to promote
gambling. This policy has helped our great fraternity earn the positive
reputation that we enjoy.
It has come to my attention that some of our Concordant and
Appendant Masonic Organizations have been violating the CMC by conducting
raffles. I have taken action to stop those violations. As a result, I have been
informed of violations by other groups including some of our youth groups.
Each of these violations were to benefit a charity or for a youth group to raise
funds for some special event. Whether it is called a raffle or an opportunity
drawing it is really a raffle in the meaning of the CMC. As I looked at the
problem it has become clear to me that there is a need for change. As our
membership declines, our monetary support for our charities and youth groups
has also diminished. In some cases our support for our youth groups has
declined very significantly. While our charities have other options for raising
funds to meet their needs, our youth groups do not have many options.
Society’s acceptance of lotteries for the benefit of education and
changes to state law make it clear that society will not have a negative view of a
limited use of raffles for the benefit of charitable causes or our youth groups for
special events such as paying the cost of attending leadership camps or the cost
of attending state and national meetings. As long as the money raised by the
raffle is not used for the normal operating expenses of the organization, they do
not give aid and countenance of Masonry to gambling enterprises and the raffles
are conducted in accordance with all federal, state and local laws they should be
authorized. Under no circumstances shall a Craft Lodge accept any of the
proceeds or profits of any raffle authorized by this change.
Resolution 05-12 seeks to change our laws to permit fund raising
through gambling. This resolution would also remove the authority of the
Grand Master to act against a Mason who promotes gambling enterprises
conducted in the name of, or for the benefit of, any lodge or Masonic
organization.
This resolution goes too far and would allow the name of

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

164

Masonry to be used to promote gambling and would allow lodges to benefit
from the proceeds of gambling. I can not support that resolution and have
proposed this recommendation to address the needs of our concordant and
appendant bodies while maintaining the integrity of our lodges. The change that
I am recommending is similar to the change you authorized a few years ago to
lift the restrictions on advertising from our concordant and appendant bodies.
This change will only apply to Concordant and Appendant Masonic
Organizations and not to the Craft Lodges.
It is therefore my recommendation that we authorize Concordant and
Appendant Masonic Organizations to conduct raffles for the benefit of their
charities and for our Masonic Youth Orders to be able to conduct raffles for
their charities and special needs such as leadership development and travel to
special events.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Section 1060 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:
§1060. DUTIES.
The Grand Master shall:
A.

Preside in Grand Lodge at all Annual and Special Communications.
There shall be no appeal from his rulings as presiding officer;

B.

Present, at each Annual Communication, a written message setting
forth:
1.

All his official acts during the year;

2.

The general condition of Masonry within the Jurisdiction; and

3.

Recommending such legislation as he deems necessary or
expedient for the welfare of the Craft;

C.

Constitute the Lodge for which a charter is granted in accordance with
the ancient usages and regulations. He may do so either in person or
by a duly authorized representative;

D.

Cause to be personally served on any Mason appearing to be engaged
in the conduct or promotion of any raffle, lottery or other form of
gambling not permitted hereunder that is being conducted, or is about
to be conducted, in the name of, or for the benefit of any committee or
other subordinate body of any Lodge or Masonic Organization, a
notice requiring that Mason to immediately discontinue his
participation.
If the Mason fails to discontinue his participation in such enterprise,
the Grand Master shall refer the matter to a Lodge of this Jurisdiction
for trial on the charge of unmasonic conduct. The Junior Warden of

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

165

the Lodge shall prefer the charges against the Mason. Pending the
trial, the Grand Master may suspend the Mason from all the rights and
benefits of Masonry;
E.

Exercise general and careful supervision over the Craft, and see that
the Constitution and Ordinances of Grand Lodge are strictly
maintained, supported, and obeyed; and

F.

Discharge all necessary executive functions of Grand Lodge when it is
not in session.
Section 15090 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:

§15090. GAMBLING.
Neither a Lodge, a Masonic Organization, nor a Mason shall, directly or
indirectly, give or attempt to give the aid or countenance of Masonry to, or
participate in, the conduct or preparation of any raffle, lottery or other form of
gambling, conducted in the name of, or directly or indirectly for the monetary
benefit of, any Lodge or of any Masonic Organization. A Lodge shall not
accept any of the proceeds or profits of any raffle, lottery or other gambling
enterprise.
Prizes may be awarded if not purchased from monies collected as admission
fees and if no separate charge is made for the chance to win.
The foregoing notwithstanding, Masonic Organizations, and their members may
conduct raffles for the benefit of their charities and Masonic Youth Orders may
conduct raffles for their charities and special needs such as leadership
development and travel to special events, if conducted strictly in accordance
with all applicable federal, state and local laws and with the provisions
hereunder related to financial support.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation 20055-02 seeks to change
section 15090 of the California Masonic Code to permit Masonic Organizations
and their members, as well as Youth Organizations, to conduct raffles or
lotteries to raise money for their charities and specific needs, if conducted in
accordance with all applicable Federal, State and Local laws.
The temptation to seek an easy way to raise funds is forever present,
but the strict Masonic Laws regarding gambling which were framed by our
forefathers have served well to protect our reputation from scandal or from
negative community opinions of our Fraternity.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

166

The laws controlling gambling in California are detailed and complex.
Only “eligible organizations” may legally conduct a raffle; Masonic
Organizations may not be eligible. Those organizations which do qualify must
provide information and be subject to audits as follows:
1.

The Organization Name

2.

All its tax and corporate numbers

3.

Name and title of a responsible fiduciary for the organization

4.

Pay an annual registration fee

5.

Become part of an automated database of raffle registrants

6.

Subject itself to audits of its records and other documents to ensure
compliance

7.

And, once registered, the organization is required to file a detailed
report of it’s raffle operations annually thereafter.

Organizations are exempt from registration if they are religious
organizations, cemetery corporations or charitable corporations organized as
religious organizations, educational institutions, hospitals or health care plans.
A raffle is exempt [from registration] if it satisfies all of the following:
1.

It involves a general and indiscriminate ticket distribution.

2.

The tickets are offered on the same conditions as tickets for which
donations are given.

3.

And the scheme does not require any participant to pay for a chance to
win.

Finally, notwithstanding that these changes only apply to our
concordant, appendant and youth organizations, every person who lets or
permits any building to be used for the purposes of [a lottery] [conducting an
unauthorized raffle] is committing a crime.
It has forever been the policy of our Grand Lodge to enact such
legislation which would protect our reputation and good standing in our
communities. Venturing into the world of gambling and encouraging our youth
organizations to participate as well, may dim our luster and bring our morals
and ethics into question.
This recommendation is not consistent with the policies and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 3
Adoption of Grand Lodge Strategic Plan
Over the years Grand Lodge has developed various “long range” plans,
some of which have had more value in the short term in shaping the direction of
Grand Lodge than others. In 1988 Most Worshipful Stanley L. Channon
appointed a committee to develop a plan, and a report was made to the Annual
Communication in 1991 on the results of their labors. Again, in 1998 Most
Worshipful Melville H. Nahin, created a “Long Range Planning Committee” to
study the need for a multi-year plan, and in 1999 and 2000 a report was made to
Grand Lodge by the Grand Lodge Research and Planning Committee – a
committee which I had the privilege to serve as chairman at one time. Grand
Lodge has been using the same Strategic Plan for several years now. Last year
efforts were begun to update the plan. This year I made the updating of the
Grand Lodge Strategic Plan a top priority, and having taken into consideration
previous efforts, with the assistance of the Executive Committee, developed a
comprehensive plan. I believe this version to be much more comprehensive
than previous ones, and a plan that can shape and guide our Grand Lodge for
years to come if we pay attention to it.
I am accordingly laying this plan before Grand Lodge for approval –
neither as a matter of law, nor as an amendment to our code – but as an agreedupon statement of what we expect to achieve over the next several years. A
copy was made available at the Wardens’ Retreats this past spring, and has been
generally available to all our leadership on the Administrative Website since
then. It consists of a Definition of Freemasonry, a Mission Statement, a Vision
Statement, and, most importantly, strategic initiatives to carry out this mission
and this vision. As with all such plans, it must evolve with time, and adapt to
new and changing circumstances. However the basic framework is a sound one
and I strongly urge each succeeding Grand Master to report to Grand Lodge in
his message at the Annual Communication each year the progress we have made
in achieving these initiatives. When the Grand Master determines there needs to
be a change in these strategic initiatives, it will also be appropriate that the
Grand Master report those changes to you in his message to the craft.
While there is no specific need to adopt the Grand Lodge Strategic
Plan, I believe that it is valuable to do so – with the clear understanding that it is
a dynamic document, and that each Grand Master can and should adapt it to

168

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

changing circumstances. I therefore recommend that the Grand Lodge Strategic
Plan as distributed to the Craft this past year be adopted as the strategic plan
until changed by subsequent Grand Masters.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master's Recommendation No. 2005-03 advocates the adoption
of the Grand Lodge Strategic Plan which consists of a definition of
Freemasonry, a Mission Statement, a Vision Statement, and strategic initiatives
to carry out this mission and this vision.
Over the years, Grand Lodge has developed various “long range” plans
which have helped in the shaping of the direction of the Grand Lodge. Most
Worshipful Melville H. Nahin created a "Long Range Planning Committee" to
study the need for a multi-year plan, and in 1999 and 2000 a report was made to
Grand Lodge by the Grand Lodge Research and Planning Committee. Last
year, efforts were made to update the plan and this year the updating of the
Grand Lodge Strategic Plan became a top priority. This plan, which was
distributed to the Craft this past year, is more comprehensive than previous
ones. As with all plans, it would evolve with time, and adapt to new and
changing circumstances.
Strategic planning charts the overall direction for an organization and
the strategic initiatives it will take to achieve its goals. Goals provide direction.
They tell you what success will look like and help you focus your attention in
order to achieve it.
The adoption of Grand Master's Recommendation No. 2005-03 will
provide a strategic plan that can shape and guide our Grand Lodge for years to
come.
This Recommendation is consistent with policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee finds that there is no financial impact to the
Grand Lodge budget or per capita.

Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 4
Revised Officer Qualification
As I have visited lodges around the state the last few years a common
concern raised by Masters was the difficulty in finding men willing to go
through the line and become Master of the lodge. The reasons for the concern
were always the same, it is difficult to find a man who is both good at ritual and
is a good leader. This challenge has resulted over the years in changes to our
ritual to make it easier to qualify. The primary change has been the removal of
material from the lectures and the placing of it in the monitor. We have all
heard the stories of the Master who could not do the lectures, but was allowed to
serve so that the lodge would have a Master. Unfortunately, these stories appear
to be true. Inspectors have been told in no uncertain terms that a lodge must
have qualified officers if they wish to continue to have the privilege to be a
Masonic Lodge. I have suspended the Charter of two lodges this year because
they could not produce qualified officers.
I do not support further reductions in the lectures of our degrees. In
fact, I have had our Deputy Grand Master look into the success of other Grand
Lodges in supplementing lectures with multi media presentations on information
founded in the monitor. I believe that we need to look at alternative ways to
improve the Masonic Education that we use to make a man a Mason and to help
him continue his lifelong quest for self-improvement.
In looking at the role of the Master and Wardens, it is clear to me that
the essence of making a man a Mason is the ceremonies of the three degrees
including the obligation. The delivery of the lecture, while a vital part of the
ceremony, is Masonic Education. It needs to be done well for it to have the
desired impact. I believe that if we separate the duty to perform the lecture in
each of the degrees from the requirements of the Master and Warden, it will not
only allow more qualified leaders to become Master, but it will also improve the
quality of the lectures. The best ritualists will be able to perfect their delivery of
the lectures the way they are supposed to be done. This proposal will not
prohibit a Master or Warden from qualifying to do the lectures; it will give them
and their lodge an option to have a more qualified brother to do the work in
their lodge.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Sections 25070 thru 25090 of the Code would be amended to read
as marked
§25070. QUALIFICATIONS OF MASTER.
Before a Master-elect can be installed there shall be presented to the Installing
Officer the Certificate of the Grand Lecturer, Assistant Grand Lecturer of the
division in which his Lodge is situated, or the Inspector of the district in which
his Lodge is situated, certifying that:

170

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
A. He has personally examined the Master-elect and that he is qualified to
give the work in all three degrees, and that the Master-elect is either
qualified to give the and lectures in the three degrees or has obtained
the assistance of a qualified Brother to assist him for the entire Masonic
year in giving the lectures of the three degrees;
B. He has received a Certificate of Proficiency in the Candidate's Lecture
of the Master Mason Degree; and
C. He is proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the
government of a Lodge.

§25080. QUALIFICATIONS OF SENIOR WARDEN.
Before a Senior Warden-elect can be installed there shall be presented to the
Installing Officer the Certificate of the Grand Lecturer, the Assistant Grand
Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is situated, or the Inspector the
district in which his Lodge is situated, certifying that:
A. He has personally examined the Senior Warden-elect, and that he is
qualified to give the work of the Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft
Degrees, and that the Senior Warden-elect is either qualified to give the
and lectures in the Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft Degrees or has
obtained the assistance of a qualified Brother to assist him for the entire
Masonic year in giving the lectures of the Entered Apprentice and
Fellow Craft;
B. He has received a Certificate of Proficiency in the Candidate's Lecture
of the Master Mason Degree; and
C. He is proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the
government of a Lodge.
§25090. QUALIFICATIONS OF JUNIOR WARDEN.
Before a Junior Warden-elect can be installed there shall be presented to the
Installing Officer the Certificate of the Grand Lecturer, the Assistant Grand
Lecturer of the division in which his Lodge is situated, or the Inspector of the
district in which his Lodge is situated, certifying that:
A. He has personally examined such Junior Warden-elect, and that he is
qualified to give the work of the Entered Apprentice, and that the
Junior Warden-elect is either qualified to give the and lecture in the
Entered Apprentice or has obtained the assistance of a qualified
Brother to assist him for the entire Masonic year in giving the lectures
of the Entered Apprentice;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

171

B. He has received a Certificate of Proficiency in the Candidate's Lecture
of the Master Mason Degree; and
C. He is proficient in those portions of this Code that relate to the
government of a Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation 2005-04 seeks to ease the
burden of the Master, Senior and Jr. Wardens by giving them the option to give
the lecture of their respective degree or have a more qualified brother selected to
do the lectures in the three degrees. The work of the Master, Senior and Jr.
Wardens is not so great as to keep them from learning their respective lectures
and giving them when needed.
In sections 25070, 25080 and 25090 of the California Masonic Code, it
states in part that the Master, Senior and Jr. Wardens be proficient in the
lectures of their respective degrees and that they have received a certificate from
the Inspector attesting to the fact that they are proficient. If they are proficient in
the lectures, they should be able to give the lectures when needed.
A number of unanswered question arise from this Recommendation. If
a “more qualified brother” was asked to do the lecture(s), would he have to be
certified by the Inspector to do so? If the “more qualified brother” were to be
certificated, would the Grand Secretary’s office be required to keep a record of
this and would the certification be entered in those brothers’ Grand Lodge
Masonic history? What if no “qualified brothers” were to be found? These
questions must be answered, and appropriate provision made therefor in our
Masonic Code, before this Recommendation could be appropriately enacted.
This recommendation is not consistent with the Policy and General
Purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The Finance Committee finds that there is no financial impact to the
Grand Lodge budget or per capita.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
This Recommendation seeks to separate the duty to perform the lecture
in each of the degrees from the requirements of the Master and Wardens as an
option in an effort to not only allow more qualified leaders to become Masters,
but to improve the quality of the lectures.

172

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Unfortunately, Grand Master Doan is correct in noting the difficulty in
finding men who are both good at ritual and leadership. Often it is also as
difficult to find such men who are willing to commit to being a leader of a
Lodge.
However, is it not the task of a Lodge to make good men better? The
process of doing this task includes progression through each degree and
advancement to being an effective leader. Seldom do we get a man into
Masonry already proficient in both leadership and ritual. They must work at
being thus proficient.
The Ritual Committee believes that in order for the Master and
Wardens to effectively recognize the quality of degree work, including lectures,
they must themselves experience the process of performing that work and
lecture in each degree TO THE BEST OF THEIR ABILITY. The Master of the
Lodge is responsible for the quality of overall work for all degrees and therefore
must know all the work.
Qualified guest lecturers approved by the Master of the Lodge often
currently give the lectures of the First and Third degrees. The Master’s work in
the second section of the Second Degree does not lend itself to separation of the
lecture portion of the work from the overall work. While the Master remains in
his station and the guest lecturer is placed next to the Master for the First and
Third degrees, the guest lecturer must replace the Master for the entire second
section of the Second Degree including inviting the candidate to a seat among
the brethren.
The Ritual of California is not separate from Masonic Education. It is
in fact the initial Masonic Education that is received by the candidate for each
degree. For these reasons the Ritual Committee submits that the proposed
change is not appropriate as an option to the current qualification requirements
for the Master and Wardens of this jurisdiction.
Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 5
Optional Grand Lodge Officers
There has been a great deal of concern about the expenses of Grand
Lodge and continued pressure to raise the per capita each year to meet the
increased operating costs and to make up for the declining membership. Grand
Lodge has made significant cuts in its expense where it could without reducing
the quality of the services provided to the craft. Until our membership numbers
stabilize, projected to be in another 8 years, we will need to continue to look for
ways to reduce our costs.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

173

One area that is not available to a Grand Master is the reduction in the
number of Grand Lodge Officers. CMC Sections 230 and 520 require all but
the Assistant Grand Organist and the Assistant Grand Tiler to appointed and
installed. I propose to give the Grand Master the option to appoint four other
Grand Lodge officers, the Grand Standard Bearer, Grand Sword Bearer, Grand
Bible Bearer, and Grand Pursuivant. While each of these officers has an
important role in our Grand Lodge Ceremonies, their presence is not crucial and
can be eliminated if the financial needs of the Grand Lodge as determined by
the Grand Master require them to be temporally left vacant. As the financial
condition of Grand Lodge changes the Grand Master can choose to appoint or
not appoint these officers.
This recommendation makes it the sole discretion of the Grand Master
to appoint or not appoint these officers.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Section 230 of the Code would be amended to read as marked:
§230.

MEMBERS AND MANNER OF ADDRESS.

Grand Lodge shall be composed of a Grand Master (whose manner of address
shall be Most Worshipful), a Deputy Grand Master, a Senior Grand Warden,
and a Junior Grand Warden (whose manner of address shall be Right
Worshipful), a Grand Treasurer, a Grand Secretary and a Grand Lecturer
(whose manner of address shall be Very Worshipful), a Grand Chaplain (whose
manner of address shall be Very Reverend), the Assistant Grand Lecturers, a
Grand Orator, an Assistant Grand Secretary, a Grand Marshal, a Grand Standard
Bearer, a Grand Sword Bearer, a Grand Bible Bearer, a Senior Grand Deacon, a
Junior Grand Deacon, a Senior Grand Steward, a Junior Grand Steward, a
Grand Pursuivant, a Grand Organist, a Grand Tiler, and, at the discretion of the
Grand Master, a Grand Standard Bearer, a Grand Sword Bearer, a Grand Bible
Bearer, a Grand Pursuivant, an Assistant Grand Organist and an Assistant Grand
Tiler (whose manner of address shall be Worshipful), together with all Past
Grand Officers and Past Masters of this Jurisdiction, and the Master and
Wardens of the several chartered and duly constituted Lodges (except Research
Lodges) or their representatives duly elected. (10/97)
A Past Grand Lodge officer is one of the elective Grand Lodge officers who has
been regularly elected and installed and has served his term in Grand Lodge.
(10/96)

174

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 2005-05 seeks to amend
Section 230 of the California Masonic Code to allow the Grand Master the sole
discretion of appointing or not appointing the Grand Standard Bearer, Grand
Sword Bearer, Grand Bible Bearer, and Grand Pursuivant. He feels this
recommendation is a cost-cutting device which will relieve some of the pressure
of raising per capita. He states that Grand Lodge has made significant cuts in its
operating expenses and if it makes more the quality of service to Grand Lodge
will be reduced.
These Grand Offices have existed for many years in the long history of
the Grand Lodge of California. There would be great inconsistency if one
Grand Master chooses to appoint some of these Grand Officers one year and the
next Grand Master chooses to fill a different set of offices, or none at all, the
following year. The ritual of opening Grand Lodge would be affected every
year according to the number of Grand Lodge Officers selected by the Grand
Master, thereby promoting confusion, and possibly discord.
This Recommendation is not consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The recommendation makes it the sole discretion of the Grand Master
to appoint or not appoint these officers based on the financial condition of the
Grand Lodge. The savings for the appointed Grand Lodge officers will be
approximately $22,400. This includes $3,300 for Annual Communication
(current and new officers), $500 for the annual orientation, $600 for the annual
weekend event and $1,200 for the annual officer travel. This equals $5,600 per
officer.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
This Recommendation seeks to make optional the appointment of the
Grand Standard Bearer, Grand Sword Bearer, Grand Bible Bearer and Grand
Pursuivant in order to meet the future Grand Lodge financial conditions.
The four Grand Lodge officers affected are “Ritually involved only in
the opening of Grand Lodge and in our Cornerstone Ceremony.” To eliminate
them in any given year would mean that the opening ritual of Grand Lodge and
that ceremony would of necessity need to be modified.
The Ritual Committee is prepared to eliminate the duties of these
officers and make the necessary changes in the opening ritual of Grand Lodge
and the Book of Ceremonies to accommodate the recommendation. Our

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

175

concern is that these changes or any supplements related to them will need to be
reprinted and maintained over a long period of time.
The Grand Master states that our membership numbers are not
projected to stabilize for another eight years. Realistically, it would probably be
several more years before these officers would reappear on the floor of Grand
Lodge. However, the Grand Master would have the option to fill any or all of
these positions in any given year. This would mean that the Grand Lodge
opening Ritual and the Cornerstone Ceremony could change back and forth
from year to year, creating a degree of confusion and inconsistent ritual.
Recommendation CARRIED-OVER.
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 6
Change the Role and Selection of Grand Lecturer
The Grand Lecturer position was created as a full time job to travel
around California and teach the approved ritual to each of the lodges in the
state. The position was made an elected office to give the craft the ability to
control the ritual and how it was taught. Over the years we have added
Officer’s Coaches, District Inspectors and Assistant Grand Lecturers to assist
the Grand Lecturer in the discharge of his duties. Ultimately, it was no longer
necessary for the Grand Lecturer to be a full time job.
Over the last several years we have taken our ritual from the memory
of the Grand Lecturer, Assistant Grand Lecturer, Inspectors and Officers
Coaches and created a cipher ritual and a floor work manual. We have taken the
control of the ritual from one person and placed into the hands of the craft.
Section 26310 of the Code states that Grand Lodge alone has power to authorize
any changes in the ritual. Any change in the ritual requires approval by the
same vote as provided for any proposed amendment to its Ordinances. Section
6170 states that the Committee on Ritual shall be the custodian of the esoteric
work. Clearly, the Grand Lecturer is no longer the sole authority on our ritual
and there are adequate safeguards in our law to make sure that our ritual is not
change without the permission of the craft.
As I look at the role of the Grand Lecturer, I see the opportunity to
combine that position with one of the Assistant Grand Lecturers as a means to
save money and improve the organization structure of Grand Lodge.
The Grand Lecturer’s role is not so demanding that it requires a
separate person to perform those duties. The addition of the Assistant Grand
Lecturer positions and the Inspectors provide the eyes and ears for the Grand
Lecturer and the cipher ritual and floor work guide assure that the ritual will be
uniformly taught. I reached this conclusion after a careful study of the Grand
Lecturers duties and observing the Ritual Committee in action. By looking at

176

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

the duties of the Grand Lecturer as outlined in Section 2050 of the Code it will
help you understand why I believe the Grand Lecturer position can be combined
with one of the Assistant Grand Lecturers.
Section 2050 of the Code states that it shall be the duty of the Grand
Lecturer to:
A. Supervise and direct the Assistant Grand Lecturers and Inspectors in
the performance of their duties, with respect to the Ritual, and to
require conformity with the adopted ritual and work;
Section A is accomplished by use of the Ritual Committee and schools of
Instruction. This would not add additional burden to the new Grand Lecturer
position.
B. Examine the Assistant Grand Lecturers and issue Certificates of
Proficiency to such as are duly qualified, on receiving notice of their
appointment by the Grand Master;
Section B is done upon appointment of a new Assistant Grand Lecturer. Since
the Grand Lecturer is selected from one of the Assistant Grand Lecturers he will
have already been found qualified, therefore it would not create a conflict. Nor
would this add a burden on to the new Grand Lecturer position.
C. See that the Inspectors examine all officers who are required to
produce certificates of proficiency at their installation in accordance
with these Ordinances;
Section C is accomplished by use of the Ritual Committee. This would not add
additional burden to the new Grand Lecturer position.
D. Render to Grand Lodge at each Annual Communication a full and
detailed account of his services during the year, and to set forth, with
respect to ritual, the condition of the Lodges;
Section D is done once a year and has become the report of the Ritual
Committee, of which the Grand Lecturer is the chairman. This would not add
additional burden to the new Grand Lecturer position.
E. Cause to be held Lodges of instruction for the Inspectors and at his
convenience to attend schools of instruction held by Assistant Grand
Lecturers or Inspectors; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

177

Section E is done by use of the Ritual Committee and his attendance at the
schools is optional. This would not add additional burden to the new Grand
Lecturer position.
F. Call and preside at meetings of the Committee on Ritual whenever in
his judgment it is necessary or advisable for the committee to convene.
Section F describes the duties of the Chairman of the Committee. This would
not add additional burden to the new Grand Lecturer position.
As you can see, the required duties of the Grand Lecturer do not place
a significant burden on the position. Therefore, I see no problem in combining
the Grand Lecturer’s position with that of an Assistant Grand Lecturer.
This recommendation would require that the Grand Master select one
of the Assistant Grand Lecturers to be the Grand Lecturer. The title of the
Grand Lecturer would remain the same, except that he would also be
acknowledge as being responsible for the supervision of one of the Divisions.
This will reduce costs and improve the organization structure of Grand Lodge.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code. Sections 510 and 520 of the Code would be amended to read as
marked:
§510.

ELECTED.

The Grand Master, the Deputy Grand Master, the Grand Wardens, the Past
Grand Masters, and three members of Grand Lodge appointed annually by the
Grand Master to serve for a single term only concurrently with his term and who
are not past or present elected Grand Officers, shall constitute a committee to
recommend to Grand Lodge a member for election as Junior Grand Warden.
The Grand Master shall call and preside at meetings of the committee, but shall
not vote except in cases of tie votes. A majority of the whole committee shall
constitute a quorum. A majority vote of those present and eligible to vote shall
be necessary to select the recommendee.
The recommendee shall be a member of a Lodge in the North and of a Lodge in
the South, alternately. Within 30 days following the selection by the
Recommending Committee, but in no case later than 60 days prior to the Annual
Communication of Grand Lodge, the name of the recommendee and his
biographical sketch shall be sent to:
A.

Each Inspector, to be communicated promptly by the Inspectors to the
Masters and Wardens of each Inspectoral District and through them to
other members of Grand Lodge in their respective Lodges; and

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

178
B.

To such other members of Grand Lodge as the Grand Master
determines.

All dissemination of this information thereafter shall be oral and not by any
means of reproduction.
The Executive Committee shall recommend members of Grand Lodge for
election to the offices of Grand Treasurer and Grand Secretary and Grand
Lecturer, to serve for the ensuing Masonic year. The Grand Master shall
communicate the names of the recommendees to all Lodges no later than 60
days prior to the next Annual Communication of Grand Lodge.
Should any recommendee be unable or unwilling to serve in the office for which
he was recommended, the Executive Committee shall make a further
recommendation.
The Grand Master, Deputy Grand Master, Grand Wardens, Grand Treasurer,
and Grand Secretary, and Grand Lecturer shall be elected by written ballot as
the final item of business before the installation of said officers at each Annual
Communication. A majority of the votes cast shall be necessary for an election.
Grand Lodge, by unanimous consent, may permit any designated officer to cast
the ballot for Grand Lodge.
The elective officers shall be installed before the close of the Annual
Communication. This installation may be public or private at the option of the
incoming Grand Master.
If an elective officer is unable to be present, due to circumstances beyond his
control, he may be installed by the Grand Master at an appropriate place, with
such ceremony as deemed proper by the Grand Master, as soon as practicable
not to exceed 60 days. If the elective officer is not installed within 60 days, the
office shall be declared vacant and the Grand Master may appoint any member
of Grand Lodge to fill the vacant elective office temporarily or for the remainder
of the Grand Lodge year.
§520.

APPOINTED.

The Grand Secretary may, with the approval of the Grand Master, appoint an
Assistant Grand Secretary.
All other appointive Grand Lodge Officers including the Grand Lecturer shall
be appointed by the Grand Master, after his election, at each Annual
Communication. The Grand Lecturer shall be appointed from one of the
Assistant Grand Lecturers.
All appointive Grand Lodge Officers shall be installed before the close of each
Annual Communication. They shall hold their respective offices during the will
and pleasure of the Grand Master. (10/97)

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Recommendation No. 2005-06 seeks to permit the sitting Grand Master
to select one of the Assistant Grand Lecturers to discharge the duties of the
Grand Lecturer. The title of Grand Lecturer would remain the same, except he
would also be responsible for the supervision of one of the Divisions. This
Recommendation is made as a cost saving measure; however, the amount of
savings that would result from its passage is unclear.
The Grand Lecturer has final authority in the interpretation and
implementation of our ritual. The position was made an elected office to give
the craft the right to choose the person in whom they had the utmost trust and
confidence to carry out these important tasks. Passage of this Recommendation
would take that choice away from the members of Grand Lodge, and instead
place it in the hands of the Grand Master, thus allowing a different Grand
Lecturer to be chosen every year.
Historically, Grand Lecturers have held their position over a number of
years, thereby ensuring continuity in the teaching of the ritual. Over the years,
Grand Lodge has added Officers’ Coaches, District Inspectors and Assistant
Grand Lecturers to assist the Grand Lecturer in the discharge of his duties. It is
important that these men know and understand the manner in which the Grand
Lecturer requires the ritual to be taught, become comfortable with his methods,
and properly carry out his instructions. Changing the Grand Lecturer every year
could easily lead to confusion, not only among these men, but among all
members of our craft. Continuity of leadership is the cornerstone of uniformity
in the teaching of our ritual. Under this Recommendation, that continuity would
be lost.
This Recommendation is not consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.

Report of the Committee on Finance:
The elimination of the role and selection of the Grand Lecturer and the
assignment of his duties to one of the Assistant Grand Lecturers would result in
a savings to the Grand Lodge of approximately $4,000 to $6,000 per year.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
This Recommendation seeks to change the selection of the Grand Lecturer
from being a position that is elected by you, the Brethren of the Grand Lodge, to
being a personal appointment by each successive Grand Master.

180

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The position of Grand Lecturer was initially an appointed position with the
first Grand Lecturer being appointed in November of 1850 and he remained in that
position for many, many years. It is interesting to note that after thirty years of
consistent ritual work, the Grand Lodge experienced a succession of several Grand
Lecturers over a period of nine years, and during that nine year period the work
deteriorated to the point that a ritual committee consisting of the Grand Lecturer
and three Brethren was established in 1897 as “Custodians and censors of the
work, whose decision on the ritual should be final.” Three years later the position
of Grand Lecturer was made an ELECTIVE position in order to oversee the work
of the Ritual Committee and to maintain ritual consistency.
Each year the Executive Committee, which includes the Grand Master,
reviews the work of the Grand Treasurer, Grand Secretary and Grand Lecturer and
makes their recommendation to you, the voting Brethren of the Grand Lodge. In
this manner, the Grand Master provides the names of the Brothers he believes is
providing the best service to the Craft in each of the positions so you can make the
final decision.
Unfortunately, this legislation is a second attempt to return to the past by
seeking to remove the Grand Lecturer from being an elected to being an appointed
position.
The first attempt was in 1999 with Grand Master Nahin’s
Recommendation No. 199-04, which sought to make the offices of Grand
Treasurer, Grand Secretary and Grand Lecturer appointive rather than elective
positions. At that time, Committee on Policy and General Purposes wrote, in part,
“The Grand Lecturer . . . is the custodian of our ritual (and) . . . there is a need for
(continuity) which transcends any given Masonic year.” The Committee found
that the effort was “not consistent with our current policy and general purposes.”
This first attempt was NOT ADOPTED by you, the members of the Grand Lodge.
Again, this matter comes before you, this time focusing only upon the
Grand Lecturer, with the caveat that (1) the Grand Lecturer could be replaced by
the cipher ritual and the supplement to the cipher, and (2) removal of the position
of Grand Lecturer is a cost savings measure as the duties could be easily added to
the work presently performed by an Assistant Grand Lecturer.
First, although much of the ritual is contained within the cipher and
supplement, there is an equivalent amount that is not covered, such as appropriate
rod and floor work, proper word pronunciation, and overall presentation of the
work. If left to individual Brothers to determine the appropriate manner to present
the work, the conferral of degrees would soon return to its previous form, where
conferral details were inconsistent between lodges. It was this form of
inconsistency that cuased, you the members of Grand Lodge, to seek out a single
person to lead a Ritual Committee to frame our work, to ensure its consistency and
to remove the position of Grand Lecturer from the hands of the every-changing
leadership.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

181

The relationship of the Grand Lecturer to the Grand Master of the Grand
Lodge is the same as it is for the Officers’ Coach to the Master of the Lodge. The
Officers’ Coach is the ritual supervisor of the Lodge and the Grand Lecturer is the
ritual supervisor of the Grand Lodge. As the Master of a Lodge is not allowed to
select his own Officers’ Coach, the Grand Master should not be allowed to select
his own Grand Lecturer. The need for independence and consistency is of
paramount importance to both the local Lodge and to the Grand Lodge.
Secondly, the alleged cost savings would be negated in the increased work
by the person assigned to accept the tasks currently being performed by the Grand
Lecturer. Currently, an Assistant Grand Lecturer’s position is a very, very busy
position as is the position of Grand Lecturer. To combine these two positions is
preposterous and pleaces an unacceptable burden upon one person.
In summary, the Grand Master is the custodian of all things administrative
and the Grand Lecturer is the custodian of all things Ritual and only you can elect
those positions. You are the Grand Lodge and only you have the authority to
make changes to either Administration or Ritual.
If you adopt this
recommendation, you the Brethren of Grand Lodge, give up your right to make
any future modifications relative to ritual. In fact, changes to the Ritual could be
made by each successive Grand Master, through the influence he has upon his
appointee, without ever receiving your input or approval. Do you really wish to
return to the failures of the past?
Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 7
Three Year Evaluation of Chance to Advance
Clearly during the last several years, there has been considerable
interest generated within the Craft in California concerning One-Day
Conferrals. It has appeared as a piece of legislation at our Annual
Communication the last two years and while a majority of those attending have
supported with the legislation, it did not obtain the necessary votes to pass.
In the past 5 years, the Grand Lodge of California has initiated 6,731
candidates. Of the candidates initiated between July 1, 1999 and June 30, 2004,
2,219 have failed to be raised to the Sublime Degree of Master Mason. That
means that 33% of those who became Entered Apprentice Masons in these five
years did not become Master Masons. We lost a third of our candidates.
The failure of these candidates to progress beyond Entered Apprentice
or Fellow Craft has resulted in a tremendous loss to the Fraternity in California.
These advancements may not be solely the fault of the candidates as some of
the investigating committees and coaches are often ill-prepared for the
responsibilities they are given. This can result in candidates who do not know

182

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

what is expected of them until they have already been initiated. Also, when
Masonic principles and symbolism are not adequately explained to the
candidates; the result has too often been candidates who elect to drop out rather
than continue a process that appears to be both discouraging and difficult to
them. The Lodge may have truly failed the candidate rather than the candidate
failing Masonry.
It is my belief that our California Masonic Code should be amended to
allow a three year pilot program to allow the Grand Master to permit lodges to
confer the Fellow Craft and Master Mason Degrees on those candidates who
have not progressed in a constituent California lodge for at least one year. Such
conferral could be done in one day, or more than one day – but the presently
required proficiency between the degrees would be waived for this purpose. If
this recommendation were adopted, the California Masonic Code would be
amended to add Section 26285 as follows:
§26285. Conferral of Degrees
Notwithstanding Section 26280 of the California Masonic Code, the Grand
Master shall have the authority to provide for the conferral of the Fellow Craft
and Master Mason degrees on candidates in one day, without satisfying the
proficiency and Masonic education requirements of the Code, provided:
A. Candidates have previously received the Entered Apprentice Degree or
Fellow Craft Degree in a constituent Lodge at a date more than one
year previous to the conferral allowed by this section;
B. Candidates must request such conferral, and be approved by his Lodge;
C. Candidates shall be required to attend a Masonic Education program on
the day of the conferral for each degree. All candidates shall attend the
Masonic Education element for the Entered Apprentice and Fellow
Craft Degrees. This Masonic Education program for each degree shall
be provided by the Grand Lodge and shall consist of an explanation of
the history, philosophy and symbolism of each degree together with an
explanation of the meanings of the obligations;
D. An examination to determine the candidates’ knowledge shall be
administered by the Grand Lodge at the conclusion of the Masonic
Education for each degree;
E. Each Lodge participating in the conferral of the degrees shall provide
one knowledgeable mentor at the conferral site for each candidate from
that Lodge to assist in explaining the process and purposes of the
degrees; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

183

F. This section shall be automatically repealed three years from the date
of its adoption, except as follows. At the end of this three year
program, the program of conferral provided herein shall be evaluated.
The Grand Master shall cause a report on the results of the pilot
program to be completed and presented to Grand Lodge. If the
program is to be continued beyond this three year period, it shall
require a resolution or Grand Master’s recommendation presented to
Grand Lodge and approved in the manner required for amendments to
these Ordinances.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Recommendation points to an issue that is the life blood of
Freemasonry. That is to initiate, pass and raise a good man to the Sublime
Degree of Master Mason. The numbers indicate, over a five year period, that
thirty-three percent of all candidates did not proceed past Entered Apprentice or
Fellowcraft to become Master Masons. There are many reasons why this has
occurred; there is no single solution.
This Recommendation provides for a One-Day class, but in a different
format from that proposed two years ago, and ultimately defeated last year.
There are those that would argue the merits of such a change, but if change is
inevitable, the path of least resistance can be helpful. This Recommendation
provides the path of least resistance. Proposed new California Masonic Code
section 26285, permitting those who have not advanced after one year to take
their remaining degrees in one day, is not mandatory; a lodge would have to
give its consent. Thus, Recommendation 2005-07, if enacted, would not
necessarily have impact on the customs used by any particular lodge. In other
words, if a lodge does not want to participate, it does not have to.
This Recommendation has been proposed in such a way that it will not
have a long term effect. It is proposed as a pilot program with a report on the
results provided at the end of the third year. If the program is successful, it can
be made permanent, but only if it is proposed again, either in a Grand Master’s
Recommendation, or in a proposed Resolution. This legislation is a viable tool
for Grand Lodge and its members.
This Recommendation is consistent with the Policy and General
Purposes of this Grand Lodge.

184

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Finance:
This program is not likely to have a negative financial impact on the
Grand Lodge budget. There are several thousand Entered Apprentice and
Fellowcraft of our jurisdiction who would be eligible to participate in this
program and this would result in a positive financial impact.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
This Recommendation seeks to allow the optional conferral of the
Fellowcraft and Master Mason Degrees on those Entered Apprentices and
Fellowcrafts who, after one year, have not progressed. Each Entered Apprentice
and Fellowcraft will participate in a Maosnic Education Program before the
conferral of each degree.
It is the opinion of your Ritual Committee that Grand Master Doan’s
Recommendation No. 2005-07 contains sufficient instruction to adequately
modify the California Masonic Code to allow for the conferral of the
Fellowcraft and Master Mason Degrees in one day.
This is a three year pilot program which will be evaluated at the end of
its term by the Grand Master.
Your Ritual Committee is ready to ensure that whatever final decision
is made by this Grand Lodge, the instructions of our Masonic principles will be
maintained at their highest level.
Please remember that the conferral of our degrees make a man a
member. What follows thereafter make the member a Mason.
Recommendation CARRIED-OVER.
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 8
Code Review Committee
Over time, inconsistencies and mistakes can accumulate in our
California Masonic Code when piece meal changes are made to it at each
Annual Communication. No comprehensive review of the Code to correct such
matters has been undertaken since the present blue code was adopted in 1991. It
is my recommendation that the incoming Grand Master appoint a special
committee to review the current Code and make recommendations for
appropriate corrections and updates. The work of the committee shall be
presented to the craft for its consideration at the next annual communication.

185

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Doan’s Recommendation No. 2005-08 seeks to have the
incoming Grand Master appoint a special committee to review the current
California Masonic Code and make recommendations for appropriate
corrections and updates. We are presently using the third edition of the
California Masonic Code. The last revision was approved in 1991.
At the 1988 Annual Communication, Grand Lodge adopted Most
Worshipful Leo B. Mark’s Recommendation to appoint a special committee
known as the “Code Revision Committee” to prepare a revised CMC written in
simple, understandable language, free from ambiguities or inconsistencies.
With the counsel of the Committee on Jurisprudence, they accomplished this
task three years later. The CMC does not provide a specific time or interval for
review or revision, and it is appropriate that we review our law from time to
time to ensure that it may be easily understood and applied by the Craft.
This recommendation is consistent with the Policy and General
Purposes of this Grand Lodge.

If this program is adopted it would require an addendum to the Grand
Lodge budget for this special committee. The expenses would be approximately
$10,000, including travel to meetings, conference calls, office supplies, Annual
Communication Report and similar expenditures.
Recommendation ADOPTED.

CARRY-OVER FROM 2004 ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
Grand Master Kirkpatrick’s Recommendation No. 2004-01
Grand Master’s Lodge
Each year a number of our lodges are faced with a decision to either
consolidate or surrender their charter. There are many reasons leading up to this
decision including but not limited to lack of officers, lack of member interest,
increased financial obligations and many more. Consolidation has been the
option chosen most often because it was the lesser of two evils but many times
consolidation was not the best choice. Our research estimates that during the
next 15 years, 160 more lodges will close.
The reason surrender of the charter was not chosen was due to the
negative effect on the lodge members. When a charter is surrendered all
members are issued a dimit which is good for one year and that member must
find another lodge within that one year or he loses all benefits of Masonry
including Masonic assistance from the Masonic Homes for both he and his wife.
Generally, the majority of these members are elderly and unwilling or unable,
due to health to actively seek out a new lodge.
In order to correct this gross inequity, I am proposing that Grand
Lodge issue a charter to a lodge called the “Grand Master’s Lodge.” The
officers of Grand Lodge would serve as officers in the Grand Master’s Lodge ex
officio. When a charter is surrendered or revoked all members of that lodge
would automatically be transferred to membership in the Grand Master’s Lodge
with all rights and benefits as they had in their old lodge. The members of the
Grand Master’s Lodge would pay dues and receive dimits when requested.
The Grand Master’s Lodge will not perform degrees or hold meetings.
It simply acts as a holding area to protect the eligibility of a Mason, his wife,
widow or orphans for assistance from the Masonic Homes of California should
the need arise.
I urge a yes vote on this proposal to help our fellow Masons who
otherwise might become homeless when his lodge surrenders its charter.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code.
Section 230 of the California Masonic Code will be amended to read
as follows:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

187

§230. MEMBERS AND MANNER OF ADDRESS.
Grand Lodge shall be composed of a Grand Master (whose manner of
address shall be Most Worshipful), a Deputy Grand Master, a Senior
Grand Warden, and a Junior Grand Warden (whose manner of address
shall be Right Worshipful), a Grand Treasurer, a Grand Secretary and a
Grand Lecturer (whose manner of address shall be Very Worshipful), a
Grand Chaplain (whose manner of address shall be Very Reverend),
the Assistant Grand Lecturers, a Grand Orator, an Assistant Grand
Secretary, a Grand Marshal, a Grand Standard Bearer, a Grand Sword
Bearer, a Grand Bible Bearer, a Senior Grand Deacon, a Junior Grand
Deacon, a Senior Grand Steward, a Junior Grand Steward, a Grand
Pursuivant, a Grand Organist, a Grand Tiler, and, at the discretion of
the Grand Master, an Assistant Grand Organist and an Assistant Grand
Tiler (whose manner of address shall be Worshipful), together with all
Past Grand Officers and Past Masters of this Jurisdiction, and the
Master and Wardens of the several chartered and duly constituted
Lodges (except Research Lodges or the Grand Master’s Lodge) or their
representatives duly elected.
A Past Grand Lodge officer is one of the elective Grand Lodge officers
who has been regularly elected and installed and has served his term in
Grand Lodge.
Section 20070 of the California Masonic Code will be repealed, and a
new Section 20085 will be added to read as follows:
§20085. GRAND MASTER’S LODGE.
A special classification of constituent Lodge is hereby established, to
be known as the Grand Master’s Lodge. The Grand Master’s Lodge
shall be subject to all provisions of this Code applicable to constituent
Lodges, except as follows:
A.

There shall be only one Grand Master’s Lodge in this
Jurisdiction, and it shall be chartered with the name “Grand
Master’s Lodge” without a Lodge number;

B.

The officers of the Grand Master’s Lodge shall be the Grand
Master as Master, the Senior Grand Warden as Senior
Warden, the Junior Grand Warden as Junior Warden, the
Grand Treasurer as Treasurer, and the Grand Secretary as
Secretary, each of whom shall serve in this office ex officio;

C.

Membership shall consist only of:

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

1.

As ex-officio members, the Grand Master, the Deputy

Grand Warden, the Grand Treasurer and the Grand

2.

Those Entered Apprentices, Fellow Crafts and
Master Masons of a constituent Lodge at the time
that the Lodge’s charter is surrendered or revoked
who are not at the time a plural or dual member in
another Lodge;

D.

Membership in the Grand Master’s Lodge shall cease for any

E.

F.

The provisions of this Code requiring certain
recommendations, notifications and certificates from an
Inspector dealing with qualifications of officers and
