Master’s Lodge, except as required to comply with the
provisions of this Section;

H.

The provisions of this Code dealing with the qualifications of
the Master, Senior Warden and Junior Warden of a constituent
Lodge shall not apply to the officers of the Grand Master’s
Lodge;

I.

The Grand Master’s Lodge shall not be entitled to
representation in Grand Lodge or any vote therein;

J.

sufficient to offset any cost to Grand Lodge for the operation
of the Grand Master’s Lodge, imposing no burden on the per
capita. These dues shall be sufficient not only to pay the costs
of the operation of the Grand Master’s Lodge, but also the per
capita due Grand Lodge each year; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA
K.

All other actions requiring the consent or vote of the Lodge
shall be effected by the Grand Master acting alone.

Finally, Section 23070 of the California Masonic Code will be
repealed.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Kirkpatrick is proposing that Grand Lodge issue a
charter to a Lodge called “Grand Master’s Lodge”. The purpose of this Lodge
would be to serve as a mechanism whereby members of Lodges which have
their charters suspended or revoked, could continue membership in a recognized
Lodge, thus not jeopardizing their status as “Masons in good standing”, and the
benefits that might be available to them as members.
Research shows that many of our elderly members are unable to seek
membership in a new Lodge when their Lodges close. If they do not affiliate
with another Lodge within one year, they will lose eligibility for Masonic
benefits for both themselves and their family.
While there is precedent for the formation of a special purpose Lodge,
no precedent can be found for the formation of a Lodge as proposed by this
Recommendation. However, to relieve our distressed brethren is a long
established policy of California Freemasonry. The establishment of a procedure
to assure that our members remain Masons if a Lodge loses its charter is
consistent with all we stand for as Masons.
This Recommendation is consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Kirkpatrick’s Recommendation No. 2004-02
Candidate’s Fees to Grand Lodge
At present a Lodge pays Grand Lodge $1.00 for every degree it confers
on its candidates, an amount that has remained unchanged since 1850. However
the cost of adding a new Entered Apprentice Mason to the rolls of Grand Lodge
is far greater than this amount. I recommend that this $1.00 per degree fee paid
by the Lodge be replaced by a new fee to be paid by the candidate along with
his application to the Lodge. The recommended amount of this fee is $30.
Because it will be paid by the candidate, and not by the Lodge directly, the
Lodge will no longer have to pay the $1.00 per degree to Grand Lodge. And
while this amount is not great, the burden on the per capita to make up the

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

190

additional cost caused by the enrolling of a new member is significant. The new
fee, paid by the candidate with his application, will defray much of the expense
that Grand Lodge will incur as he takes his degrees.
I urge you to support this more rational way of passing along the cost
of enrolling a new member to the member himself, rather than to his lodge.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code.
Section 13020 of the California Masonic Code will be amended to read
as follows:
§13020. LODGE CONTRIBUTIONS.
Each Lodge, whether chartered or under dispensation:
A.

B.

Shall pay with its Annual Returns the following contributions:
1.

Every degree it has conferred during the year - $1,

2.

Each Master Mason carried on its rolls as of June 30,

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Kirkpatrick’s recommendation Number 04-02 seeks to

administrative costs of this Grand Lodge have risen substantially. The proposed
increase of the fee to $30 will be part of the fees for application and will be sent

2005 RESOLUTIONS
Resolution No. 05-01
Unmasonic Conduct
The proponents of this Resolution seek to make offenses committed against
Masonic affiliated organizations unmasonic conduct.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 32030 of the California Masonic Code says that
it is not unmasonic conduct to violate the laws of other fraternal or social
organizations, including Masonic Organizations; and
WHEREAS, our obligations are directed to a brother or a Lodge; and
WHEREAS, grievous offenses have been committed against Masonic
affiliated organizations and concordant bodies; and
WHEREAS, this type of offense has not been properly addressed in
the California Masonic Code; and
WHEREAS, Masonic law should be changed to address these types of
occurrences.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 32020 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§32020. UNMASONIC CONDUCT.
The following breaches of faith or moral turpitude constitute unmasonic conduct
sufficient to support the filing of charges against a Mason:
A.

Any violation, before or after initiation, of the criminal laws of the
United States or of any other governmental entity involving moral
turpitude;

B.

The following acts committed against a Brother Mason, or the wife, or
child, or widow of such a Brother:
1.

Use of defamatory, vile, indecent, profane, abusive or
threatening language,

2.

Habitual intemperance and drunkenness,

3.

Personal violence,

4.

Falsehood, treachery and deceit, or

5.

Actions similar to those offenses listed herein;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA
C.

D.

193

The following acts committed against his Lodge or the Master thereof:
1.

Misrepresentation of any fact in an application for degrees,
affiliation or restoration,

2.

Declaration that he does not believe in a Supreme Being,

3.

Attempted resignation from or renunciation of Masonry,

4.

Visiting a clandestine Lodge or vouching for a member of a
clandestine Lodge,

5.

Disclosure of secret or confidential matters to a non-Mason or
by a member of a Lodge to a non-member Mason not
otherwise entitled to the same,

6.

Reducing secret work to writing or use of a cipher ritual
during a tiled meeting,

7.

Refusal to obey Master’s authority properly exercised,

8.

Refusal to sign Lodge By-laws,

9.

Improper use of ballot or objection
advancement without proper cause, or

10.

Actions similar to those offenses listed herein;

to

Masonic purposes., or

194
G.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
Acts or actions that cheat, wrong, defraud, or fail to perform a fiduciary
duty toward any Masonic Organization.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 32030 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§32030. NOT UNMASONIC CONDUCT.
An act does not constitute a Masonic offense that neither violates this Code nor
involves a breach of faith or moral turpitude.
The following do not constitute unmasonic conduct sufficient to support the
filing of charges against a Mason:
A.

A business dispute not involving fraud or one that involves the
innocent inability to pay a just debt as promised;

B.

Political differences and acts in respect thereto;

C.

A possible conflict of interest arising from a transaction between a
Mason in his private capacity and his Lodge or Temple Association
where a full disclosure of the possible conflict has been made prior to
the transaction; or

D.

Actions allegedly violating the laws of other fraternal or social
organizations, including Masonic Organizations, except as provided
elsewhere in this Code.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The purpose of this Resolution is to clarify that portion of the
California Masonic Code which defines the term “unmasonic conduct”
sufficient to support the filing of charges against a Mason. As currently written,
the California Masonic Code proscribes certain conduct committed by a Mason
against his Lodge or its Master, or against Grand Lodge, the Grand Master, or a
Brother Mason. This Resolution would add subsection G to Section 32020 to
include in the definition of unmasonic conduct those acts or actions that cheat,
wrong or defraud any Masonic Organization. In other words, the definition of
unmasonic conduct has been broadened to include proscribed acts committed by
a Mason against any appendant Masonic body.
Section 32020, as currently written, could be interpreted as already
including as unmasonic conduct, proscribed acts committed against an
appendant Masonic body. However, in light of recent discoveries of acts of
fraud and theft committed by a Mason against at least two appendant bodies, it

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

195

would be prudent to make absolutely clear that such conduct will not be
tolerated against any entity with Masonic affiliation. This proposed resolution
makes it abundantly clear that all Masonic bodies are afforded the same
protections under the California Masonic Code as are Brothers, Lodges and
Grand Lodge.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Trial Review:
We agree with the proposed addition to Section 32020 which would
include as unMasonic conduct, “actions that cheat, wrong or defraud any
Masonic organization.”
However, adding “failure to perform a fiduciary duty” to that phrase
could expose a brother to a charge of unMasonic conduct for simple negligence.
In addition, we do not agree with potentially adding the phrase, “except
as provided elsewhere in the code” to Section 32030(D). This addition could
involve a Masonic Lodge in the internal affairs on “laws” of other fraternal or
social organizations. We would not like to see charges of unMasonic conduct
brought against a brother for his failure or refusal to pay “fines” levied against
him at his Rotary Club luncheon or Toastmasters breakfast.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-02
Previous Verdict
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow evidence of prior Masonic trials
in setting penalties in a Masonic trial.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the Manual for Masonic Charges and Trials indicates that
it follows California law; and
WHEREAS, California law has a “Three Strikes” provision therein;
and
WHEREAS, results of Masonic trials of a particular brother should be
reviewed and considered in determining the appropriate and proper penalty to
be given at the conclusion of a subsequent Masonic trial; and

196

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

WHEREAS, the Trial Commissioners, Trial Master and/or Trial
Review Committee does not review any of the data and information of prior
Masonic trials and/or results thereof.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 32350 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§32350. VERDICT.
After all testimony has been received in a trial, when before Trial
Commissioners, the Commissioners shall proceed without delay to deliberate
upon their verdict and sentence. During deliberations, only the Commissioners
may be present.
The guilt or innocence of the accused must be determined by the
Commissioners. Only those Commissioners who have participated in the entire
trial are entitled to participate in its deliberation and findings. The Trial Master,
being only the presiding officer, cannot decide the facts or dismiss the case at
the conclusion of the trial, except in the case of a trial held before a Trial Master
alone, where, following a trial conducted in accordance with the procedure set
forth elsewhere in this Code for trials before a Commission, the Trial Master
alone shall determine the guilt or innocence of the accused and shall decide on
the penalty. In determining the penalty, the Trial Master may consider the
particulars of any previous Masonic trials of the accused which resulted in a
finding of guilt.
After sufficient consultation and deliberation, a vote by ballot for “guilty” or
“not guilty” shall be taken upon each specification, and each vote shall be
recorded. A separate vote shall be taken upon the charge of unmasonic conduct
and be recorded to determine if the facts proven and found constitute a Masonic
offense. A majority vote of the original number of Commissioners elected is
required to find a verdict of guilty. If the Commissioners cannot find the
accused guilty of the charge or specification, they may delete any part which
they do not find against the accused, and return a verdict, or judgment, for that
portion of the specification or charge of which they find the accused guilty.
Should the accused be found guilty of one or more of the specifications, and of
the charge of unmasonic conduct as originally charged, or as modified by their
findings, the Commissioners will proceed to vote upon the sentence. In
deliberating on the sentence, the Commissioners may consider the particulars of
any previous Masonic trials of the accused which resulted in a finding of guilt.
A majority of the original number of Commissioners elected will be required to
determine the sentence. The vote shall be by ballot and the ballots shall have
written on them “aye” or “no.” The first vote will be upon the question - “Shall
the accused be expelled?” If there is not the required majority vote for
expulsion, the question will next be - “Shall the accused be suspended?” If there

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

197

is not the required majority vote for suspension, the last question will be - “Shall
the accused be reprimanded?”
All questions and votes shall be recorded, giving the number of votes, both aye
and no, upon each question.
If the required majority of the Commissioners cannot agree on the matters of
guilt and punishment, the Trial Master must discharge the Commissioners and a
new Trial Commission must be elected by the Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Unlike the criminal law in California, current Masonic law, as set forth
in the California Masonic Code, does not permit a Trial Master or Trial
Commissioners to consider, when determining an appropriate penalty after
making a finding of guilty, the prior Masonic disciplinary history, if any, of a
Brother found to have committed a Masonic offense. This Resolution
specifically would permit consideration, during deliberations in the penalty
phase of a subsequent Masonic trial, of the particulars of any Masonic trial in
which the accused previously had been found guilty. Consideration of prior
discipline would be permitted during the penalty phase only. Prior Masonic
discipline would not be considered in making the determination of whether the
accused has committed another Masonic offense.
This Resolution would more closely align the provisions of the
California Masonic Code with current state criminal law. In virtually every
criminal proceeding where the defendant has been found guilty, the court will
consider prior criminal conduct before issuing a sentence. However, the
California Masonic Code is not a criminal code. It embodies the laws, rules,
and regulations that govern our conduct as Masons, and is more civil, rather
than criminal, in nature. In the civil law, prior conduct of an individual is
generally not taken into account in subsequent proceedings. Each case stands,
and is judged, on its own. That same even-handed approach applies, as it
rightly should, to Masonic adjudication.
This Resolution is not consistent wit the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
Most Worshipful, the Finance Committee has reviewed the proposed
resolution and has determined an estimated additional expense of $2,250.00 to
Grand Lodge per year.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

198

The additional cost is based on 30 Trials per year. It is estimated that
annually 750 notifications at $3.00 each will be mailed to 25
organizations/members.
Report of the Committee on Trial Review:
The very basis of justification for this resolution is erroneous in that it
states that “the Manual for Masonic charges and trials indicates that it follows
California law.” We cannot find any such statement or inference in the Manual
or in the CMC.
The Manual states, on page 14 E General Procedure 3, “The ordinary
rules of evidence employed in the Courts of the State of California shall be
followed as nearly as the circumstances permit”. This identical language is
found in the CMC Section 32320 – General Procedure at Page 252. This is the
only reference to California law in the CMC or the Manual.
We believe that the Trial Master and/or Commissioners should be
concerned only with the facts of the case before it. The sentencing should be
based on the seriousness of the unMasonic conduct according to the trial
findings and not on any previous problems of the accused. To expand the
inquiry for sentencing purposes by including prior offenses would necessitate
obtaining evidence and records from other lodges or jurisdictions. The
proceedings and findings of another lodge are confidential and not open by
review or use by anyone outside of the lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-03
Transfer of Masonic Trials
The proponents of this Resolution seek to require notification of a transfer of
Masonic trials, notice of the pending trial to members of the Lodge in which
charges originated and notice to all Lodges and Masonic Organizations of which
the accused was a member of the results of the trial.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the Grand Master may issue a change of venue for a
Masonic trial upon showing due cause; and
WHEREAS, various delaying tactics, including change of venue, are
most likely to be used by the accused when they have been accused in a prior
Masonic trial; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

199

WHEREAS, the originating Lodge membership is usually not notified
of the charges and the name of the accused, the pending Masonic trial or the
change of venue thereof; and
WHEREAS, the membership of the originating Lodge should be
notified of the change of venue as well as the charges and name of the accused;
and
WHEREAS, Lodge secretaries sometimes do not have a complete
record of all other Lodges and Masonic-affiliated organizations in California
that a member belongs to; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Secretary has this information regarding
membership in other Lodges.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that a new Section 32125
be added to the California Masonic Code as follows:
§32125. TRANSFER OF MASONIC TRIALS.
If an accused is being tried in a Lodge other than the Lodge in which the
charges originated, after the Trial Commissioners have been elected, the Grand
Secretary shall notify the Lodge in which charges originated of the transfer.
The Secretary of the Lodge in which the charges originated shall notify the
Lodge’s membership of the transfer and, if he has not already done so, of the
charges and the name of the accused.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 32430 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§32430. NOTICE TO MEMBER’S LODGE IF TRIAL ELSEWHERE
AND MASONIC ORGANIZATIONS.
In all cases where a trial is held in a Lodge other than the Lodge of which the
accused is a member or where the accused is a member of more than one Lodge,
the Grand Secretary shall notify the every Lodge of which the accused is a
member, of the result, upon the receipt by him of the transcript of the trial
record. The Grand Secretary shall notify every Masonic Organization, of which
he has reason to believe the accused to be a member, of the result, upon the
receipt by him of the transcript of the trial record, and shall notify each such
organization on the accused’s restoration.

200

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
From time to time it becomes necessary to move the place of a Masonic
trial from one Lodge to another. When this occurs, the members of the Lodge
in which the charges have originated may not be notified. The actual charges
and name of the accused are not disclosed to the Brethren until a trial
commission has been elected. If the place of trial has already been moved, the
Lodge in which the charges originated may have no actual notice of who the
accused is, and what the charges are. In a similar vein, because the verdict and
penalty are read in the Lodge where the trial is held, the Lodge in which the
charges originated may not receive formal notice of the results of the trial. As a
practical matter, lodge members do learn of the place of trial, and the results
thereof, through regular Masonic intercourse.
This Resolution provides that once Trial Commissioners have been
elected in the Lodge where the trial is to be held, the Grand Secretary would so
notify the secretary of the Lodge where the charges originated. The Lodge
secretary would, in turn, notify the members of the original lodge of the charges,
the name of the accused, and the place of trial. This Resolution further provides
that, once the trial has been completed and the trial record filed, the Grand
Secretary shall notify every Lodge of which the accused is a member, and every
Masonic Organization which the Grand Secretary believes the accused is a
member, of the results of the trial, as well as any subsequent restoration.
It is true that every member of an accused’s Lodge should learn when
one of its members has been charged with unmasonic conduct, and where the
trial is to be held. However, it is not the right of every Masonic Organization to
learn the results of the trial at the time the record is filed with Grand Lodge.
The results of the trial do not become final until after the proceedings are
analyzed by the Committee on Trial Review, that Committee makes its report to
Grand Lodge, and the members of Grand Lodge vote to accept or overturn the
results. It would be premature to require the Grand Secretary to send out
general notification of trial results before the decision becomes final. This could
possibly injure a Brother in his person or good name if, for example, he is
wrongfully found guilty of unmansonic conduct, the Grand Secretary sends out
his notifications, then the Committee on Trial review and Grand Lodge overturn
that verdict. Other Masonic Organizations have no need to know the results of a
Masonic trial until the verdict is final and adopted by Grand Lodge, and then
only if the verdict results in suspension or expulsion of a Brother.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

201

Report of the Committee on Trial Review:
This resolution attempts to insert provisions pertaining to the changes
of venue of a Masonic trial into the Manual. However, we cannot find anything
in the Manual addressing change of venue. More appropriately, this resolution
should involve an addition to CMC Section 1050 Powers (of the Grand Master)
D.
We agree that the lodge of the accused should be notified of the change
of venue when ordered by the Grand Master. However, announcing the name of
the accused and the charges may not be appropriate in all circumstances.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-04
Committee Recommendation
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow all committees to which
business is referred to recommend for or against the matters referred to them.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the purpose of referring business to be conducted at an
Annual Communication to committee is to have the committees inform the
voting delegates of the ramifications of such matters; and
WHEREAS, the committees to which business is referred are selected
because of their expertise in the subject matter of the business; and
WHEREAS, in the past some committees have been authorized to
make a recommendation and others have been precluded from doing so; and
WHEREAS,
recommendations.

there

should

be

consistency

in

a

policy

of

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 6230 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§6230. REFERENCE OF BUSINESS.
All business shall be referred to and reported on by one or more committees
before it can be finally acted on by the members of Grand Lodge, except by
their unanimous consent. All such committees shall report before any vote shall
be taken in Grand Lodge., and each such committee, except the Committees on
Jurisprudence and Finance, shall recommend for or against the business referred
to it. The vote shall be taken upon the business as originally presented.

202

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Resolution seeks to have all reporting committees, except
Jurisprudence and Finance, recommend, in addition to its report, a “for or
against” vote on business referred to it.
This proposal would change the entire nature of Committee legislative
reporting.
The purpose of the various Committee reports is to inform the Brethren
of the nature of the proposed legislation, its possible effects on our Grand
Lodge, and whether the proposal is or is not consistent with the policy and
general purposes of our institution. The Brethren themselves then debate the
issue, and make their decision based upon the Committee reports and the
arguments of their Brothers.
It is up to the Brethren assembled at the Annual Communication to
determine whether any particular proposed legislation should be enacted. That
determination can be made only if the Brethren receive neutral and unbiased
reports from the Grand Lodge Committees. By definition, if a reporting
Committee also suggests how the Brethren should vote, that Committee’s report
is no longer unbiased and neutral.
As an example, when this Committee states that a proposed resolution
is or is not consistent with Grand Lodge policy or general purposes, the
members or Grand Lodge are being informed as to whether the proposal
changes current Masonic Law, and if so, whether that change coincides or
conflicts with the policies this Grand Lodge has established over the past 150
years. The Committee does not, and should not, state whether that policy is
good or bad, or whether the policy should or should not be changed. That is for
the Brethren to decide, not this Committee.
The Legislative power of Grand Lodge rests in the hands of the
delegates to the Annual Communication. These delegates, the Masters and
Wardens of the constituent Lodges, have the confidence and trust of the lodge
members who elected them, and who also invested them with the authority to
vote on proposed legislation. These delegates have the ability to analyze
whether any given piece of proposed legislation should be enacted. They do not
need to be told how to vote by a Grand Lodge Committee.
The Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

203

Resolution No. 05-05
Emergent Matters
The proponents of this Resolution seek to specify what constitutes an emergent
matter.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, there is a requirement that most business be submitted to
the Grand Secretary 180 days in advance of an Annual Communication; and
WHEREAS, an exception is made from such requirement in the case
of emergent matters; and
WHEREAS, nowhere in the Code is there a definition of an emergent
matter; and
WHEREAS, non-emergent matters have heretofore been submitted as
emergent matters; and
WHEREAS, there is confusion over what constitutes an emergent
matter.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED a new Section 14 be
added to the California Masonic Code to read as follows:
§14. EMERGENT MATTERS.
Those matters which have arisen unexpectedly and therefore could not have
been presented to the Grand Secretary at least 180 days prior to the
commencement of the next Annual Communication, and which demand prompt
action.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the current Sections 14 through
23 of the California Masonic Code be renumbered to account for the insertion
of a new Section 14.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Current Masonic law addresses the procedural manner in which
emergent matters may be presented and considered at the Annual
Communication. The California Masonic Code provides that all proposed
legislation must by submitted no later than 180 days prior to the opening of the
Annual Communication, but matters of an emergent nature may be submitted
after that time. It is then left to the delegates at the Annual Communication to
determine whether the emergent matter should be considered on its merits. This

204

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

proposed resolution would add to the California Masonic Code the following
definition of “emergent matter”:
Those matters which have arisen unexpectedly and therefore
could not have been presented to the Grand Secretary at least
180 days prior to the commencement of the next Annual
Communication, and which demand prompt action.
It is not appropriate to place an artificial limitation on what constitutes
an emergent matter. Many matters may “arise unexpectedly,” but that does not
make them “emergencies.”
The facts and circumstances surrounding the reasons a matter should
be handled on an emergency basis differ with every emergent resolution, and
each such resolution should be dealt with based on the unique circumstances
that apply to it.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policies and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution CARRIED-OVER.
Resolution No. 05-06
Emergent Matter Debatable
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish that a motion to consider an
Emergent matter is debatable.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the determination whether to consider an emergent
resolution lies with the voting delegates; and
WHEREAS, that determination can only be properly made after
discussion of the propriety of its consideration.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that that Part V of the
California Masonic Code be amended to read as marked:
PART V
RULES OF PROCEDURE FOR ANNUAL AND
SPECIAL COMMUNICATIONS
The following Rules of Procedure shall govern the deliberations at each Annual
and Special Communication of the Grand Lodge.
All committee reports must be in the hands of the Grand Secretary by the close
of business on the first day of the Communication. Legislation shall be heard at

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

205

the sessions on the second and subsequent days of the Communication. Each
chairman of a committee to which legislation has been referred should be
present at all times during the sessions at which legislation will be considered
until all reports from his committee have been given. If a committeeman is not
present and prepared to report when called for, the Grand Secretary shall read
the committee’s report. If the Grand Secretary does not then have a copy of the
report to read, the vote will be taken without that report.
The Jurisprudence Committee shall report only once, unless there is a special
matter before the Grand Lodge which the committee and presiding officer
believe warrant individual attention. The presiding officer shall announce the
vote required for passage of each item before discussion begins.
Any legislation to be considered which has not been mailed to the Lodges shall
be printed in advance and distributed at or before the session at which the matter
shall be considered. No legislation may be modified at any time without the
consent of the proponent or his authorized representative written on the face of
the modified proposal. If any proposal is modified after it has been made
available to the delegates, any changes must be clearly brought to the attention
of the delegates at the Grand Lodge session by the presiding officer. If not so
presented by the presiding officer, the proposed modification shall NOT be a
part of the adopted legislation.
The Grand Secretary shall not read the minutes of any session during the
Communication. The minutes will be printed instead in the proceedings of the
Annual Communication.
A motion to consider an emergent matter shall be debatable.
When discussion of each resolution or other item of business is called for by the
presiding officer, the proponent, or his authorized representative, shall have the
option of speaking first or last. The proponent shall be allotted five minutes.
All other speakers from the floor shall be allotted three minutes each.
If the proponent speaks first, a speaker opposed to the matter shall speak next,
and the pro and con speakers shall alternate thereafter. If the proponent chooses
to speak last, a speaker in opposition shall begin the discussion, and the
speakers shall alternate thereafter. Only three speakers on each side of an issue,
including the proponent, shall be permitted on each item under discussion.
Speakers in favor shall speak from the microphone on the Grand Master’s left.
Speakers opposed shall speak from the microphone on the Grand Master’s right.
There shall be no lights on the floor nor shall any speaker’s microphone be cut
off. There shall be signal lights at the Grand Master’s station. The presiding
officer shall advise the speaker when his time has expired and request that he
immediately conclude his remarks.
If circumstances or the issue under discussion warrant, the presiding officer, in
his sole discretion, may waive any of the guidelines set forth above. For

206

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

example, the presiding officer at his discretion may grant speakers additional
time or may give more than six speakers permission to present their views. It is
contemplated that the waiver of these rules would only occur on issues of great
importance to the Craft.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The purpose of this Resolution is to require the delegates at the Annual
Communication to debate the propriety of considering proposed legislation that
comes in as an emergent matter. Current Masonic law provides that before an
emergent matter may be considered on its merits, the delegates must determine,
by a 5/6 vote, whether the matter should be considered at all. If 5/6ths vote in
favor, then the matter will be reported upon by all appropriate committees, and
will be debated and voted upon as would any other proposed legislation. The
first vote, that is whether to consider the matter at all, is strictly procedural in
nature, and has nothing to do with whether the proposed emergent matter should
become Masonic law.
It has never been the policy of this Grand Lodge to debate mere
procedural matters.
A review of the proceedings of past Annual
Communications shows that the issue of whether a proposed emergent
resolution will be discussed on the merits is voted upon without debate. This
saves valuable time that can be better spent on other issues that do require
debate, thoughtful analysis, and careful consideration. Procedural matters
simply do not merit that type of attention. Discussion on the procedural issues
involving emergent matters is unnecessary.
This Resolution is not consistent with policies and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.
Resolution CARRIED-OVER.
Resolution No. 05-07
No Report From Executive Committee
The proponents of this Resolution seek to preclude business before Grand
Lodge being referred to the Executive Committee.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the Executive Committee’s function is to counsel with
the Grand Master concerning the operation and affairs of the Grand Lodge; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

207

WHEREAS, the mission of those Committees to which business has
traditionally been referred is to study and counsel on matters which deal with
the business of the Grand Lodge and the constituent Lodges; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Master is a member of the Executive
Committee and should not engage in debate while presiding.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 6230 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§6230. REFERENCE OF BUSINESS.
All business shall be referred to and reported on by one or more committees
other than the Executive Committee before it can be finally acted on by the
members of Grand Lodge, except by their unanimous consent. All such
committees shall report before any vote shall be taken in Grand Lodge. The
vote shall be taken upon the business as originally presented.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this legislation seek to amend the California
Masonic Code to preclude the Executive Committee from reporting on the
business brought before the members of Grand Lodge, but still require that other
Committees, as appropriate, report before any vote is taken.
The function and duties of the Executive Committee, as set forth in
section 2060 of the California Masonic Code, is to counsel with the Grand
Master concerning the operation and affairs of the Grand Lodge, and to perform
such other duties as he designates.
Under our current law, special committees designated by the Grand
Master may be requested to make oral reports during the Annual
Communication. However, there is no designation of such a function within the
description of the duties of the Executive Committee.
If a the Executive Committee desires to share the views of its members
on proposed legislation, nothing precludes a member of that Committee from
going down on the floor to argue a given resolution, on the level, as do others
attending the Annual Communication.
This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
this Grand Lodge.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

208

Report of the Executive Committee:
California Masonic Code Section 6230 presently requires that all
business be referred to and reported on by committees before any vote is taken
at Grand Lodge.
Appendix A, Part V also requires that each committee to whom
legislation has been referred shall read its report at the Annual Communication.
Resolution No. 05-07 seeks to carve out an exception by providing that
there shall be no referral to, or reporting by, the Executive Committee of any
business to be voted upon by the Grand Lodge.
As a matter of reference, the California Masonic Code Section 2060
provides that the Executive Committee shall consist solely of the Grand Master,
Deputy Grand Master and Grand Wardens who are required to meet monthly
concerning the operations and affairs of the Grand Lodge. No other Grand
Lodge committee meets as long or as often as the Executive Committee which is
the only committee responsible for considering all issues before the Craft and
seeking continuity and direction in governance.
The Resolution would prevent the very officers you have elected to
progressively serve you from considering or reporting on important matters on
any Resolution which might affect our operations or affairs.
Brethren, this Resolution is designed to exclude you from hearing from
your elected officers.
The Resolution in its Whereas clause implies that the Executive
Committee, as opposed to other committees, would not study or seek counsel
regarding important matters before the Grand Lodge. It is further stated in the
Resolution that the Grand Master should not engage in debate while presiding.
The Grand Master has always presented his own recommendations, and when
he does so, the Deputy Grand Master presides.
It has been said that if the Executive Committee wishes to share its
views, nothing prevents an individual member from joining those on the floor.
The same logic could apply to all committees and require an individual member
of each committee to speak from the floor. To avoid committeemen racing for
the microphone where speakers are limited to three (3) Pro and three (3) Con,
our Grand Lodge has its committees report their findings by a committee report.
Your elected Executive Committee should not be treated differently.
Make sure my Brethren the Craft is fully informed.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

209

Resolution No. 05-08
Conducting Business In Any Degree
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow a Lodge’s presiding officer the
discretion to conduct business on the degree of his choice.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, regular Masonic lodges in the United States and
worldwide currently and historically conduct business on the first degree; and
the custom of doing business on the 3rd degree was an innovation in the U.S. in
1843; and
WHEREAS, it is necessary to maximize potential for membership
retention among Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft Masons by making lodge
activities as inclusive and available as possible; and
WHEREAS, many Masons who have not progressed due to time
constraints will appreciate the opportunity to attend meetings and will feel a
greater and ongoing fraternal connection with their lodge thus increasing the
likelihood that they will advance; and
WHEREAS, participation in the affairs of the Lodge and the
transaction of business by Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft Masons will
involve, encourage and motivate the leadership capabilities of its new members;
and
WHEREAS, an ability for Lodges to choose the degree upon which
they transact business will remove the impetus to rush Masons through the
degrees and enhance the opportunity for California Freemasons to embrace a
greater Lodge culture of Masonic education and Masonic formation; and
WHEREAS, under this proposed resolution, as in regular Masonic
jurisdictions, Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft Masons cannot vote, hold
office, or chair committees, candidates will have a greater opportunity to
observe Masonic Law and customs among their fellow brothers and learn of
these responsibilities before being charged with their duties and thus be far
better prepared to execute them.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 16010 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

210

§16010. CONDITIONS FOR CONDUCTING AND BUSINESS TO BE
TRANSACTED.
A Lodge shall not be opened, nor shall any work be done or business transacted,
except by, or in the presence of, and under the continual direction of the Master
or the ranking Warden or the Grand Master or his designated representative.
All business, except the examination of candidates in the lectures of the Entered
Apprentice and Fellow Craft degrees, the conferring of the Entered Apprentice
and Fellow Craft degrees, and the consideration of relief of a worthy Brother,
shall be done in a Lodge of Master Masons. Business may be conducted in a
Lodge of Entered Apprentices, Fellow Crafts or Master Masons, as the
presiding officer may determine. No Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft shall
be permitted to ballot on applications or for the election of officers, or to debate
or to vote on any other Lodge business. No Lodge shall transact any business
with less than five members present except the conferring of degrees. The Tiler
is present while at his post in the Tiler’s room.
All business shall be transacted at a Stated Meeting, except the following may
also be conducted at a Special Meeting:
A.

Collections or appropriations for charity;

B.

Conferring degrees;

C.

Ceremonial observances;

D.

Electing a representative to the Grand Lodge;

E.

Balloting for commissioners to try charges of unmasonic conduct; or

F.

After dispensation from the Grand Master:
1.

Balloting for and conferring the degrees upon an applicant
without reference to a committee, or

2.

Receiving and acting upon the application of a rejected
applicant within less than 12 months after the date of his
rejection.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
From time to time this Grand Lodge has been called upon to determine
whether it will change its long-standing custom and rule of conducting business
only in the Third Degree. On previous occasions, Resolutions permitting the
conduct of business in the First or Second Degree, or at an untiled meeting, have
failed to achieve the necessary votes to become law.
The proponents of Resolution No. 05-08 are suggesting a somewhat
different procedure. Instead of providing that lodges shall do their work in the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

211

First or Second Degree, the proponents propose that “Business may be
conducted in either a Lodge of Entered Apprentices, Fellowcrafts or Master
Masons, whichever the presiding officer deems necessary.”
The presiding officer of course may be the Master or one of the
Wardens, as the case may be. Consequently the decision may not be up to one
officer alone, the Master, but on the desires or decisions of one of three officers,
whomever may be presiding at that particular time.
This would promote inconsistency if the Master, for example, is
desirous of maintaining the present practice of conducting business only in the
Master Mason Degree, while one of his Wardens, when acting as Master, may
opt to hold the meeting in a different degree. Consequently, Entered
Apprentices or Fellowcrafts may attend and participate, insofar as permitted,
depending solely on who may be conducting that particular stated meeting, and
there would be no required advance notice to Entered Apprentices or
Fellowcrafts that they might attend and participate. Consequently, the decision
of the presiding officer would be somewhat meaningless unless Entered
Apprentices or Fellowcrafts “just happen” to be present.
The Resolution does provide that where the particular presiding officer
decides to have business conducted in a lodge of Entered Apprentices or
Fellowcrafts, the Entered Apprentices or Fellowcrafts “shall not be permitted to
ballot on Petitions or for elections of officers or to debate and vote on lodge
business.” Apparently, therefore, they will be there just to listen, and perhaps
voice their opinions, but not have any real say in the conduct of the lodge
business.
This Resolution generates too many questions of procedure and
practice that would not necessarily be uniformly resolved within the constituent
lodges.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Your Ritual Committee acknowledges this concept and has carefully
evaluated the impact it could create, if adopted.
To allow a Lodge’s presiding officer the discretion to conduct business
on the degree of his choice would create a practice of inconsistency and further
challenge this Committee’s effort to maintain uniformity throughout this
Jurisdiction.
If the business of a Stated Meeting was conducted in a Lodge of
Entered Apprentices or Fellow Crafts, various changes in our adopted ritual
would be required such as instructions to Master Masons on approaching the

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

212

Altar when balloting on candidates and the conclusion of such ballot. The
monitoring of eligible voter identification and method of voting on any
administrative issue of the Lodge when Entered Apprentices and Fellow Crafts
are present. The proponents propose to change the CMC Section 16010 stating
in part “No Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft shall be permitted to ballot on
applications or for the election of officers, or to debate or to vote on any Lodge
business.” How can they participate and be motivated if they are excluded in all
matters?
A Master Mason proficiency or any ritual or discussion pertaining to
the third degree could not be conducted with those present who are not Master
Masons and they would have to be excluded and the opening of a Lodge of
Master Masons would then be required.
Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft Masons could be embarrassed
and excluded when attending their own Lodge or visiting another if they arrived
and were informed that the presiding officer was opening the Lodge in a degree
higher than their own.
Your Ritual Committee does not support the adoption of this
Resolution.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-09
Storage of Alcoholic Beverages On Masonic Property
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow Lodges to store alcoholic
beverages on Masonic property under certain safeguards and conditions.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, a Lodge may, under California Masonic Code Section
15050, serve alcoholic beverages under suitable conditions within its facilities;
and
WHEREAS, if such beverages are to be made available in appropriate
quantities, they must be obtained in advance, requiring storage until the time of
intended use; and
WHEREAS, it is inevitable that there will, from time to tome, be
surplus amounts of such beverages; and
WHEREAS, these must be stored somewhere if there is any reason to
believe that they could be used at a future event; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

213

WHEREAS, the packing, transporting, and storage of such beverages
elsewhere may cause an undue burden on the brother required to perform such
duties, and will also require them to be moved again; and
WHEREAS, the repeated multiple episodes of transportation of such
beverages carries the probability of loss due to breakage in such processes,
causing unnecessary waste.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 15050 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§15050. ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES.
Alcoholic beverages may be sold, served, and consumed on property owned by
or leased to a Masonic Lodge, Temple Association, or Masonic Organization
and at Masonic activities held elsewhere if the following requirements are
observed:
A.

Alcoholic beverages may not be sold, served, or consumed in a Lodge
room or in a room in which Masonic ceremonies are normally
conducted except when such room serves a dual purpose as a
ceremonial room and social room;

B.

A Masonic Lodge, Temple Association, or Masonic Organization may
not maintain, on a regular or permanent basis, a bar stocked with
alcoholic beverages, but may store alcoholic beverages elsewhere on
the property in a secured area to which unauthorized persons do not
have access, such as a locked cabinet or closet, to the extent permitted
by and in accordance with rules established by the property owner;

C.

The funds of a Masonic Lodge or Temple Association may not be used
to purchase alcoholic beverages;

D.

Alcoholic beverages may not be served, consumed, or sold during the
course of a meeting of a Masonic Lodge, Temple Association, or
Masonic Organization;

E.

A Masonic Lodge or Temple Association may not directly or indirectly
apply for, assume title to, or operate under any government license or
permit for the purpose of consuming, serving or selling alcoholic
beverages, whether in, on, or away from premises owned, leased, or
rented by either of them;

F.

A Masonic Lodge, Temple Association, or Masonic Organization may
not rent or lease property in which it has an ownership interest to
business establishments wherein alcoholic beverages are served,
consumed or sold unless such service, consumption, and sale are
incident to and not the primary purpose of the business; and

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

214
G.

Any sale, service, or consumption of alcoholic beverages pursuant to
this section must comply with all applicable laws of the State of
California.

If this resolution is adopted, the following change will be made to the
last line of the fourth paragraph of the interpretation of Section 15050 of the
California Masonic Code in the Appendix:
Alcohol should not be stored elsewhere on Masonic property either.
Alcoholic beverages obtained in accordance with the provisions of the
California Masonic Code may, with the express consent of all Lodges
and Temple Associations involved, be stored in an appropriately
secured location on Masonic property.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of Resolution 05-09 seek to modify our law to permit
the storage of alcoholic beverages on property owned or leased by a lodge,
building association or Masonic Lodge.
Alcoholic beverages in California are strictly controlled by State Laws
which are enforced by State and Local law enforcement officers. Section 15050
of our Masonic Code was carefully written to accomplish the following:
1.

To permit a lodge to sponsor an event wherein the incidental
use of alcoholic beverages on lodge properties is allowed.

2.

To protect a lodge property from the requirements of State
licensing laws applicable to those who furnish, sell or serve
alcoholic beverages.

A close reading of section 15050 of our Masonic Code makes clear that
the lodge never owns the alcoholic beverages which may have been served at an
event, and the lodge’s funds are not used in purchasing any alcoholic beverages.
Further, excess ticket sales proceeds over the costs of the event cannot be given
to the lodge until all reimbursements have been made. Thus, a lodge is not in
the business of profiting from the sale of liquor. These conditions were
included to protect our lodges from the intrusion of the State Alcohol Beverage
Control Authority.
Storing and serving alcohol beverages is not like storing and serving
coffee. The storage and subsequent serving of alcohol on our properties will not
only compromise the narrow line which we now walk, but may bring our lodge
premises under the authority of the State Alcohol Beverage licensing laws,
requiring a State ABC license, often a City license, and subsequent inspections

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

215

of our lodges properties and financial records by state and local officers, entirely
at their pleasure. Furthermore, it may jeopardize our fraternal status and bring
us under the many controls that apply to all businesses in this state.
Though the proponents of this resolution intend it to make life less
complicated for those lodges who have chosen to have alcohol served at their
events, these changes, however, will create a status under State law which is
unacceptable from a fraternal point of view.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Insurance:
Your Insurance Committee was asked to report on Resolution No. 0509 “Storage of Alcoholic Beverages on Masonic Property” with regard to any
insurance coverage restrictions.
While the committee’s concern evolves around the quantity which may
be stored, there is no restriction in the Property Insurance Policy or the General
Liability Policy relative to “storage of alcoholic beverages”.
Report of the Committee on Masonic Properties:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow Lodges to store
alcoholic beverages on Masonic property under certain safeguards and
conditions.
The Masonic Properties Committee has reviewed the resolution and the
committee is divided as to how to handle the storage of alcoholic beverages.
Many of our lodges in the state do have a social time before social
functions such as Hiram Award Dinners, Golden Veteran Award Dinners, etc.,
and they do serve alcoholic beverages to their guests.
They have stored the beverages at the Lodge, and in the cases I have
observed, they have both removed it from the premises after the function, and/or
also stored it on the property.
It is our position that alcoholic beverages obtained in accordance with
the provisions of the California Masonic Code may, and with the express
consent of all Lodges and Temple or Hall Associations involved, be stored in an
appropriately secured location on Masonic property.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

216

It is also our position, that alcoholic beverages will not be used or
served prior to or after a Tiled Lodge or Masonic Meeting, only social type
functions.
Resolution CARRIED-OVER.
Resolution No. 05-10
Past Masters Vote
The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit every Past Master a vote in
Grand Lodge.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, every Past Master of this jurisdiction in good standing is
in fact a member of this Grand Lodge; and
WHEREAS, the Past Masters of each lodge are currently allowed only
one (1) vote collectively while many other members of Grand Lodge e.g. Past
Grand Officers, sitting Masters, Senior and Junior Wardens of all chartered
lodges, all currently sitting elected Grand Lodge Officers and most of the
currently sitting appointed Grand Lodge Officers enjoy the accepted democratic
right of one man one vote; and
WHEREAS, the policy of disallowing Past Masters the right to an
individual vote that is enjoyed by other Grand Lodge members is in effect a
disenfranchisement of the Past Masters who have over the years contributed so
much to the fraternity and in total comprise the majority of the members of this
Grand Lodge; and
WHEREAS, each and every member of this Grand Lodge should be
free from bias or favoritism of any kind, most especially where the ballot is
concerned; and
WHEREAS, this Grand Lodge as a body must have absolute zero
tolerance for bias or favoritism of any kind toward any of its members.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 620 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§620. VOTES.
The following shall each be entitled to one vote:
A.

Elective and appointive Grand Lodge Officers except the Grand Tiler
and the Assistant Grand Tiler;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

217

B.

Past Grand Officers;

C.

Masters, Senior Wardens and Junior Wardens of chartered Lodges; and

D.

Past Masters collectively of each chartered Lodges.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this resolution seek to allow Past Master’s of this
jurisdiction, who attend the Annual Communication, the right of one vote per
Past Master attending. However noble this may sound, in this particular
incidence it would prove detrimental.
The voting rights of Grand Lodge members, as set forth in California
Masonic Code section 620, are primarily vested in the constituent Lodges, by
giving each Lodge four votes, one each for the Master, Senior and Junior
Wardens, and a collective vote for the Past Master’s of the Lodge. Thus the vast
majority of voting power is evenly spread among the Lodges, and in particular,
in those men who are currently active in the government of their Lodge.
To give each Past Master a separate vote would contravene Grand
Lodge policy in a number of ways. It would potentially give the older Lodges
(those with a greater number of Past Masters), more voting power than other
Lodges. Next, it would give the Past Masters of any Lodge, men who may not
necessarily be actively engaged in the day to day governance of their Lodge,
greater voting power than those who run the Lodge on a daily bases.
Historically, many ,if not more, Past Masters attend the Annual Communication
than do the Senior Lodge Officers, and Past Masters living closer to Grand
Lodge are, because of the expense involved, more likely to attend the Annual
Communication than those living further away. Thus, at any given Annual
Communication, Past Masters would have disproportionate voting power,
destroying the balance of power embodied in the Masonic Code. If the Past
Masters of each Lodge retain their single collective vote, the balance of voting
power will remain equitable with all of the constituent Blue Lodges.
Resolutions similar to this one have been considered in 1998, 1999 and 2000,
and were defeated each time.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

218

Report of the Committee on Finance:
Most Worshipful, the Finance Committee determined there will be an
estimated cost of $2,250 per year expense to Grand Lodge for printing of
additional ballots. Grand Lodge estimate presently there are 9,000 Past Masters
from Grand Lodge database.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-11
Grand Lodge Budget
The proponents of this Resolution seek to add additional requirements with
respect to the Grand Lodge Budget, including a requirement for a two-thirds
rather than a simple majority vote for adoption.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, in this time of financial hardship in individual lodges, of
corporate scandal, and of gross financial misrepresentation in the business
community, it is of utmost importance that Freemasonry lead by example; and
WHEREAS, to make the statement to all that honest and above board
financial dealings should be the norm for all society; and
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodge of California wishes to set the example
and hold our heads high with the openness and thoroughness of our finances;
and
WHEREAS, we expect no less of ourselves than we expect of others,
let us show the way.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 7040,
paragraphs A, C and E of the California Masonic Code be amended as follows:
§7040. GRAND LODGE BUDGET.
Grand Lodge shall have a budget for each fiscal year as follows:
A.

The Grand Lodge Budget shall be prepared under the direction and
control of the Deputy Grand Master. The Grand Treasurer and the
Grand Lodge Committee on Finance shall consult in the preparation of
the Grand Lodge Budget as requested by the Deputy Grand Master.
The Deputy Grand Master shall be assisted in the preparation of the
budget by such other persons as he may select to assist him. The
budget shall be in sufficient detail to permit a full and complete

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

219

understanding of all budget items and sub-items. The budget shall
provide line items detailing the total compensation (including salaries,
benefits, perquisites, rents, honoraria, etc.) to each Grand Lodge
Officer for the fiscal year.
B.

Each Grand Lodge Budget shall be based upon the per capita sum to be
collected for Master Masons carried on the rolls of each Constituent
Lodge as of the June 30th most immediately preceding the
commencement of the fiscal year covered by such budget.

C.

The proposed Grand Lodge Budget shall be transmitted by the Grand
Secretary to the Masters of the several Lodges, Inspectors and
members of the Committee on Finance at least 30 days prior to the
commencement date of the next Annual Communication. The Master
shall post the budget in a conspicuous place in his Lodge for
examination by any and all members. Copies shall be made available
to any member in good standing so requesting a copy, for a fee not to
exceed the cost of the copy and mailing. No administrative fee may be
charged.

D.

The proposed Grand Lodge Budget shall be referred to the Committee
on Finance for its review and report to Grand Lodge before the vote is
taken whether to adopt the budget.

E.

On the first day of the Annual Communication, Tthe Deputy Grand
Master shall personally present the proposed budget to the members of
Grand Lodge for their approval. The budget shall then be open to
questions from the members assembled for not less than four hours.
The Grand Lodge Budget shall, on the same day, be adopted by a twothirds majority vote. If this majority is not obtained, the budget from
the fiscal year prior to the year of the defeated budget shall remain in
force.

F.

The Grand Treasurer and the Committee on Finance shall monitor
compliance with the adopted Grand Lodge Budget during the Grand
Lodge year.

G.

There shall be three expense categories in the Grand Lodge Budget:
fraternal, appropriations and general and administrative. An expense
category in the Grand Lodge Budget shall not be exceeded without the
consent of the Grand Master. An expense category shall not be
exceeded by more than five percent without the consent of at least two
of the following: the Grand Secretary, the Grand Treasurer and the
Chairman of the Committee on Finance.

H.

No appropriation of money shall be made at any Annual
Communication after the election of Grand Officers, except by a
three-fourths vote of the members present.

220

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of Resolution No. 05-11 seek to have a four-hour
minimum discussion of the budget that will be presented to the Grand Lodge,
“on the first day of the Annual Communication”. The addition of what, in
effect, is another half day would, we think, increase the budget of the Grand
Lodge, and although the purpose of education is laudatory, the additional
expense to the Grand Lodge by adding an additional half-day session would
result in an additional, not reduced, expense for the conduct of the Annual
Communication.
The Grand Lodge of California has been under severe financial
restraints and has been trying all kinds of methods and means to reduce the cost
of the Grand Lodge Sessions now taking place on Monday through Wednesday
of Grand Lodge Week, and the Public Opening on Sunday afternoon.
Opportunities are being afforded for changes in the timing of the Grand Lodge
even to the extent of having them on weekends for financial reasons, if no other.
The proposed legislation also mandates that the Grand Lodge budget
“on the same day shall be adopted by a two-thirds majority vote,” and further
that “If this majority is not obtained, the budget from the fiscal year prior to the
year of the defeated budget shall remain in force.”
We can anticipate bedlam and confusion in the Craft if, as provided in
this proposed code revision, a budget is not passed and the reversion to a
previous budget would prevail. The very areas which the proponents seek to
discover and educate us on would be lost. We would not be dealing with a
current budget for the future, but rather a budget, the expenditure of which we
had just completed. If there were, for example, a substantial deficit in the prior
year’s budget, we would be approving the same deficit for the current year.
We appreciate the need for greater education in the finances of our
Grand Lodge, but do not believe there are any secrets, or any need to impose
such a penalty upon the Grand Lodge financial situation as an additional four
hours time, as well as the necessity of a 2/3 affirmative vote, or the reversion to
the previous year’s budget this proposed legislation would require.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
Most Worshipful, the passage of this resolution would have significant
impact on the expenses of Grand Lodge. The cost of this year’s Annual
Communication less fixed expenses is $125,000. The number of hours that
Grand Lodge is in session is 22 hours. The cost per hour therefore is $5,682.
Considering that the time spent approving the budget in previous years is

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

221

approximately one half hour, the increased time to set aside 4 hours for debate
would add 3 ½ hours to the Annual Communication, or an increase cost of
$19,887. This equates to an increase in per capita of $0.27.
The passage of this resolution has other potential effects beyond those
mentioned above. If the budget did not pass the 2/3 vote requirement, and
Grand Lodge was forced to operate under the previous year budget, it would
effect the operation in other areas as well:
1.

Employee salaries would be frozen at prior year levels causing
potential turnover issues.

2.

Employee benefit costs would be frozen, and with continuing increases
in cost of living expenses, would result in cutting back benefits.

3.

Property and liability insurance would be frozen. With ever increasing
insurance premiums, coverage for the lodges would have to be
adjusted. Grand Lodge pays for property, liability and workers comp
coverage for Lodges.

The Finance Committee therefore, recommends a NO vote on
Resolution 05-11.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-12
Gambling
The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit any gambling activity not
prohibited by federal, state or local law.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, most of the Lodges in this Jurisdiction are experiencing a
decline in membership; and
WHEREAS, most of the Lodges in this Jurisdiction are therefore also
struggling to maintain a dues structure that is adequate to pay the Grand Lodge
per capita tax and meet operating demands; and
WHEREAS, the ancient landmarks of Freemasonry only prohibit
individual Master Masons from frequenting gambling houses, and not from
gambling per se; and
WHEREAS, the original Section 288, Regulation Against Gambling,
was written merely to keep individual Lodges from violating State and Federal
gambling laws, which have been significantly revised from their prior
restrictions; and

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

222

WHEREAS, the current Section 15090 is unclear, unsettled and a
constant cause of dissention and disagreement among Master Masons; and
WHEREAS, no record can be found that Section 15090 was
specifically presented, and voted into existence in a fashion consistent with the
by-laws and ordinances of the Grand Lodge of California.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 1060 be
amended as marked:
§1060. DUTIES.
The Grand Master shall:
A.

Preside in Grand Lodge at all Annual and Special Communications.
There shall be no appeal from his rulings as presiding officer;

B.

Present, at each Annual Communication, a written message setting
forth:
1.

All his official acts during the year;

2.

The general condition of Masonry within the Jurisdiction; and

3.

Recommending such legislation as he deems necessary or
expedient for the welfare of the Craft;

C.

Constitute the Lodge for which a charter is granted in accordance with
the ancient usages and regulations. He may do so either in person or
by a duly authorized representative;

D.

Cause to be personally served on any Mason appearing to be engaged
in the conduct or promotion of any raffle, lottery or other form of
gambling that is being conducted, or is about to be conducted in the
name of, or for the benefit of any committee or other subordinate body
of any Lodge or Masonic Organization forbidden by applicable federal,
state or local laws, a notice requiring that Mason to immediately
discontinue his participation.
If the Mason fails to discontinue his participation in such enterprise,
the Grand Master shall refer the matter to a Lodge of this Jurisdiction
for trial on the charge of unmasonic conduct. The Junior Warden of
the Lodge shall prefer the charges against the Mason. Pending the
trial, the Grand Master may suspend the Mason from all the rights and
benefits of Masonry;

E.

Exercise general and careful supervision over the Craft, and see that
the Constitution and Ordinances of Grand Lodge are strictly
maintained, supported, and obeyed; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA
F.

223

Discharge all necessary executive functions of Grand Lodge when it is
not in session.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 15090 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§15090. GAMBLING.
Neither a No Lodge, a Masonic Organization, nor a or individual Mason shall,
directly or indirectly, give or attempt to give the aid or countenance of Masonry
to any game, lottery, bingo, or other gambling enterprise forbidden by
applicable federal, state or local laws, nor shall any Mason, or participate in, the
conduct or preparation of promotion of any such game, any raffle, lottery,
bingo, or other form of gambling enterprise, conducted in the name of, or
directly or indirectly for the monetary benefit of, any Lodge or of any Masonic
Organization. No A Lodge shall not accept any of the proceeds or profits of any
raffle, lottery or other gambling enterprise forbidden by applicable federal, state
or local laws.
Prizes may be awarded if not purchased from monies collected as admission
fees and if no separate charge is made for the chance to win.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 15095 which reads as
follows be deleted in its entirety.
§15095. BINGO.
A Lodge or Masonic Organization may sponsor a bingo game, if the sponsor
observes the following rules:
A.
Attendance at the event is limited to the sponsor’s members, their
families and friends.
There are no signs or other advertising visible to the general public
B.
referring to the game.
C.
No donation, admission fee, meal purchase or other charge is accepted
or required for the opportunity to participate.
D.
Prizes of minimal value may be awarded if purchased by the sponsor or
donated.

224

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this resolution seek to change our laws to permit
fund raising through gambling. This resolution would also remove the authority
of the Grand Master to act against a mason who promotes gambling enterprises
conducted in the name of, or for the benefit of, any lodge or Masonic
organization. There has been a long standing policy in California Masonry,
based upon moral and ethical beliefs, to prohibit gambling within our Fraternity.
The proponents of this Resolution suggest that our current law, as
found in California Masonic Code section 15090, may not have been
legitimately enacted. However, the following brief history shows that is not the
case. At our 1938 Annual Communication, the Grand Lodge adopted a decision
of Grand Master Earl Warren which made it a Masonic offense to gamble in
lodges and other Masonic organizations. That prohibition against gambling was
codified and, subsequently, at the 1991 Annual Communication, the Special
Committee on Code Revision, which had been formed in 1988, moved adoption
of its final report, which recommended adoption of their revised Masonic Code,
to be effective at the close of the 1992 Annual Communication. Grand Lodge
adopted the motion. The prohibition against gambling thus became section
15090.
At the 1994 Annual Communication of Grand Lodge, Grand Master
Blomseth recommended that a special committee be appointed to study our
prohibition of Bingo in our lodges and Masonic Organizations. The subsequent
report of that committee served as the basis for Grand Master Stovall’s
Recommendation No. 95-08, which, when adopted, became section 15095.
This section permits lodges and Masonic organizations to conduct the game of
bingo as a purely social activity not in conflict with the gaming laws of
California pertaining to games of chance, as found in the California Penal Code.
The modifications proposed for section 15090 will exempt Masonic
organizations, but not lodges or individual masons, from giving the aid and
countenance of Masonry to gambling enterprises. This proposal will also
permit the proceeds or profits of a gambling enterprise to be accepted by a
lodge. These modifications, as in the changes proposed for section 1060, also
rely on adding the phrase, “gambling enterprise forbidden by applicable federal,
state or local laws.” This means that the name of Masonry could be used to
promote properly licensed, controlled, policed and audited gambling enterprises.
This would result because there are numerous forms of gambling not forbidden
by law if the proper fees, licensing, strict controls and auditing are exercised.
Finally, this resolution proposes to delete section 15095, which permits
social bingo, from our Code, and returns the game of bingo to the list of other
games of chance and gambling enterprises, which are strictly controlled by
federal, state and local gaming laws. This action would prohibit our lodges and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

225

Masonic organizations from conducting social bingo games, returning us to the
law that existed prior to 1995.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 05-13
Withdrawn
Resolution No. 05-14
Permits Sierra Valley Lodge No. 184 To Hold Only 10 Stated Meetings A Year
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow Sierra Valley Lodge No. 184 to
hold only ten stated meetings per year.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the 2000 Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of
California overwhelmingly approved the Gravel Range Lodge No. 59 and
Harmony Lodge No. 164 resolution to allow only ten stated meetings per year;
and
WHEREAS, Sierra Valley Lodge No. 184 suffers similar difficulties
with inclement weather, small number of membership, aging and infirm
members, many of whom live significant distances from the Lodge.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Section 16030 of
the California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§16030. STATED MEETINGS.
A Lodge shall hold a Stated Meeting for the transaction of its regular business
once in each calendar month, except that Lodges whose By-Laws provide for
meetings fixed by the phase of the moon shall hold a Stated Meeting each lunar
month. The foregoing notwithstanding, Sierra Valley Lodge No. 184, Gravel
Range Lodge No. 59, Mountain Shade Lodge No. 18, Truckee Lodge No. 200
and Harmony Lodge No. 164 shall only be required to hold a Stated Meeting for
the transaction of its regular business once in each of ten calendar months
during the year, as it may provide in its By-laws.
A Lodge may be opened for a Stated Meeting prior to the time fixed in its ByLaws. No business shall be transacted prior to such time.

226

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Inspector may grant a dispensation to change the date of a Stated Meeting,
as provided for elsewhere in this Code.
In the event that the Master of a Lodge determines that inclement weather,
including ice, snow, and flood conditions, existing on the day established in the
By-Laws of the Lodge for its Stated Meeting, makes it hazardous for members
of the Lodge to attend the meeting, he may, with the consent of the Inspector of
the District to which the Lodge is assigned, postpone the Stated Meeting to the
same day of the week next following the week scheduled for the Stated
Meeting. Upon such postponing, the Master shall notify, to the extent possible,
all members of the Lodge residing within 50 miles of the meeting place of the
Lodge.
Should a Stated Meeting be postponed, the business transacted at the postponed
Stated Meeting shall be limited to the reading of the minutes of the last Stated
and subsequent Special Meetings, action thereon, payment of the invoices of the
Lodge, and such other business of an emergent nature as determined by the
Master to require immediate consideration. No other new business shall be
considered.

* Requires appropriate revision for Lunar Lodges and for Gravel Range Lodge

Lodge No. 164 and Sierra Valley Lodge No. 184.

Mountain Shade Lodge No. 18, Gravel Range Lodge No. 59 and Harmony
Lodge No. 164.
Sierra Valley Lodge No. 184, having similar issues as the above
mentioned Lodges, should be granted the same consideration.
