REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE
ON FINANCE
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
During the past year, the Finance Committee has also been restructured
to comply with emerging Federal and State regulations, and provide the highest
level of financial oversight for this Grand Lodge. The Finance Committee now
has three distinct roles to service the Grand Lodge and Related Entities: as
Finance Committee for the Grand Lodge; Audit Committee for the related
entities; and Retirement Committee.

282

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Audit Committee is charged with the review of the annual audits
for the Grand Lodge and Related Entities, recommending to the Grand Master
the retention of audit firms, and overseeing any internal audits requested by any
of the several Boards, or the Committee itself. The Committee reviewed the
relationship with the external auditors, PricewaterhouseCoopers. Due to the
changing public accounting environment and the changing needs of the Grand
Lodge, the Committee recommended to the Grand Master the appointment of
Moss Adams as the independent auditors for the Grand Lodge and all Related
Entities.
The Retirement Committee is charged with oversight and general
administration of the Grand Lodge and Related Entities Defined Benefit
Retirement Plan. Working closely with external actuaries, independent
accountants and the Investment Committee of the Grand Lodge, the Committee
assures the effective and efficient management of the retirement plan.
Our external auditors, PricewaterhouseCoopers completed the audit of
the Grand Lodge books and records of the other related entities for this period
ending July 1, 2005. They issued an unqualified opinion on the management of
Grand Lodge and found no areas of concern.
The Finance Committee of your Grand Lodge reports that is has
reviewed all the Grand Master’s Recommendations and the proposed
Legislation before the Grand Lodge. Several items, including Grand Master’s
Recommendations Number s 05-5, 05-6, 05-7, 05-8 and Resolution Numbers
05-02, 05-10, 05-11, 05-13 will be reported on at the appropriate time. All
other Grand Master’s Recommendations and Resolutions will have no financial
impact to the Grand Lodge budget.
The Finance Committee has thoroughly reviewed the fiscal year 20052006 budget proposed by Deputy Grand Master Fred Sorsabal. The proposed
budget reflects an increase just over $64,000 or 3.29% over the prior approved
budget. Of this amount, lodge insurance and workers compensation coverage
provided for the lodges accounts for approximately 1/3 of the total increase.
The budget also projects a continuing decline in membership over the previous
year. The Grand Lodge budget projects 68,816 members, or a decrease of
slightly more than 4,000 members. Your Grand Lodge leadership is working to
create new and exciting programs to increase the membership of the fraternity.
The Finance Committee has also reviewed the proposed per capita
request from Senior Grand Warden Melvyn Stein. Through this per capita, the
Grand Lodge will continue to provide necessary support for the lodges, Masonic
hall associations and to put more resources in critical programs such as
membership and leadership development around the state, even as it continues
to face significant cost increases for these programs.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

283

The Finance Committee, in its multiple capacities, continues to provide
you and your Grand Lodge with effective financial oversight and transparency
of the financial resources entrusted to us.
Respectfully submitted
Rodger D. Siems, Chairman

REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE
ON INVESTMENT

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F. & A. M. of California:
The Investment Committee is charged with the prudent handling of
Grand Lodge investment funds. These funds consist primarily of the General
and Endowment funds for the Masonic Homes of California, but also include
the investment portfolios of the California Masonic Foundation, California
Masonic Memorial Temple, the Grand Lodge Retirement plan and the Grand
Lodge itself. We take this charge very seriously. In its simplest terms, we
manage the investment funds with an eye both for growth and preservation of
capital. We strive to maintain a diversified portfolio that is designed to generate
the necessary funds for the continuing operation of the Masonic functions as
well as allow for growth for future needs. In doing so, we constantly scan the
horizon in an attempt to manage the funds to take advantage of long-term trends
and opportunities. The Grand Lodge Investment Committee utilizes the services
of professional investment management firms to gain access to the capital
markets. Funds are primarily invested in U.S. and international equity and fixed
income markets.
The capital markets in which the Grand Lodge invests had positive
performance over the past fiscal year. U.S. Equity and Fixed Income markets
had single digit returns while the International Equity market posted a low
double digit return. The equity markets have struggled to gain ground as
potential uncertainties surrounding oil prices, housing, Iraq and interest rates
continue to trump the solid gains in corporate profitability. Despite the Federal
Reserve’s efforts to control inflation by raising interest rates, the Treasury bond
yield curve has continued to flatten.
The U.S. equity markets returned 8.1% (as measured by the Russell
3000 index) over the twelve-month period ending June 30, 2005. Performance
in the second half of 2004 was mixed. Stocks were down 1.9% and up 10.2%,
respectively, in the 3rd and 4th quarters of last year. While returns in the first
half of 2005 were also mixed, performance was less volatile. The U.S. stock
market returned a negative 2.2% in the 1st quarter of this year and positive 2.2%

284

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

in the 2nd quarter. The international stock markets were up 13.7% as reported
by the MSCI EAFE index. The Lehman Aggregate Bond Index returned 6.8%
for the year.
“Muddling along” best describes world markets over the last fiscal
year. Investors have had to deal with volatile oil prices, a rising US dollar,
ambiguous economic reports and puzzling yield changes in the bond market.
The coming fiscal year is difficult to predict with the Fed hinting at raising rates
plus the continuing high cost of oil. As these two indicators play out, it will be
interesting to see how global markets react. Although the markets may remain
volatile as events play out, the Investment Committee will continue to oversee
the management of the portfolios under its charge in the same consistent and
prudent manner that has been utilized in the past.
The Investment Committee does not attempt to take advantage of
perceived short-term anomalies in the markets by chasing the most recent top
performing asset class. Rather, it arrives at its recommended allocation to
stocks and bonds and other asset classes only after very careful due diligence
and consideration. The purpose of the funds, their spending requirements,
investment time horizon and ability to assume risk are all factored into the
recommendations. The possibility and duration of potential losses are carefully
weighed against the long-term potential for appreciation of assets after
withdrawals are taken into account. The Investment Committee recommends
asset allocations that will provide the highest probability that the funds will be
able to meet their respective objectives over the long-term.
In order to fulfill our responsibilities in accordance with the Grand
Lodge Investment Policy and Philosophy, the Investment Committee meets
quarterly with our Investment Consultant, Ennis/Knupp and our individual
portfolio managers. We review their respective performance compared to
various indices representing their objective. This process provides the
Investment Committee with the necessary data to adequately evaluate each
investment manager on a timely basis, and to observe whether they continue to
achieve the objectives of their mandate.
The market value of the Masonic Homes General and Endowment
portfolios as of June 30, 2005 was $660,564,720 compared to $633,222,171 a
year earlier. This, combined with the other investment portfolios on which the
Investment Committee advises, resulted in combined assets totaling slightly less
than $729,000,000.
As of June 30, 2005, the combined funds of Grand Lodge and the
related Masonic entities were up 5.8% for the twelve-month period. This
compares to 8.9% for our custom benchmark index. While a consistent trailing
performance relative to the benchmark would be of concern to us, the
Investment Committee is not troubled over the results of the past year. The
underperformance was primarily due to the U.S. Equity Manager aggregate

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

285

portfolio returning only 2.1% while the Russell 3000 Index returned 8.1% for
the same time period. Our portfolio was over-weighted value stocks which had
the largest negative impact on the portfolio.
For the three-year period ending June 30th, the combined Grand Lodge
and related entities funds were up 7.6% versus 9.5% for the benchmark. Over
the past five-year period, however, our funds have returned 2.7% annually
versus the 1.0% return of the benchmark.
These mixed long-term results are attributable to both the U.S. Equity
and International Equity managers. The relative over-weighting to U.S. Equity
value managers has hurt us in the three-year time frame, but in the longer term,
it has benefited the fund. Our international investment manager has had
difficulty over the last few years in stock and sector selection, but the last few
quarters have been favorable and their investment selection seems to back on
track. The bond manager continues to generate alpha for the portfolio. Please
refer to the attachments that follow for a complete performance summary for
each manager for periods covering the fiscal quarter and year.
The Committee continues to support the investment philosophy of its
managers. There have been no changes in investment managers during the last
fiscal year. The Committee has approved adding a Real Estate Manager to the
portfolio within the coming fiscal year with an initial allocation of 5%.
In addition to making recommendations for the Grand Lodge and its
related entities’ investment portfolios, the Committee also works closely with
the Director of Development, Doug Ismail, to ensure that donors to the various
Grand Lodge charities are provided the most competitive investment options.
The Gift Annuity program has been greatly enhanced, offering potential donors
with much higher income streams than were previously available. This
improvement has translated into a greater number of gifts. Due to Doug’s
efforts, the philanthropic program has brought in more than $900,000 in new
gifts over the past year plus about $15 million in bequests and other planned
giving.
Your Grand Lodge Investment Committee is also available to assist
individual lodges in determining appropriate investment strategy. One available
vehicle to aid in this effort is the “Investment Options for California Masonic
Lodges” program which is intended to assist Lodges in determining an
appropriate investment strategy and asset allocation. It will also help Lodges
establish a set of policies and procedures that are designed to ensure assets are
invested according to the needs of the Lodge and to safeguard against the
misuse of funds. Investment Committee members continue to make themselves
available to the Lodges for assistance with this program and other questions
regarding lodge investments.
It is with deep regret that I received word that Most Worshipful Warren
Blomseth is relinquishing his role as Grand Treasurer this year. I am most

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

286

appreciative for the dedication and able support, guidance and wisdom he has
provided us in recent years. I look forward to working closely with Most
Worshipful Bill Holsinger, should he be elected Grand Treasurer for the ensuing
Masonic year.
Our former Manager of Investments, David Schwarzenberger, left us to
work for a private investment manager. I am very grateful to his contribution to
Grand Lodge during his brief, but very productive tenure.
In May, we hired Rich Moreau to fill the position of Manager of
Investments. I have thoroughly enjoyed working with Rich and have every
confidence that he will serve our Craft well.
Lastly, my term as Chairman of your Investment Committee comes to
an end with this Annual Communication. The past five years during which I
have had the honor to serve as Chairman have been very rewarding. I have
learned much and am very grateful to have had the opportunity to lead such a
fine group of men on the Grand Lodge Investment Committee. I thank you for
the opportunity and wish to express my appreciation to the Committee for the
commitment and devotion they have demonstrated for the Masonic causes.
They have always been forthright with their opinions and expertise. I am
convinced we have the finest group of investment professionals fulfilling our
very important mission.
I pledge to support incoming Chairman, Worshipful Rick Hutchinson,
as he assumes the new role. I have every confidence the Committee will
continue to flourish under his guidance.
In summary, while the financial markets continue to be volatile, we are
pleased with the results we continue to achieve. Rest assured we remain ever
diligent in our attempts to achieve adequate returns to support the Homes and
other Masonic charities while at the same time preserving our asset base for
future ages.
Respectfully submitted,
Russell E. Charvonia, Chairman

REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE
ON JURISPRUDENCE
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F. & A.M. of California:
The Committee on Jurisprudence serves as the legal adviser to the
Grand Master and the legal adviser to the Grand Lodge when in session. The
Committee reviews proposed resolutions and recommendations to be considered
at the next Annual Communication and prepares any necessary legislation to
carry out the intent of the proponents. At the Annual Communication, it
comments on the legality of the Grand Master’s decisions and dispensations and
on the legal effect of the proposed recommendations and resolutions. After the
Annual Communication, it prepares the changes to the California Masonic Code
to reflect the legislation which was adopted.
In the Committee’s view, those of Grand Master Doan’s decisions and
dispensations coming to its attention were legal and within his discretion. In the
Committee’s view, the legislation mailed by the Grand Secretary on August 3rd
of this year is sufficient to carry out the intent of the proponents, and the
legislation contained in Grand Master Doan’s recommendations which he will
present in his message is sufficient to carry out his intent. We believe that each
piece of this legislation has the legal effect set forth in it.
The legislation mailed by the Grand Secretary on August 3rd of this
year and the legislation contained in Grand Master Doan’s recommendations
require no separate motion because they are automatically before this Annual
Communication. The presiding officer will announce the vote required for the
passage of each item before discussion begins. Any legislation which does not
amend the California Masonic Code or the Ritual requires a simple majority
only for adoption. Any amendment to the California Masonic Code or the
Ritual must be adopted by a five-sixths affirmative vote. If the amendment
receives a simple majority but less than a five-sixths affirmative vote, it carries
over to the next Annual Communication when only a two-thirds affirmative vote
is required for adoption.
We thank the Grand Master for the opportunity which he has given us
to serve this year.
Respectfully submitted,
R. Stephen Doan, PGM, Chairman
Michael D. Castelli
Lester W. Miller

Jack R. Levitt, PGM
Richard L. Riemer

290

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE
ON LEADERSHIP DEVELOPMENT

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F. & A.M. of California:
Leadership Development is one of the key priorities outlined in the
Grand Lodge Strategic Plan released earlier this year. The Leadership
Development Committee has taken aggressive and dedicated steps to align our
efforts with this strategic plan.
Nearly 1,000 people participated in the leadership training programs
offered by the Grand Lodge in this fiscal year. The training programs include
the Wardens Leadership Retreats, Lodge Management Certification Programs,
and Inspectors Seminars.
Wardens Leadership Retreats
The focus of the Wardens Leadership Retreats is to prepare lodge
wardens to be leaders of Masonry in their community. Through a two-part
strategic planning program divided between the Junior and Senior Wardens
Retreats, wardens learn to develop a vision for their lodge, goals and objectives
to meet that vision, and a financial plan to support the strategic initiatives of the
lodge. Time is also devoted to sharing ideas about lodge programming in the
areas of membership development, Masonic education, and community
involvement. The wardens are encouraged to bring their spouses and a special
training program is provided for them, in addition to their participation with the
wardens on certain subjects.
Lodge Management Certification Program
The Lodge Management Certification Program offers in-depth training
on some of the most fundamental lodge operations. The program covers five
subjects: Lodge Finance, Program Planning, Hall Associations, Membership
Development, and Administration. Each subject has four courses and the
training takes one entire day. After completing that four course in one of the
above subjects, attendees are given an exam on the material. Those who pass
the exam are given a certificate in that subject.
Inspectors Seminars
These seminars include a four hour course for new Inspectors on the
eve of a full day of training for all Inspectors. The program includes training
and discussion on the overall leadership training strategy of the Grand Lodge,
technological resources, lodge administration and finance. Inspectors consider
ideas on how to most effective in their leadership role.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE
ON MEMBERSHIP DEVELOPMENT

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
My name is Gary L. Peterson, and I am a Past Master of Saddleback
Laguna Lodge No 672, and Chairman of the Membership Development
Committee.
It is my pleasure to present to you the Annual Report of the
Membership Development Committee for the 2005 Masonic year.
I would first like to acknowledge and thank the committeemen and
Grand Lodge staff members who throughout this year have continued to
contribute their time and energy in supporting and making our “Pass It On” new
membership development program a success. Those committee members are
Wor. Gary L. Peterson, Past Master of Saddleback Laguna Lodge No 672; Wor.
James E. Banta, Past Master of Fresno Lodge No 247; Wor. Salvatore A.
Sciortino, Past Master of Wisdom L odge No 202; Wor. Felipe G. Leon, Past
Master of San Jacinto Lodge No. 338; and Wor. Edward Avetisian, Past Master
of Burbank Lodge No 406. The Grand Lodge staff members are Wor. Allan L.
Casalou, Assistant Grand Secretary and a Past Master of Acalanes-Fellowship
Lodge No 480; Brother Angel Alvarez-Mapp, Administrative Assistant and a
member of Acalanes-Fellowship Lodge No 480; and Wor. Erik Peterson, Past
Master of Acalanes-Fellowship Lodge No 480.
As reported to you in past years, our committee was charged with the
task of developing and implementing a “Comprehensive Membership Plan” that
would successfully address the issue of increasing our membership levels by
attracting new leaders to our Fraternity. The two primary goals of the
membership program were to increase lodge awareness of the need for new
members by getting their individual members thinking and talking about a
positive approach for new candidates, and by doing so, offer the lodges a
dynamic program that would assure their ability to raise a minimum of five new
Master Masons each year.
Thus was created the “Pass it On” membership program. The program
contains comprehensive materials basic to a successful membership marketing
and retention program such as directions on target marketing of specific types of
prospects, attractive full-color wallet brochures and member informational
cards, new informational packets for applicants as well as one for those men
who have decided to pursue degrees. The program also contains informational
material for spouses, scripts and a follow-up survey; much of it contained on a
CD.
If properly used, the items in this “Pass it On” program will attract the
type of man our Fraternity seeks by illustrating to him that we are indeed an
organization that he can be comfortable in; with the complete knowledge that he

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

295

will be able learn and lead, while enjoying personal relationships, dignity and
pride among Brothers, friends and their families.
Again this year, “Pass It On” presentations were made at the Wardens’
Retreats and the Lodge Management Certification Programs. Additionally, our
committee this year began systematically making ‘Pass it On” presentations to
individual lodges throughout the state, and have currently done so to over ninety
lodges.
In order to accomplish this increased volume of presentations, the
Membership Development Committee recruited the assistance of trained
committee “Associates” who have offered their time for the betterment of this
fraternity, and to them, our thanks are certainly due. The Associates are: Wor
John B. Feringa, Past Master of Temascal Palms No. 314; Wor Richard Brown,
Past Master of Pacific-Star King No. 136; Brother Ted Lappe of Santa Monica
Palisades No. 307; Brother David Ferreria of Santa Monica Palisades No. 307;
Brother Angel-Alvarez Pagan of Woodbridge No. 131; Wor David Daley of
Oakdale No 275; Wor Anil Comelo, Master of Naval No. 87; Brother Larry
Becker of Orinda No. 122; Brother Eric Bork of Saddleback-Laguna No. 672;
Wor Dan Moran, Past Master of Long Beach No. 327; Wor Frank Estep, Master
of Coronado No 441; Wor Sean Ford, Master of Las Palmas-Ponderosa No.
366; and Wor. Allen Miller, Master of Olive Branch No. 269.
I am pleased to inform the Grand Lodge that the program goal of a
minimum of five raisings was attained this year by 89 of our lodges. Out of
those 89 lodges, 46 lodges raised six to nine, and 12 lodges raised between ten
and twenty-nine Masons. For the fourth year in a row our lodges raised more
Masons than in the proceeding year. In fact, this year the number of raisings by
our lodges was the greatest in any single year since 1984, a remarkable
achievement twenty-one years in the making. With the continuing awareness
and implementation of the “Pass It On” program, we believe this trend will
continue.
We continue to evaluate the program as to its overall effectiveness as
well as the individual components. Necessary adjustments will continue to be
made as the program implementation continues. An important consideration is
any relationship between the program usage, membership gains and any
perceived societal changes.
With the involvement of all the members of our fraternity, the heritage
we have received from our fathers should continue. The glass, my Brothers is
not half empty, it is indeed, half full.
On behalf of the Membership Development Committee for the Grand
Lodge of the State of California, I wish to thank you and the Most Worshipful
David R. Doan for giving us the opportunity to update you on the progress and
activities of our committee, and most importantly, on the ‘Pass It On”
membership development program.

Thank you.

Respectfully Submitted,
Gary L. Peterson, Chairman
Edward Avetisian
Felipe G. Leon

REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE ON
TRIAL REVIEW
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
Since the 2004 Annual Communication your Committee on Trial
Review has reviewed 17 completed trials, two applications for restoration from
expulsion, six cases which were resolved before trial, and 23 trials pending but
not yet completed. The Committee met with the Grand Secretary’s assistant,
Pamela Lindsten, weekly from the 1st of February 2005 through the 30th of
September 2005, in an effort to expedite the administrative and procedural
processes in each case. We would like to acknowledge the generous
cooperation and support of Very Worshipful John L. Cooper and Pamela
Lindsten in this endeavor.
The Committee has reviewed each of the trial records numbered 2465,
2467, 2470, 2471, 2472, 2475, 2476, 2477, 2478, 2479, 2480, 2481, 2482 and
2483, and finds that in each case there was sufficient compliance with the
procedural requirements of our Constitution and Ordinances; that the findings
and conclusions are supported by the trial record; and, that there was no
miscarriage of justice.
Your Committee therefore submits and moves for adoption the
following resolution:
“Resolved that in each of the trials numbered above the judgment and
penalty imposed be and are ratified, affirmed and approved.”
Resolution ADOPTED.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

297

Your Committee feels that in each of the following trials a special
commentary must be stated:
Trial Record No. 2461
Because of the accused’s falsification of his application to a Masonic
Organization and continuous dissembling throughout the investigation and trial,
this committee recommends that the penalty be increased to expulsion, and
therefore submits and moves for adoption the following resolution:
“Resolved that in the matter of trial record 2461 the penalty be
expulsion.”
Resolution ADOPTED.
Trial Record No. 2468
On review of the entire trial record the Committee felt that because of
the accused’s position of public trust as a county official and his violation of
that trust the penalty should be expulsion, and therefore, submits and moves for
adoption the following resolution:
“Resolved that in the matter of trial record 2468 the penalty be
expulsion.”
Resolution ADOPTED.
Trial Record No. 2474
Because of the accused’s conduct, which involved a physical assault
upon the daughter of a Mason, the Committee recommends that the penalty be
increased to expulsion, and therefore, submits and moves for adoption of the
following resolution:
“Resolved that in the matter of trial record 2474 the penalty be
expulsion.”
Resolution ADOPTED.
Your Committee has reviewed trial records concerning applications for
restoration.
Trial Record No. 2455
This is an application for restoration from expulsion. The accused was
found guilty of theft from his employer. He was a mechanic in the service
department of an auto dealership. It was a common practice to include two or

298

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

three cars at the end of a payroll period for unit compensation for the mechanic
before the repair work was completed. The cars would be “flagged” for
completion on the following day, or the beginning of the next payroll period. A
new service manager from the east coast was not aware of this practice and
reported it to the police. The accused was represented by a public defender and
pleaded guilty to grand theft. In a subsequent Masonic Trial he was found
guilty of unmasonic conduct and expelled. In January of 2005, the conviction
was reduced to a misdemeanor and dismissed. The applicant’s lodge voted 17
to 1 to restore him. The applicant’s father is currently the Junior Warden of the
lodge. His testimonial in this matter was given additional weight by the
Committee.
The Committee feels that the circumstances of this case warrant
restoration, and therefore submits and moves for adoption the following
Resolution:
“Resolved that in the matter of Trial Record No. 2455 Restoration be
granted.”
Resolution ADOPTED.
Trial Record No. 1994
The accused had been convicted of defrauding three banks of
approximately $250,000 dollars each. In a subsequent Masonic Trial he was
found guilty of unmasonic conduct and expelled. In 2001 his application for
restoration was denied by his lodge. His application to Grand Lodge was also
denied. On August 3, 2005 his application for restoration was denied by his
lodge by a vote of 15 to 3. This application to Grand Lodge includes a letter
from his son, a letter from a non Mason friend, a letter from a former lodge
member and two informal notes from neighbors at his senior residence. The
Committee feels that there is insufficient evidence to warrant restoration and,
therefore, submits for adoption the following Resolution.
“Resolved that the application for Restoration in Trial Record No. 1994
Restoration be denied.”
Resolution ADOPTED.
Respectfully submitted,
Thomas C. Letcher, Chairman
Donald B. Constine
David A. Goble
Kirk M. Livermont

Richard A. Meyers
Donald R. Fretz, Consultant

NAVIGATION TIPS

Scroll through the document using
scroll bars on the side of the window or
the page navigation tool at the bottom.

Click on any of the items in the bookmark
section of the document and you’ll navigate
directly to that page or section.
You can then move to the next page using the
ARROW KEYS on your keyboard.
Return to the beginning of the document at any
time by clicking on the Table of Contents
Bookmark.
If you want to enlarge or reduce the size of the
document, click on the Zoom In/Zoom Out
bookmarks.
Navigate through the document
by clicking on any of the items in
the Table of Contents or the
Index.
The “Hand Tool” will change to
an “Index Finger”…click on the
line and you’ll move to the
desired page.
To get back, click on the Table
of Contents/Index bookmarks.
